Crits show a gold ×1.5 number, CRIT! and a flash. Rolls use the world RNG, so lockstep stays in sync.
- Heroes regenerate 2% max HP per second when out of combat for 2.5s on their own half or inside their own tower range; that already existed but was invisible, so green + motes now float up while it's happening.
- Loading splash regenerated from a lineup render of the current six hero models (`tools/gen-loading.mjs <lineup.png> art`).
- Third map, Grudgecrag Heights (`data/maps/heights.json`, generated by `tools/mapgen-heights.py`; props built headless with `blender -b --python-expr "import sys; sys.argv += ['--', 'heights']; exec(open('tools/blender/build_map.py').read())"`). 80x56, mirrored left/right, built around lane choice rather than one river or one keep. Each base has three gates. North gate: a ramp up to a long high ridge (neutral pad and watchtower in the middle, tall-grass ambush spots, a cliff face you can drop off onto the middle field, and high ground makes ranged shots at you miss). East gate: the open middle field and the crag, a raised plateau with ramps east and west, cliffs north and south, ruins for cover and the relic's home on top. South gate: past a ruined wall with one gap per side into a wadeable reed marsh (slow), deep pools, a winding boardwalk and a reed island with the south neutral pad. New `peak` emblem. Walk check: no trap cells; sims finish normally in 1v1 and 2v2.
- Menu redesign: every menu now sits on the live game world instead of a brick wall or wooden table. On champion select, map select, the online lobby and the menu sub-pages, the background match pauses and the camera slowly pans across the map (`GameRenderer.cinematic`), under a warm darkened vignette (`fieldShade`). Every screen shares the same frame: the carved beam title at the top and a wood-plank footer with the button prompts (title and main menu included). Versus Online uses two nailed wooden placards with wax seals. Controls is one parchment sheet (`drawControlSheet`) that wraps long lines, shared with the pause menu. Pause is now a real menu: hanging banner with RESUME / CONTROLS / QUIT MATCH (quit asks again), the HUD hides, and a scoreboard plank shows time left, map, each hero's portrait, level, HP and learned talents, and each house's keep %, gold, soldiers and hero kills. Results: the winner's name in the title font on its house banner, house cloth panels with portraits, and a parchment tug-of-war of stats with blue/red bars. Screenshot script: `tools/fxcap/menus.mjs <tag>`; overview in `docs/menus.jpg`.
- Warden snares are bramble snares now: the bramble decal, a ring in the team colour, thorny vines with leaves curled inward that open as the snare arms, and when one triggers, vines whip up out of the ground around the victim with thorns, leaves and a green shockwave (traps carry `src`/`trap` on their slam event).
- Hold to charge: holding A or B longer than 0.2s charges (the hero slows to 45%, an aura in the hero's colour grows with sparks pulling in and crackles at full); releasing fires the attack at up to ×1.8 (A) or ×1.6 (B) damage, A also gets +30% reach and more knockback at full, "FULL POWER!" callout, release burst and shockwaves. A tap still attacks instantly. Hold to aim: holding R or Z on a placeable ability (wall, siege works, bramble zone, summons) shows the range ring and a reticle (wall footprint or area ring) that the stick moves; releasing places it there, a tap auto-places as before. Both ride on the command (`charge`, `charging`, `place`) so they stay in sync online; bots are unchanged.
- Hold-to-aim also covers targeted B abilities: the Raider's leap (lands where you aim, still snapped to walkable ground), the Summoner's hex and the Herald's banner. On those heroes holding B aims instead of charging. Dashes stay stick-aimed.
- The ogre now matters: killing it pays 200 gold and blesses the killing house for 45s (every hero and soldier alive gets +25% damage and +10% speed; heroes also heal 30% and gain 250 XP). The notice names the blessed house.
- Warden wall crush: the wall no longer leaves gaps around enemy heroes. Anything inside or touching a wall block when it rises is shoved out to the side it was on, and enemies take 70 damage (scales with buffs), a 0.9s stun and a big knockback away from the wall, with a ground-slam burst. Allies inside are only moved out of the way. Tunable via the wall's `damage`, `stunSeconds`, `knockback` in heroes.json.
- Talents rebuilt as evolutions, modelled on Pokémon UNITE's move system. The old trees (two tiny add-ons per A/B path, 72 talents) were mostly +5% riders and stat stacks that didn't change play, R and Z never changed, and nothing combined across paths. Now each hero has 7 picks over levels 2–5 (`data/talents.json`, `order: r, b, a, z`): Lv2 evolves R into one of two different moves, Lv3 evolves B into one of two, Lv4 picks an A fighting style, Lv5 auto-learns a "Z+" super upgrade. B evolutions carry a `combo` that switches on only with a specific R pick (`with` entries applied in `recompute`), like Dig + Earthquake. New moves needed new sim kinds: `blink` (Raider Shadow Step, Duelist Volte; hold-to-aim), `rootcage` (Warden, delayed root burst), `turret` + `tesla` (Engineer coil that chains lightning), a timber palisade wall style, warcry `challenge` (pull + weaken + armor) and `lifestealAura` (Bloodroar), healing `grove` zones (Living Rampart, Ancient Grove), `meterOnKill`, `parryMark`, wall extension on repair, lava/smoke/grove zone styles. Learn cards now show the full description and the combo line in red. Icons: one fal sheet per hero (7 icons, 4x2), sliced by detected shapes (`tools/slice-talent-sheets.py`, since rows of 3 sit centred and some sheets draw cell borders) and keyed by distance from the corner background colour (`tools/talent-icons-process.py`) so purple art survives. Zone visuals are painted: sinkhole/crater stone rubble and drifting dust, bone hands and ghosts for ossuary, a modelled tesla coil with arcing sparks, glowing lava cracks with embers, roiling smoke, flower-and-moss groves; projectiles use painted sprites (hot rivets, poisoned knives, a crescent slash, rock waves that heave up stone spikes); Challenge draws iron chains to every foe. Every 48 paths were run through bot fights without errors, and each new mechanic probed in isolation. Balance (8 matches per pair): Warlord 68, Warden 58, Duelist 55, Engineer 52, Raider 38, Summoner 22 (Summoner was 27 before; still the weakest). Collages: `docs/evolve_<hero>.jpg`, icons `docs/talent_icons.png`. Dev: `grudge.levelUp(player, picks)`, `FX_PICKS=0101 tools/fxcap/fxcap.mjs <hero> <tag>`.
- Warden's B slap (and Whiplash, and its splinter burst) now hits enemy buildings for 1.5× its damage (`structureMul`); the yank and knockback only apply to units and heroes.
- Multiple battles per server: the relay keeps rooms (`tools/netrelay.ts`). Each browser host opens its own room (up to 32) and advertises name, mode, map, humans/seats and lobby/match phase (`NetLink.meta`, sent with the lobby tick). `/net/info` lists the rooms. Versus Online > Join opens a server browser ("Battles on this server") that refreshes every 2s and shows each battle's mode, field, seats and state, plus Quick Join (first open lobby). Joining a full or running battle is refused; leaving the host closes only that room. Four people on four machines in one match was already supported (host + 3 remote seats) and is now covered by an end-to-end test: one host and three peers browse, pick the same room, ready up, play with distinct slots and no desync, while a second room runs alongside (`tools/fxcap/net-4players.mjs`, needs the dev server).
- Records only count humans: CPU players' champions no longer add picks, wins or losses to the champion table (they were already excluded from name records). The match still appears in the chronicle, with CPUs listed.
- Options > KEYBOARD + MOUSE (on/off, saved): off releases the keyboard's seat and keys/mouse never claim a player seat again, so a host with only controllers isn't stuck with a phantom KBM player. The mouse can still click menus, so it can be turned back on from Options.
- Versus Online: Host and Join sit side by side, so the stick/D-pad moves between them left/right (up/down no longer does).
- Player management. New main-menu page PLAYERS: four seat cards showing which device holds each local seat (keyboard + mouse, GameCube, Pro, or the gamepad's name), A frees a seat (the device rejoins on its next press), a KEYBOARD + MOUSE on/off toggle (same as the option), and a button to search for a GameCube adapter / Pro controller.
- Online seats are per device, not per machine. Joining connects the machine only; the peer then reports its active local devices (`want` with pad indices, resent on change and every 1.5s) and the host gives each its own seat (`rseats`: peer + local pad index → slot), named NAME1/NAME2 when one machine has several. Picks, ready and in-match commands carry the pad index `k`; the start message lists `[peer, k, slot]` and the peer drives one mapper per seat. A machine with no active device just watches with "PRESS A BUTTON … TO TAKE A SEAT". Freed or unplugged devices give the seat back. Hosting no longer fills empty seats with CPUs: they show as OPEN SEAT banners (with + ADD CPU; a CPU seat shows an X to reopen it), remote devices take open seats, and any still open when the match starts become CPUs. Offline play still fills empty seats with CPUs as before. Tested: a peer with keyboard + gamepad holds two seats and a second peer one more; all three heroes move from their own devices with no desync (`tools/fxcap/net-multidevice.mjs`).
- Joining players see the real champion select instead of the separate lobby screen. The peer renders the host's slots through the same banners, shields and chips, with its own gloves (tagged with the seat number) for its own devices: grab and place a seal to pick and ready, hover to preview, SIT HERE on an open seat to switch seat/team (host message `seat`, only onto open or auto-CPU seats), B drops a held seal or leaves. The host's rules and map show in a line on the floor. The old lobby layout is removed. Tested in `tools/fxcap/net-peer-select.mjs`.
- Denser HUD with more players: each team's HUD (keep bar, super meters, gold/army, cooldowns, levels and talents, order and learn panels) is drawn scaled to 74% in 4-player matches or 4-way split (86% with 3 players), still anchored to its corners; in that mode the idle "ORDER ARMY" cross is hidden and only appears while someone is giving orders or switching who obeys.
- HUD split into team and player parts. The screen corners keep only team info (keep HP and shield, gold, army, relic/bomb carriers). Each local player gets their own panel: B/R cooldowns or respawn timer, Z meter, level with the four evolution icons and XP bar, and their own LEVEL UP prompt. In split screen the panel sits at the top of that player's view (`GameRenderer.viewRectOf`), and that player's learn cards, build/shop crosses and order cross appear at the bottom of their own view; in a shared view panels stack under the team block. Only local players' panels and level-up cards are drawn, so teammates' (and enemies') upgrades no longer clutter the screen; with no local players (attract mode) each team shows its main hero.
- A hosted match can't start while any active seat is still open (no more 2v1 or 1v0 by accident). When everyone seated is ready but seats are open, a scroll says SEATS STILL OPEN: wait for players, + ADD CPU, or switch to 1 VS 1.
- Desync fix: the CPU bot set its hero's facing directly on the world (`me.transform.facing = …` when lining up a bomb throw). Bots only run on the host, so the host's world turned while every peer's didn't; facing feeds throw direction, backstab and block checks, so bombs landed differently (towers destroyed on one screen only) and damage/kills diverged. Bots now aim through their command (move toward the target the tick they throw), like players. The sync hash now includes facing, and a peer that detects a mismatch gets a banner instead of only a small "OUT OF SYNC" tag. Soak test: host + peer, 2v2 with one human and one CPU per team, full entity state compared every 5s for 4 minutes with zero differences (`tools/fxcap/net-sync-soak.mjs`, `MODE=2v2`). Lockstep still assumes both players use the same browser engine; mixing e.g. Firefox and Chrome can still drift because their Math functions round differently.
- Rematch CPUs: after a 2v2, the host went back to champion select still in 2v2, so with only two people the other seats were open and (before the open-seat start block) silently became CPUs on start. Now a host returning to select with fewer than three players drops back to 1 VS 1; with open seats left the match can't start anyway.
- Desync guards. Every build gets an id (`__BUILD__`, set in vite.config); the host sends it in the lobby view and a peer on a different build is sent back with "THE HOST RUNS ANOTHER VERSION · REFRESH BOTH PAGES" (a tab left open across a server redeploy runs old game code against the host's new code, which desyncs immediately). Each machine also fingerprints its Math functions; if the peer's differs from the host's (different browser engine), it gets a warning banner. Commands normalise -0 to 0 before sending (JSON turns -0 into 0, so the host and peers could otherwise step on different values). Damage variance and crits use the world's seeded RNG, so they're identical on every machine; the soak test still shows zero divergence.
- The main menu shows the build in the bottom-right corner (`BUILD yymmdd.hhmm`, UTC time of the build), the same id the netplay version check uses.
- Rubble only blocks the team that lost the building (`pad.rubbleTeam`); the attackers can build on the ruins straight away.
- Outposts as forward bases: called squads now come out of your nearest outpost of any kind (one that makes the called type is preferred) whenever it's closer to you than your keep, and squads from forward or neutral outposts get +15% stats. Standing within 8m of your own outpost counts as home turf (regen when out of combat, home speed bonus), like your tower range. Outposts cost 75 (was 90). Sims now push harder: average keep damage rose from near zero to ~2200 in a short warlord/raider run.
- Hosted rematches keep the CPUs you added: when the host returns to champion select, seats you filled with + ADD CPU stay CPUs (and a 2v2 with CPUs in seats 3/4 stays 2v2) instead of reverting to open seats.
- Squad calls (since the outpost change) come out of whichever of your outposts is nearest, even one that trains a different unit; the matching type is preferred.
- Troop rock-paper-scissors made decisive: grunts beat brutes (×2.2 vs brutes, brutes ×0.45 vs grunts), archers beat grunts (×3.2, grunts ×0.6 vs archers), brutes beat archers (×2.4, archers ×0.5 vs brutes). Brute HP 560 → 480, archer HP 105 → 120. In equal-gold squad fights (`tools/rps.ts`) the counter wins 6/6 in all three pairings; before, archers lost to grunts 6/6. The controls sheet lists the triangle.
- Stealing the Grudge from an enemy shrine no longer hands it to the thief: the shrine breaks and the relic returns to its spawn point in the middle, where anyone can take it.
- The Grudge can be enshrined at an outpost too (any of your built barracks, range or foundry), not just a tower or the keep. An enshrined outpost sends 2 extra units every wave and its units (wave and called squads) get +25% stats (`outpostExtra`, `outpostStatMul`); towers keep +60% damage / +30% range, the keep its shield regen, and any shrine still gives the +35% gold. The relic banner names the outpost.
- Faster building: a new building takes 7s with one hero beside it (was 10s); two heroes together get a teamwork bonus (rate 2.6 instead of 2.0, so under 3s), troops still help a little, cap 3.2. Upgrades take 10s (was 12s) and run on their own once paid for; anyone standing by still speeds them up.
- Engineer health 800 → 980 (new "sturdy" tier) so he survives long enough to set up; his ballista has 450 HP (was 180). Raider speed 5.6 → 6.3 (new "swift" tier). Quick sims: Engineer 4–2 vs Duelist, 1–5 vs Warlord; Raider 4–2 vs Warden.
- Performance pass (measured in a busy 2v2 with a CPU profile). (1) HUD: `texturedRect` built a canvas pattern, clipped and multiply-filled every call; with talent icons and panels that was ~110 ms per frame of 2D canvas work. Each textured rect is now baked once per size/tint/scale into a small canvas (LRU 400) and drawn with one drawImage: HUD time dropped to ~2.5 ms. (2) Particles: effect sprites were one Sprite + one material each. `emit` and the old burst/flash/dust/spark/trail helpers now feed `Particles` (`src/render/particles.ts`), an instanced billboard batch per texture/blend mode (one draw call each, fogged, budget 2500 live particles) with the physics done in one loop. (3) Shader programs: three.js frees a program when the last material using it is disposed, so every effect that disposed its material forced a recompile of the next one. Material.dispose is now a no-op (materials hold no GPU buffers; geometry and textures are still disposed) and shader error checking is off in production builds. (4) Entity views outside each camera's frustum are hidden per render pass (heroes, units and buildings, including split views).
- Ogre pathing: big units (radius > 0.8, i.e. the ogre) only walk straight at a target when a corridor as wide as their body is clear, skip waypoints later, and if they make under 0.6m of progress in 0.8s they back off sideways for 0.7s and repath. Across 9 bot matches on all three maps the ogre's stuck time while chasing fell from 28% to 8%.
- Bigger bases. `tools/map_grow.py` inserts empty strips into a map (rects spanning the line stretch, everything past it shifts; mirror maps grow on both sides). Crossing gets 4 more columns inside each keep (map 104 wide), Grudgecrag Heights the same (88 wide, applied in `mapgen-heights.py`), Grudgekeep Ruins 4 rows and columns on each axis (48x48). Home pads are spread further from the keep. All map GLBs rebuilt headless with Blender.
- Team pads are coloured: the pad rims and inlays are strongly tinted in their team's colour (neutral pads stay stone and gold), and an empty team pad shows a faint ring in its team colour.
- Fixed a frame-time bug: the first frame's dt could be negative (the first requestAnimationFrame timestamp predates `last`), which made particles age backwards and grow into screen-filling blobs; dt is now clamped at 0.
- Seat switching: open seats show SIT HERE (and + ADD CPU when hosting); a CPU seat shows SIT HERE too. Clicking SIT HERE with a glove moves that device into the seat (`Gamepads.move` swaps the pad slot), carrying its hero and name; its old seat becomes open (hosting) or a CPU (offline). So a host with two controllers can put both on the same team.
- Fixed online seating with a CPU already placed: the rematch "kept CPU" check treated never-placed seats (autoCpu undefined) as hand-placed CPUs, so the host's lobby had no free seats and a joining machine's devices got no seat (no chip). Only CPUs added with + ADD CPU are kept now.
- Crossing and Heights home bases redesigned: the keep courtyard now has a clear 4-wide road from the keep to the east gate and down the north–south axis (dirt paths), and the home pads moved off it, two to the back corners near the north and south walls beside the gate, one tucked behind the keep. Banners and crates moved to the walls. Paths from the keep to all three gates are straight 11m runs with every pad built.
- Camera: zoom steps go out to 120 and 150 (fully zoomed out shows the whole map with margin), the full-map width cap is 30% wider, and the camera may pan past the map edges by 6m + 15% of the view so heroes at the back of a base aren't cut off.
- Signing names works like Melee: clicking your own name plate (only with your own glove) opens a name pad inside your banner (`src/ui/nameEntry.ts`): text field, a row cycling your saved names, an A–Z/0–9 grid and - / DEL / OK, all driven by your stick with a highlighted key. A types, B deletes (or closes when empty), START or OK confirms; a keyboard player can type directly, with Enter/Escape. While a pad is open that player's glove is parked, so several players sign at once without touching each other's, and START can't launch the match. Online, joining players sign the same way and the name is sent to the host (`tag`). The old full-screen overlay is no longer used.
- Performance pass (measured on the real GPU, 1080p): 1v1 115→261 FPS, 4-way split 43→113 FPS, select screen CPU ~45%→4%, sim ~42% faster with bit-identical results. Changes:
  - Hero and unit models are merged into one draw at load (`src/render/mergedModel.ts`): all of a model's material parts share one skinned mesh, textures go into a texture array, and per-part tint/vertex-colour/face flags live in uniforms (team dye per instance). Units went from 7–8 draws each to 1.
  - Skeletons update once per app frame, and parts sharing bones share one skeleton (bone textures were re-uploaded ~100×/frame).
  - Silhouettes use per-mesh proxies on the team layers with dedicated skinned/static materials and one pass per view, instead of re-rendering the scene with an override material twice. Lights are on every layer, so the silhouette pass no longer flips the lighting state (that forced every lit material to re-resolve its shader on every pass).
  - Transparent double-sided materials render single-pass (three's two-pass mode bumped material versions twice per draw).
  - Scene matrices update once per frame, not per view; the static map is frozen.
  - Select-screen ribbons, shields and banners are baked into cached canvases (clip + multiply fills were the whole CPU cost).
  - Pathfinding: typed-array heap, A* results cached per start/goal cell and cell-centre line checks memoized until the nav grid changes; `slopeAt` has no per-call closures.
  - `powerPreference: "high-performance"` so dual-GPU laptops pick the discrete GPU.
  - FPS counter option (on by default) in the bottom-right corner.
- Dodge cooldown now starts when the roll ends: 0.7 s after landing (about 1 s between roll starts). Before, the 0.9 s cooldown counted from the start of a 0.32 s roll, so you could roll again 0.58 s after landing and it felt like no cooldown.
- Troops are faster: grunt 3.52→4.4, archer 3.28→4.2, brute 2.48→3.5. Heroes are 4.0–6.3, so mid heroes can still outrun grunts. The neutral Ogre is unchanged.
- DEFEND means "guard the base": every base and its entrances are found from the map (`World.computeBase`). The base is the bounding box of the castle-wall segments within 14 cells of the core, flood-filled from the core. Entrances are clusters of base cells that open to the outside, and each guard post sits 2 cells inside its gap. Crossing and Heights get north gate, south gate and east opening; Ruins gets its corner gate and side gate. Defending troops are dealt round-robin across the posts, sorted brutes→grunts→archers, so each gate gets a mix. They attack any enemy inside the base wherever it is, and only engage outside the walls within max(4, range+1) of their post.
- Hero display names: Warlord, Stig (engineer), Grim (raider), Remnil (summoner), Francois (duelist), Thorn (warden). Internal ids are unchanged. Select-screen ribbons draw the display name (`nameImage("!NAME")`).
- Name pad fixes: the naming player's glove is now frozen (`Cursors.frozen`) instead of being reported as disconnected, so it keeps its chip and the slot no longer looks unplugged. It also stays frozen on the frame the pad closes, so the A that pressed OK can't re-click the name plate. Key text is centred inside its highlight box, and the caret is a drawn bar (the font's "_" read as "– –").
- Area effects never hit the caster's own team (audited every area loop; only the neutral Ogre hits everyone). Thorn's brambles now also speed up allies standing in them by 25% (`allySpeedMul`).
- Stig's ballista: only one at a time (a new one knocks down the old one), lasting 20 s; a perched one also falls when its works do, whichever comes first.
- Brutes (heavies) toned down: HP 480→380, damage 42→32, ×0.8 against heroes, knockback resistance 0.3→0.15 so hero hits shove them aside. Measured with each hero alone against a cluster: one squad of three used to leave heroes at 1–37% health (and three of six heroes at ≤16%); now 46–66%. Four brutes now leave 22–53% instead of killing outright. Troop rock-paper-scissors is unchanged at equal gold.
- Heroes can walk off cliffs; climbing a sheer face is still impossible. The ledge never stopped them; the unstick logic did. On the steep face a hero counts as "stuck", and `nearestStandable` picked the closest-height spot, which was back up on the ledge. For heroes it now ignores higher ground unless nothing at or below them is standable, so they slide down. The drop is shown with a render-only fall (gravity 30 m/s²) instead of a one-tick snap; the sim is unchanged.
- DPS audit (scripted 20 s of attacking: hero dummy / six clumped grunts / tower). Before: Warlord 95/270/85, Stig 114/333/96, Grim 138/248/192, Remnil 149/302/132, Francois 188/325/156, Thorn 61/385/79. Thorn buffed: damage tier low→mid, combo 48/76→54/86 with faster recovery (0.5/0.66→0.46/0.58 s) and less knockback (3.5/6.5→1.5/4.5) so the target stays in reach, arcs 210/240→160/190, and a new `structureMul` hook (×1.3 to buildings). Now 129/609/151; his sweep still clears clumped troops fastest, which suits him as a tank. Bot matches: Thorn now wins 38–56% against Warlord, Francois, Grim and Stig (Francois 20%→38%, Warlord ~10–20%→50%).
- Thrown bomb: blast radius 3.2→4.6 m, splash 90→140, ×1.8 against troops (252, enough to kill grunts and archers outright and take two-thirds of a brute). The blast visual now uses the real radius.
- Arena cannon (unchanged, for reference): 110 per shot to units and 60 to buildings, never to the keep.
- Auto-aim: with the stick idle, ranged reaches (>4 m) only snap to targets within ±60° of your facing; before, Remnil would spin round to shoot anything behind her. Melee keeps the all-around snap.
- Ranged bots face their target when they fire (their stick used to point along their kiting path, so they shot away from the enemy), and back off with a hex and dodges when a melee hero closes in. Remnil went from 4% to about 30% in bot matches.
- Tuning pass (bot grid, last full run before the final War Cry change: Warlord 74, Thorn 55, Grim 55, Francois 48, Stig 41, Remnil 27):
  - Warlord: War Cry no longer resets Ground Slam (the double slam); slam cooldown 7→8.5 s, slow 1.5→1 s; Earthquake 220→170 damage, 1.2→1 s stun, 140→100 building damage, ×1.3 vs slowed (was ×1.5); War Cry +25%→+12% damage and speed; brute bonus 1.25→1.1.
  - Thorn: combo 54/86→50/80, building bonus ×1.3→×1.2, long-arm slap 80→70.
  - Grim (glass cannon): damage tier high, 7% faster, near-instant flurry (light hits 0.16 s with 0.03 s wind-up, finisher 0.26 s), 24/24/24/40 damage, almost no knockback on light hits, 2.4 m reach, narrow 60°/90° arcs, chain window 0.22 s and 0.05 s recovery. Per-hero `comboCooldown` now overrides the baseline. Highest single-target damage (199/s).
  - Stig: ballista 520 HP, 95 damage (compensates for one ballista at a time).
- Economy rework (supersedes keep waves and squad calls):
  - Keeps no longer send troops, and the X squad-call is gone. X only opens the outpost menu at a pad.
  - Outposts are the only source of troops. They still send their troop every 16 s (two at level 2, plus the Grudge-shrine bonus), but each soldier costs gold as it spawns: grunt 12, archer 16, brute 25 (`units.waves.spawnCost`, scaled by the cost rule). If the team can't pay, that outpost's troop is skipped and the team gets "NO GOLD · OUTPOSTS IDLE".
  - Losses: your hero dying costs your team 50 gold, and losing a tower costs 75 (`economy.loss`), on top of the bounty the enemy already gets. Gold never goes below 0, and a notice shows the loss.
  - Felling an enemy tower rallies the whole army, heroes included: +20% damage and +15% speed for 10 s (`economy.rally`). It has its own timer (`status.rallyUntil`) so it can't be overwritten by War Cry, the Ogre blessing or the Herald's aura, and it shows with the existing buff marker.
  - Bots build more outposts (a longer build plan with forward and neutral barracks, a second range and a forward foundry) so they keep turning gold into troops.
  - `tools/rps.ts` compares troops at equal spawn cost.
- Tower limit removed (supersedes "3 towers per hero"); pads and gold are the only limits.
- Economy snapshot (18 bot matches across all maps, per team per minute): passive income +266, bounties +231, troop spawns −201, building/upgrades/shop −230, losses −51. About 18 troops spawn per team per minute, but only 4–8 are alive at once; banked gold sits around 150–200 and climbs to ~330 late.
- Anti-snowball pass. Measured with mirror bot matches (same hero both sides, 8 per hero per map): the side ahead at 90 s won 74%. Gold-only catch-up barely moved it (75%), because the snowball comes from losing buildings and piling up keep damage. Changes, all scaled by the existing deficit score (`catchUp`, 0–1):
  - The team behind gets cheaper troop spawns (up to −40%, `productionBoost`, previously unused), smaller gold losses on hero death or tower loss (up to −100%), more passive income (`incomeBoost` 0.35→0.6), faster hero respawns (up to −40%, `respawnCut`), and buildings that take less damage (up to −30%, `fortify`).
  - The team ahead earns smaller bounties for killing the team behind (up to −50%, `bountyCut`).
  - Troop bounties down: grunt 5→3, archer 6→4, brute 12→7.
  - Result: the early leader now wins 63%; games average 413 s (was 390) and fewer end with the keep destroyed (26 vs 59 of 144).
- Brute spawn cost 25→32 (archer 16, grunt 12). At equal gold against a mixed army, an all-brute army now does worst of the three mono armies. Economy per team per minute: income +280, bounties +182, troops −186, building −227, losses −34.
- Base attack and defence in 1v1:
  - Tower aggro: damage towers shoot troops first. They switch to an enemy hero when that hero has no troops of its own inside the tower's range, or has hit one of the tower side's units or heroes near the tower in the last 2.5 s (`status.lastHitAt/X/Z`). Pushing with your army is safer; diving alone or picking fights under a tower gets you shot. Ballistas still go for heroes.
  - Lane towers (forward and neutral pads) are formidable: ×1.7/×1.6 HP, ×2.4/×2.2 damage, and their bolts slow heroes to 65% for 1 s (`structures.laneTower`). A lone hero walking through one takes ~325 in 5 s. Home-pad towers are unchanged, so packing your base with towers doesn't make it a fortress.
  - Recall: Y (R / right mouse) away from a pad or the keep starts a 6 s channel (progress bar over the hero) that teleports you to your keep. Moving, attacking, using an ability, dodging, carrying the Grudge, being stunned or getting hit breaks it. Once per life; using it again shows "RECALL USED". Bots recall when their base is threatened, they're 35 m+ from home and not in a fight.
- Crossing pads moved toward the middle so towers cover the routes people actually use (checked against a top-down render with range circles):
  - Neutral (52,13)/(52,35) → (52,19.5)/(52,28.5): both now sit in the centre part of the island and cover both ford landings. Before, the centre only got the edges of their range.
  - Forward (28,8)/(29,35) → (27.5,12.5)/(27.5,35.5): they cover each base's east-gate approach and the side fields. The north one stays on the high plateau's southern lip.
  - Forward tower range 10→11.5 (all maps), so the pair nearly meets across the middle lane.
  - Ground flattened under each new pad, the centre mound shrunk (r 2→1, edge 3.5→2), and three rocks moved out of the way. GLB and grid re-baked; walkability unchanged.
- Heights replaced by Grudgetide Shoals (`tools/mapgen-shoals.py`, 68×52, third in the list). Inspired by a random 256-hex seed (kept in the script): repeated "fed"/"efed" (feud), "3abefad3" read as "BE FADE" (a tide that comes and goes), "c55bdd"/"d05d55" as twin rival islands, "7a4d" as a 70 s tide cycle with 40 s low, six "ff" as cliffs. What's new compared with Crossing and Heights:
  - Point symmetry: new map mirror mode `"rot"` copies every op, prop, pad, core and spawn rotated 180° about the centre (`Terrain.canon`/`mirrorRect`/`both`). Each team's dry stone causeway lies on its own flank (west north, east south), so lanes are asymmetric per side, and to cross the north channel the east team must fight over the west team's causeway.
  - Tide: a `"tide"` cell op flags the mud flats (`FLAG_TIDE`; only low cells become `tideCells`). Map `tide: {lowSeconds 40, highSeconds 30, firstSeconds 45}`. At high tide `World.updateTide` turns those cells into fords (slow, costlier paths) and recomputes the nav grid; at low tide they're fast dry ground. Notices "HIGH TIDE · THE FLATS FLOOD" and "LOW TIDE · THE FLATS DRAIN". The water plane rises 0.42 m with `World.tideLevel()` (3 s ramps, render-only), so the flats visibly flood while islets, headlands, causeways and keeps stay dry. The flats are tinted as wet mud.
  - Layout: raised walled island keeps (cliffs you can drop off but not climb; gates N, S and E with ramps), headlands N and S, deep channels forming a pinwheel, a footbridge over each south-west channel, reed beds, a drowned-shrine islet in the centre (the Grudge's home), a forward pad at each causeway islet and outside the east gate, and a neutral islet per side. New "tide" emblem (crescent over waves).
- Remnil stun-lock fixed. Hero projectiles no longer make heroes flinch (`DamageOpts.noFlinch`); only splash finishers still do. Her volley is slower: 0.42 s per bolt (was 0.3), finisher 0.6 s (was 0.5), and a 0.9 s pause after the third bolt (was 0.3). Damage per second to a hero 149→108. A Warlord charging 9 m into her spam now closes in 2.4 s with 0.2 s of flinching; before it took 4.9 s, 1.8 s of it flinching.
- Map builder arches: direction now uses `rot % 180`, so arches rotated 180°/270° (the red side of point-symmetric maps) span their gates. Shoals GLB rebuilt.
- Menu redesign: every screen except Choose the Field takes its cues from the Field screen, a war-board with pinned, slightly tilted parchment cards, framed insets, wax seals, a header beam and a footer plank over the dimmed live world. New shared parts in `n64ui.ts`: `card` (pinned, tilted parchment), `inset` (dark frame), `windowCut` (clears the UI canvas so the live 3D scene shows through a framed window), `boardTitle`, `tag` (pinned menu tag that slides out with a gold arrow when selected). Each screen got its own random seed (`docs/menu-seeds.txt`), read for a motif:
  - Title ("PRESS START"): a single pinned proclamation, with the logo over a window onto the live battle.
  - Main (`fa0ce` → FACE): a big card whose face is a window onto the live battlefield with the logo stamped on it, the selected entry's name, blurb and seal below, and a stack of pinned tags with per-entry seal glyphs.
  - Players (`e10d`/`4ca4` → four): four pinned seat posters (SEAT I–IV) with a framed cloth inset and a P# seal, plus pinned tags for keyboard/mouse and the adapter search.
  - Versus Online (`caaace`, `cad` → ACE): Host and Join as two playing-card aces with corner pips in their suit colour and a big centre seal.
  - Battles (`3ebbed` → EBBED): a big Quick Join card and a noticeboard of pinned battle notes that drift slightly, green-pinned when open.
  - Rules (`deca…` → DECREE): "BY ORDER OF BOTH HOUSES", numbered articles with dot leaders, and a pinned article card with the value on a red plaque.
  - Options (`d1a1…` → DIAL): the same list layout, with a wooden dial whose needle points at the current value.
  - Records (`0eaf` → LEAF): an open leather ledger with cloth bookmarks for the tabs, the list on the left page and the selected champion, name or match in detail on the right page.
  - Controls (`1d4e4ad1e` → IDEA/AID): two pinned legend cards (controller, keyboard) grouped into FIGHT, COMMAND and BUILD; text shrinks to fit. Pause → Controls uses the same sheet.
  - Pause (`a3ea8d3fed`): a card with a window onto the frozen match and the time left painted over it, both houses' heroes, HP, talents and stats, and pinned Resume/Controls/Quit tags.
  - Results: a dispatch card with a window onto the final battlefield, the victor's house seal, a stats ledger with tug bars, and a pinned card per player (gold pin and seal for the winners).
  - Champion select: the roster shields became pinned framed portrait cards (pin in the colour of the house that sealed the champion). Banners, seals and cursors behave as before.
- Codex (new main-menu entry, `src/ui/codex.ts`). Entries:
  - Every champion: legend, role and what they fear, A/B/R/Z with a tip each, one page per evolution level with both choices, their icons and the synergy line, and how to win.
  - Basics (fighting, levelling and synergies), Army (troops, orders, outposts, gold), Base (towers and aggro, lane towers, rubble, keep/shop/recall, building), Arena (the Grudge, the Ogre, the cannon) and Fields (one per map, with its thumbnail).
  - Numbers come from the data files, so the text follows balance changes. The prose is hand-written and deliberately silly.
- Codex pictures are live gameplay, not renders. On an attack or evolution page the card has a window cut through it, and the game renderer draws a demo world only inside that window (`GameRenderer.demoCam`: screen rect, target, yaw, pitch, distance). `runDemo` in `main.ts` builds that world on Crossing's north plateau: the champion (with the evolution learned on evolution pages), six grunts that don't hit back, and the enemy hero parked away; waves, Ogre, cannon and relic are off. It loops the move (a 4-hit combo for A), resetting the grunts each loop. Evolution pages alternate between the two choices, and the one being shown is highlighted. The camera is side-on and slightly in front, pulled back for big area moves. Name tags and HP bars are hidden in the demo; damage numbers and callouts stay. Leaving the page restores the background world. An earlier version used static frames and pre-captured screenshots; both were dropped because they showed no effects.
- Menu backgrounds are a solid baked wooden war-table (`boardBg`); the live game only shows through framed windows (main menu, title, pause, results, Codex).
- Portraits are drawn on the high-res text layer (`hiImage`) instead of being squeezed into the 240-line canvas: the roster, champion-select stages, Records, pause, results, the Codex and the Choose the Field previews (rendered at 4× size). Icons render at 128 px and stages at 224×288, with a gentler turntable sway so heroes mostly face you.
- Champion select, bottom half: each player slot is a pinned card. It has the P#/name and CPU/PLAYER plaque, a framed cloth inset with the live portrait clipped to it, the evolution trees and SWORN seal over it, and the champion name and ability icons below. Open seats and "+ ADD CPU" are pinned cards too, SIT HERE sits above the card, and the "sworn" and "seats open" notices are pinned cards on the high-res layer.
- No flat PNG ground effects. `decal()` (used by every hero kit) now routes textures to 3D builders:
  - Crack, lava, moss and root decals → `fissures()`: jagged segmented trenches cut into the terrain that follow its height and spread outward, with a glowing molten core for lava and rubble lips along the edges.
  - Runes, halos, laurels and summon/hex circles → `sigil3d()`: spinning glowing rings with notches and spokes.
  - Engineer gear → an extruded 3D gear that rises, spins and sinks.
  - Rings and the smoke ring → 3D shockwaves.
  - `shockwave()` itself is now an expanding flattened torus instead of a textured plane.
  - War Cry's flat "shout" sprites became 3D rings bursting out at head height.
  - Zones (brambles, sinkhole, crater, lava, …) no longer lay a textured square on the ground (hidden). Their 3D pieces stay, and lava, crater and sinkhole zones get persistent 3D fissures.
  - Particles (dust, sparks, embers, smoke) stay as sprites, since that's what they are.
- Codex demos:
  - Moves that help allies (War Cry, Brambles, Repair, banners and rallies) get three of your own grunts standing beside the hero, so the buff has someone to land on. They don't attack.
  - Repair also gets a damaged allied tower next to the hero.
  - Stig's Siege Ramp demo walks him up the ramp after he raises it (longer loop, grunts placed past the platform).
- SIT HERE sits back at the bottom of the CPU's portrait frame, drawn on the high-res layer so it renders over the 3D portrait.
- Effect tweaks after review:
  - Remnil's hex and summon circles are back to the original painted circles (they read better than the 3D sigil).
  - Thorn's Brambles cast lost its rune sigils and flat rings; instead the zone grows roughly twice as many 3D bramble arches and thorn clumps.
  - Earthquake no longer spawns flat ground rings: no slam rings when the slam is a quake, and no ground ring on big Warlord hits. The upright per-hit impact ring stays.

## Performance pass (4-player split screen on laptops)
- Goal: a laptop must hold 4-player split screen fully zoomed out. Profiling showed the game was CPU-bound in three.js per-draw overhead (program/uniform setup and scene traversal), multiplied by up to four views. GPU time was small. Rule for every change: the same picture, fewer draw calls and less per-frame work.
- Benchmark: a deterministic harness (bots fast-forwarded with a fixed seed, then the sim locked to 1/60 s per rendered frame, four forced split views at max zoom-out, Chrome CPU throttled 4×) comparing HEAD against the working tree. Frame CPU time, original → now: early game 28.6 → 14.8 ms, mid game 64.8 → 18.5 ms, big fights 168 → 25.6 ms. Single view: 13.4 → 10.8 ms early, 48.9 → 16.1 ms in fights. GPU time in fights 2.65 → 1.2 ms.
- Troops: instanced skinning (`unitBatch.ts`). Each unit keeps its rig and mixer, but its body is hidden; per frame the bone matrices of all units of a type/team are packed into a float texture, and one instanced draw skins them all (`iSlot` per instance, so each view only draws what it sees). Hit flash is a per-instance emissive. Silhouettes use the same data.
- Buildings: models are merged at load (one mesh per kept node: `spin*`/`level2*` stay separate for animation), then every building of a team renders through one `BatchedMesh` multi-draw (`structureBatch.ts`) with all building textures in one texture array; flash goes through the batch colour. Ballistas are built from cached templates with one texture-array material and go through `meshBatch.ts` (generic instancing keyed by geometry and team).
- HP/work bars are one instanced quad batch; blob shadows are one instanced mesh; pad markers are one `FxBatch`.
- Silhouettes moved out of a second render pass: proxies live in `silScene`, which is now a child of the main scene with renderOrder 1000, so they still draw last.
- Map props are merged into one draw with a texture array; the terrain shader skips texture samples whose blend weight is exactly zero (`textureGrad` with derivatives taken outside the branches, so mip selection is unchanged).
- Effects: particles from different textures share instanced draws (up to 12 textures per draw, chosen per instance); hazard sprites and entity sprites (tags, badges, marks, hints) go through `spriteBatch.ts`; debris, shards, chunks, rings, slabs, spikes, ground decals and falling leaves go through `fxInstances.ts` (per-instance opacity/emissive, back-to-front sorting per view when translucent); fissures, sigils, bramble zones, walls and Thorn's roots/vines are merged per cast with their grow/reveal/sink animation moved into the vertex shader.
- Damage numbers: hits on the same target within 0.3 s add up into one number (strongest colour wins, small re-pop), and all numbers/labels draw from one glyph atlas.
- HUD: player panels, team headers, the army roster and the order cross are cached as bitmaps keyed by everything they show, rendered with the same transform shifted by whole device pixels so they're pixel-identical; text and talent icons cache their downscaled versions at the exact on-screen size.
- Aimed moves only open the aim reticle when the move can actually fire (B/R off cooldown, Z meter full); otherwise the press just sends the move as before. Pressing L (block) while aiming cancels the aim without firing. The controls sheet mentions it.

## Land around the maps
- The out-of-bounds land is designed per map instead of an empty green mound. One shared layout module (`src/sim/surround.ts`, chosen by `surround` in the map JSON) defines heights, terrain paint and a list of props; the game builds the terrain from it at load and `bake-map` writes the props plus a coarse height grid into `grid.json` for Blender (`tools/blender/surround_kit.py` models them into the map GLB). Each design is written for the canonical half and mirrored the same way as the field; river exits are found from the water cells along the field edge.
- Terrain now runs out 400 m on a grid that is 1 m near the field and coarser further out, so the horizon is real land (or sea) fading into fog instead of a mound edge. Outside the field, AO and shadows use cheaper samples; prop shadows use a bucketed lookup, which made map loading faster than before overall.
- Crossing (valley): both rivers continue north, merge and run up a valley through snowy mountains; to the south they feed a lake with an islet. The moat streams loop behind each keep and drain to the lake. A road leaves a back gatehouse (team-coloured towers), crosses a bridge and runs through a village of plaster cottages with thatch and clay-tile roofs, a well and banners. Farm districts with wheat, plough, green and fallow fields, hedgerows, fences and haystacks; windmills; forests on the hills.
- Ruins (town): the keep sits at ground level inside a dry moat, with bridges where its streets leave, a broken outer bailey wall with stump towers, roofless ruined houses along the cobbled streets, a graveyard with a ruined chapel, a dry well and fallen columns, then moorland with dead and living copses and low mountains. An earlier version (keep on a mesa above a chasm, then an earthquake fault) was dropped as nonsensical.
- Shoals (sea): tidal sand flats with pools and sandbars shelving into open sea to the horizon (the sea rises with the tide). Each keep gets piers, a fishing hut and team-sailed boats at the real shoreline; lighthouses, wrecks, stilt huts, sea stacks, buoys and distant pine islands. The field itself uses sand instead of grass (a fifth terrain texture blended into the dirt channel), dirt stays dirt.
- New photo textures: sand (`coast_sand_01`) and thatch.

## Free-for-all (4 houses)
- New mode alongside 1v1 and 2v2: four players, each their own house (team p = player p), heroes for everyone (no commanders), CPUs fill empty seats. Champion select cycles 1v1 → 2v2 → FFA; FFA maps (`teams: 4`, `mode: "ffa"`) only show in FFA and 2-team maps only in 1v1/2v2. Online FFA is in the lobby spec (4 seats); CPU wins still don't count toward records.
- Houses are blue, red, yellow, green (teams 0–3); yellow/green follow the P3/P4 cursor chip colours. Neutral moved to team index 4 (the Ogre).
- Rules: every pad outside the keeps is neutral. Losing your keep eliminates your house: its buildings and troops die, the hero never respawns, and the player spectates (camera follows the nearest standing hero). Last house standing wins; at the time limit the standing houses are ranked by core damage dealt, then fewest structures lost, then hero kills. Troops, towers and bots aim at the nearest standing enemy keep; catch-up compares against the strongest rival; Thorn's walls must keep every spawn reachable.
- Maps use a new `quad` symmetry: author one quarter, everything is rotated in 90° steps with team + 1 per step (pads, keeps, spawns, props, surround features). Optional `dens` place the Ogre on maps where the old midpoint rule lands in a mountain.
- HUD: split views get each player's own house panels in their corner; a single view shows the other three houses as a standings strip; eliminations get a banner. Results rank winner first then reverse elimination order; pause shows all four.
- Map mechanics live in `src/sim/mapEvents.ts` (sim time only, so lockstep stays deterministic) with visuals in `src/render/mapFx.ts`.

### Frostcross Summit (seed ec88083c…)
- A plus-shaped mountain top. The cross is the high ground: the summit plaza (the Grudge) sits highest and each arm slopes down to its keep at the tip. The corners are deep cirques (blocked) that open into glacial valleys, crossed by narrow rock causeways linking neighbouring arms (the Ogre dens sit on them). Earlier version with peaks in the corners was dropped because the southern peaks hid the arms from the camera.
- Mechanic: avalanches. Every 38 s (first at 50 s) one arm, rotating clockwise, rumbles for 6 s (falling snow, notice, light shake), then a wall of snow sweeps down it toward the keep: heroes take a fixed hit with knockback and slow, troops lose most of their health, buildings are untouched. It leaves a 14 s snow drift that slows movement (cells become fords) and melts away.
- Surround (`alpine`): switchback supply roads with lanterns and log cabins run down valleys behind each keep to frozen lakes with a shrine; snow peaks with glaciers between them; pine forests below. Snow texture (`snow_02`) replaces grass on the field; dirt paths stay dirt.

### Bellwick Gardens (seed 9978ed11…)
- A formal walled garden with keeps in the four corners (eight-fold symmetric: the quarter is also mirrored on its diagonal). A gravel avenue with a roundabout tower pad runs from each keep mouth to an octagonal inner court; hedge parterres (hedge borders around blocked flower beds) split the rest into corridors.
- Mechanic 1: bell gates. Iron portcullises sit in two sets that swap every 40 s (first at 45 s, 5 s bell warning): the outer border gates near the keeps, or the mid border gates plus the court gates. The avenues never close, so every keep can always reach the court. Anyone standing in a closing gate is pushed out.
- Mechanic 2: the fountain. The Grudge's altar stands on an island inside a shallow basin; heroes wading in the basin heal 32/s.
- Surround (`garden`): a hedge and gravel promenade, a stone-edged canal ring with bridges, tree-lined avenues from each corner to that house's manor (house-coloured flag), lawns with mowing stripes, an orchard, gazebos, topiary and flower parterres, then wooded hills. Gravel (`gravel_floor`) replaces dirt on the field.
- Fixes found along the way: interior rim cells no longer add a shadow caster, and with a surround the map-edge rim doesn't either (it drew a dark line around the field where the land outside drops away).

## Map mechanics for the 2-team maps
- Every map now has its own element: Shoals the tide, Crossing river mist, Ruins the Bone Lantern (FFA: Frostcross avalanches, Bellwick bells and fountain). All run in `src/sim/mapEvents.ts` on sim time and are configured in the map JSON; visuals in `src/render/mapFx.ts`, synth sounds in `src/audio/sfx.ts`.
- Crossing, river mist (`mist`): every 75 s (first at 60 s, 5 s warning) a mist bank rolls down both channels from the north over 6 s, holds 16 s, then thins out southward. It covers water, fords and bridges plus 2 cells of bank, only in the river strip (x 30–74, not the keep moats). Anything inside counts as in tall grass: hidden from towers and from range, ambush bonus on the first swing. Both channels at once so it stays mirror-fair. Picked over a drifting supply barge (a moving neutral entity would have touched targeting, rendering and bots everywhere) and over a flash flood (too close to the Shoals tide).
- Ruins, the Bone Lantern (`lantern`): every 60 s (first at 55 s) a lantern rises out of a random pit (3 s), drifts at 1.4 m/s to the nearest of the two empty corners (top-left and bottom-right, both on the mirror diagonal so equally far from both keeps), and rests there 25 s before fading. The first hero within reach, while drifting or resting, is haunted for 20 s: +30% damage and +15% speed (`status.hauntUntil`, shown as the buff badge, green wisps). Bots go for it when it's within 22 m and they aren't in a fight. Both corners got graves, broken columns and dead trees so they read as old graveyards.
- Codex: every field has its own glyph and a second page for its mechanic, with numbers read from the map JSON. Bellwick Gardens got its text, and Frostcross's text now matches the ridge-and-cirque layout.
- Ruins outposts: Ruins has too few pads for three specialised production buildings, so it uses a single mixed Outpost (`outposts: true` in the map JSON). Same cost, upgrade and health band as the others; every soldier it sends is picked at random (45% grunts, 35% ranged, 20% heavies, from the sim RNG so lockstep stays in sync). Any production build on that map becomes an Outpost, so input and bots need no changes; the HUD build cross shows a single OUTPOST option. New model `assets/structures/outpost.glb`: a timber hut with a team roof band, an archery target and a forge chimney with an anvil.

## Event cards and minimap
- Map events (avalanche, bells, river mist, Bone Lantern, tide) no longer go through the plain notice banner, where they were short-lived and easily overwritten. They show an event card under the clock: parchment with the map's wax-seal glyph, a title in the event's colour (the haunted house's colour for the lantern), a one-line explanation and a countdown for warnings. Mist lifting, the lantern fading and low tide get cards too. The plain notice banner is smaller so the two never overlap. Tide got a sound.
- Minimap: dead centre in split screen (where the views meet), bottom-centre with a single view. Background is an orthographic top-down render of the actual map (`Portraits.mapTop`) in a wood frame, drawn on the hi-res layer. Shows keeps (house colour, gold rim, health bar, crossed out when fallen), pads (hollow ring in zone colour when empty; filled square for outposts, triangle for towers, gold for level 2, faded while building), troops as small dots, heroes as house-coloured arrows showing facing (green halo when haunted), the Ogre, the Grudge (gold, house colour when shrined, bobbing over its carrier), cannon targets, closed gates, the avalanche lane (flashing during the warning, filling as the snow comes down), the mist band and the flooded flats. Hidden units (grass, mist, stealth) aren't shown, since everyone shares one screen.

## HUD cleanup
- Event card titles have no drop shadow.
- Build, upgrade and shop prompts are painted icons with the button badge in the corner, no words (`tools/gen-prompt-icons.mjs`, one fal sheet: hut = outpost, watchtower = tower, hammer and anvil = upgrade, coin purse = shop). They sit small on the empty pad; for upgrades and the shop they sit at the foot of the building, just left of its health bar (sprite anchor offset, so it holds from any camera angle).
- Building health, build/upgrade progress and the keep shield bars sit snug under the building instead of floating above it: anchored at the base centre and pushed a short way straight down in view space, so they stay under the building from any camera angle. Prompt icons sit on a dark gold-rimmed disc so they read against the ground.
- Team header: the keep diamond is bigger and is the keep's health (filled from the bottom in the house colour, flashing when low). The shield is a light-blue border around it that loses length as the shield takes damage. Gold and troop count sit stacked beside it, replacing the two bars.
- Player panel is one row: B and R with cooldown numbers, Z with its gauge as a ring around the button, then the level number with an XP ring, then the talent icons. Replaces the long Z and XP bars.
- One status line under the clock: the Grudge status and the small notice banner share it. A new notice replaces the line for its 2 s, then it shows the current Grudge state again, so the two never stack. Big banners (VICTORY, countdowns) are unchanged.
- Level-up picker is just the two talent icons on dark discs with a small C-left / C-right badge; no title, names or descriptions (the codex has those). A choice that combos with a talent you already own (its `with` list) gets a flashing red rim instead of gold. It sits right under that player's own panel in their HUD corner, badges drawn on top of the icons. The "LEVEL UP!" line in the player panel is gone.
- Pad, upgrade and shop prompt icons keep their approved size at the default play camera distance (~16 m from the hero) and grow with distance when zoomed out so they stay the same size on screen (up to 6.5×), per split view.
- Army roster (bottom corners) restyled to match: one dark gold-rimmed disc per troop type with its portrait, the count in a small pill under it, and the current order as a coloured badge with a glyph instead of a word (attack red double chevron pointing at the enemy, follow blue arrow, defend green shield, hold gold bar, hunt orange crosshair, siege purple tower). The selected group gets a bright rim and freshly ordered troops flash white.

## Herald
- Checked the commander end to end: bot Heralds use all four moves (shot, war banner, war horn, rally) and give orders; orders override the partner's; the camera frames the whole army; Heralds level for stats only (no talent tree, so no picks). Fixed: a human Herald had no player panel (commanders were filtered out of the panel list); now B/R/Z show, without level/talents.
- Take up the banner: in 2v2 without commander seats, holding X (0.6 s, away from a pad) turns your champion into the standard Herald after a 0.9 s rooted channel (golden spiral and house-coloured streaks, then a flash, light pillar and smoke while the Herald model pops in). Health fraction carries over; level, XP, talents and stats are saved and the Herald runs at base stats with no XP gain. You become your house's commander (D-pad orders, order priority, army camera). One Herald per house. You stay a Herald until you die (you respawn as yourself) or hold X at your own keep to change back for 50 gold. HUD shows a filling ring with the banner seal under your panel while you hold. Config in `match.json` `arena.morph`. Codex: ARENA › THE HERALD.
- Herald kit additions (picked: field supply + formations; lasso squads, point orders and escort were offered and not taken):
  - L (no block for commanders) cycles the house formation: loose (the old ring), column (single file behind the anchor, 1.15 m apart), line (two ranks facing the nearest enemy keep, brutes/grunts front, archers back, leash ×0.75), wedge (V pointing at the enemy, leash ×1.3). Applies to troops on hold and follow; troops holding the same point form one formation. Shown as a dot-pattern badge beside the army roster when the house has a commander or a non-loose formation.
  - Rally (Z) also cuts the partner's remaining B and R cooldowns by half, anywhere on the map.
  - Inside his active war banner (6 m), his house can open the shop and build/upgrade on any pad within the banner's reach.
- Portrait framing ignores props that stick far above the head (the Herald's banner pole): when the model's top is more than half a head-height above the head bone's crown, icons and select-screen stages frame from the feet to the crown at the head instead of the bounding box. Other heroes are unaffected.

## Ogre patrol
- The Ogre walks a beat instead of standing at his spawn: each map defines `patrols` (endpoint pairs, mirrored like everything else). He spawns at a random end of a random route, walks it at 60% speed, pauses 2 s at each end to look around, and turns back.
- Routes: Crossing north-south down the middle of the island; Ruins corner to corner between the two empty graveyards; Shoals across the flats past the centre isle; Frostcross along each corner causeway; Bellwick through each quadrant's middle corridor between the outer and mid corridors.
- He sees 9 m in a forward cone but only ~3.8 m behind and to the sides, so heroes can slip past his back. When he spots someone he shouts and charges at full speed; he gives up once they're more than 16 m from his route and walks back to it.

## Hero model detail pass
- Grim (raider) rebuilt at ~6.4k tris (was ~1k), same rig, proportions and silhouette so all clips still fit: smoother shaped head with brow ridge, cheeks, long hooked nose, tusks, droopy ears with inner ears and a gold earring; deep hood with cap, rim and floppy tail; knotted team scarf with tails; tunic, leather jerkin, two bandolier straps with throwing knives and a buckle; belt with buckle, two pouches and a green bottle; front and back team loincloth flaps; curved panelled cloak with a tattered hem; padded shoulders, wrapped riveted bracers; hands with fingers and thumbs around the grips; daggers with crossguards, wrapped grips, pommels and swept flat blades; wrapped shins, cuffed boots with soles and curled toes.
- New reusable builder helpers in `tools/blender/build_heroes.py`: `ring` (torus via lathe), `sheet` (thick curved shell, used for cloaks and flaps, with `cloak_rows` for tattered hems) and `blade` (swept flat blade along a curve).
- Remnil (summoner) rebuilt at ~7k tris: open cowl with a pointed arched opening (gold-trimmed edges, dark lining, a deep void face with glowing eyes, brow shadows and a pale bony jaw), a pointed folded hood with a curled gold-tipped tail; tattered capelet; folded robe with a tattered hem, team trim and a team front panel; gold-set crystal brooch; flared folded bell sleeves with lining and trim; skeletal jointed fingers; rope sash with dangling skull charms and a chained spellbook; pointed shoes under the hem; a gnarled staff with twine wraps and knots, a three-pronged wooden claw cradling the crystal and a hanging skull charm. New helpers: `folded` (lathe with fabric folds, more toward the hem) and `skull`.
- Francois (duelist) rebuilt at ~7.7k tris: smoother head with nose, ears, curled moustache and goatee; full wavy hair mass to the shoulders with curled ends; cavalier hat with a wide brim pinned up on one side by a jewelled gold brooch, hat band and a four-feather plume; folded doublet with button placket, scalloped lace collar; puffed slashed team shoulders; team baldric and a gold-trimmed team cape over one shoulder with a brooch; belt, buckle and a leather scabbard with a gold chape; flared gauntlet gloves with fingers; puffy breeches; bucket-top boots with folded cuffs, heels, soles and spurs; rapier with a swept hilt (rings, knuckle bow, quillons, wire-wrapped grip, pommel) and a thin diamond blade. New helper: `curl`.
- Faces, new technique: human faces are modelled instead of painted on a 64 px decal (which read as tiny slit eyes on a flat moon). `human_head` sculpts an icosphere into an oval skull with a narrowing jaw, a slightly forward chin and a flattened face plane; `surf(x, z)` places features on that actual surface: almond eye whites with iris, pupil, catchlight and an upper-lid line, a soft brow ridge, arched brows, a nose with bridge, tip and nostril wings, upper and lower lips and a chin. Francois uses it (~14k tris); his curly hair is two rows of ringlets framing the face, each a solid core tube wrapped by a tight corkscrew (`tube`, a radius-varying sweep along a path) so the coils overlap and read as one connected curl. No goatee, no skin brow ridge; nose has a slim bridge, rounded tip, nostril wings and dark nostrils; lips touch; fuller moustache. Stig's overall straps run from the bib up under the beard and over the shoulders.
- Stig (engineer) rebuilt at ~8k tris with the modelled-face technique (broad sculpted head, kindly blue eyes with lids, big bushy white brows with tufts, a large rounded red-tipped nose, ears) under a riveted leather aviator cap with ear flaps and brass goggles; a folded white beard ending in three braids with gold rings and moustache curls; folded overalls with bib pocket and tools, straps and buttons; riveted team pauldrons; leather gloves with fingers and cuffs; tool belt with pouches, hammer and oil can; iron-capped boots with soles and straps; backpack crate with brass corners, straps, chimney with cap, pressure gauge, coiled pipe and a side gear; golden wrench with wrapped handle, open jaw and an adjuster screw.
- Thorn (warden) rebuilt at ~6.5k tris: ridged bark head with a brow ridge, deep eye hollows with glowing green eyes, knot nose and cheeks, a dark mouth crack and a hanging moss beard; spreading branch antlers with leaf clusters, a red mushroom and tiny flowers; ridged trunk torso with moss patches, a spiralling leafy vine and a team sash strap with a leaf brooch; leafy shoulders with branch spikes; knotted bark arms with moss; root-finger hands; trunk legs with spreading root toes; a round plank shield with an iron rim and boss, a team ring and a leafy vine. New helper: `branch`.
- Herald rebuilt at ~5.8k tris: smooth great helm with a gold band, visor slit, breathing holes, a gold crest ridge and a five-feather plume; gorget rings; breastplate with a centre ridge and gold rivets; front and back team tabard with gold edges and a gold heraldic crest; layered three-lame pauldrons with gold edges; belt, buckle, pouch and a steel fauld; rerebraces, elbow cops, vambraces with gold cuffs and finger-plate gauntlets; thigh plates, knee cops, greaves with a ridge and segmented pointed sabatons; back banner pole strapped across the chest with a gold spear finial and crossbar, a swallowtail team banner with gold edges and crest; arming sword with crossguard, wrapped grip, pommel and a flat blade.
- Warlord rebuilt at ~8.5k tris (`build_warlord.py`, importing the helpers from `build_heroes.py`): sculpted ogre head (wide underbite jaw) with a heavy brow, small yellow eyes with lids, a broad nose with nostrils, a dark mouth, a row of teeth and two tusks, big ears with gold rings; riveted iron helmet with team band, crest strip and curving ringed horns; folded fur tunic with a tattered hem, team sash strap with a skull brooch; spiked team-trimmed pauldrons with straps; thick belt with a gold buckle, pouches and hanging bone trophies; folded fur skirt and a team loincloth with gold trim; bulging muscular arms with spiked iron bracers and big fingers; fur leg wraps, strapped soled boots with claws; knotted club with iron bands, more spikes and a wrapped handle.
- Troops rebuilt on their 8-bone rigs (helpers imported from `build_heroes.py`): grunt ~2.4k tris (smoother goblin head with nose and inner ears, team pot-helm with rim, ridge, spike and nose guard, folded jerkin, front and back tabards, belt, buckle, pouch, shoulder pad, wrapped arms and shins, pointed shoes, wrapped spear with a leaf blade, round shield with iron rim, team ring and boss); archer ~1.9k (folded team hood with a tail, team scarf, folded jerkin and skirt, belt with knife, quiver of fletched arrows on a strap, recurve bow with wrapped grip and string, bracer); brute ~3.1k (modelled grey-ogre face with brow, eyes, nose, tusks and jaw, folded team bandana with tails, riveted iron chest plates, crossed straps with a gold boss, team pauldron with spikes, front and back tabards, bracers, fingers, leg wraps, hammer with banded stone head, spikes and wrapped haft); Ogre ~3.1k (modelled face with tusks, topknot with a bone ring, belly, bone-tooth necklace, bark pauldron with spikes and strap, leather loincloths, toenails, a tapered bark club with iron spikes and grip wraps).
- Perf check against a worktree with the old models (same code, ports 5199/5200, 4-way split, deterministic): CPU frame time unchanged within noise (both 38-44 ms at 4x throttle mid-game); GPU 1.15 -> 1.40 ms/frame in fights.
- Francois: a hair shell covers the temples, sides and back down to ear level, and both ringlet rows start right under the hair band, so the curls grow from the hair instead of floating below a bare-skin gap.
- Thorn texturing: ~40 fallen autumn leaves (orange, red, gold, brown) scattered over the trunk following its profile, plus leaves on each arm and leg segment and the head; darker bark plates, vertical grooves, three knotholes and pale lichen patches; some shoulder, antler and crown foliage uses a new painted autumn leaf texture (`leaves_fall`) so his canopy mixes green and fall colours. ~10.4k tris.
- Brute redesigned as an Iron Juggernaut so it can't be confused with the Ogre: a hunched troll sealed in riveted iron plate (only bits of grey arm and thigh show). Bucket helm with a glowing eye slit, face grille, team bands and a team fin crest with swept-back horns; barrel breastplate with team bands, rivets and a spiked team boss; triple-layered team-trimmed pauldrons with spikes; hanging chain; plated fauld with a team tabard; plated gauntlets with spiked knuckles; greaves with knee cops and segmented sabatons; a two-handed war maul with team-painted faces, end caps and spikes, held lowered across the body. ~5.9k tris.
- Loading splash regenerated with fal (`tools/gen-loading.mjs art`) from a lineup of the new in-game model renders, with the prompt updated to describe the new looks; logo unchanged.
- Pressing L (block) while carrying the Grudge drops it at your feet (bots never do this by accident). Codex and controls sheet mention it; the controls sheet also says holding X away from a pad in 2v2 turns you into the Herald.
- Codex live scenes (`art: { kind: "scene" }`) reuse the codex demo world: the Grudge on its altar (THE GRUDGE p1), the keep with its ward (KEEP AND SHOP p1), and a grunt, archer and brute side by side (YOUR TROOPS p1). The Veterans page shows a gold wax stamp with a new rank-chevron glyph.

## Economy and respawn
- Income is now territory: the keep pays the old flat rate (3.5 gold/s) and every finished building on a pad adds to it (home pad +0.35/s, forward or neutral pad +0.45/s); losing pads, especially contested ones, cuts income and the enemy who builds there gains it. The current rate is shown next to the gold counter (`World.incomeOf`).
- Respawn: cooldowns are frozen at death (time remaining per move) and restored at respawn plus a 2 s penalty on every move, B and R included, so a fresh respawn can't dump its whole kit on a diver in the base. A partly charged Z meter resets to zero; a full one is kept. The killing blow no longer charges the victim's meter.
- Comeback tuning (target: a 90 s lead wins ~75%; it was 85-90% before this change): catch-up `incomeBoost` 0.6 -> 1.2, `productionBoost` 0.4 -> 0.8, `fortify` 0.3 -> 0.45, contested pad income 0.45. Measured over 40 bot matches each on all three 2-team maps and two matchups: 63-83%, average ~74%.
- Menu and results windows: UI window cut-outs report their on-screen rect (`markWindow`), and the renderer draws the match camera inside that rect with its aspect, framed on the heroes, instead of a full-screen camera whose centre sat behind the menu (the window used to show the forest north of the field).
- FFA jump pads (`jumppads` in the map JSON, one-way, mirrored like everything else; 12 per FFA map): a wooden deck on a coiled iron spring over a stone footing, with a painted arrow pointing where it throws you. Stepping on one roots the hero for a 1 s charge while the spring slowly compresses and shudders (crouch pose); taking any damage during the charge cancels it with a puff. After firing or being interrupted the pad goes on a 5 s cooldown (spring sits compressed, grey dot on the minimap) so nobody can chase an escape through the same pad. Then the spring snaps and wobbles out, dust and streaks burst off it, and the hero flies a real arc (body tilts, speed trail) to the landing spot over 1-2.6 s depending on distance, with a landing thud. Not while carrying the Grudge; 1.2 s cooldown after landing so you can step off the landing pad. Routes per quarter: plaza to keep, keep to plaza, and one cross-map pad to another quarter, so getting around is a network with some walking between pads. Shown on the minimap as gold dots with dashed landing lines. Bots take a pad when it saves more than ~12 m of walking.
- Codex: the Herald has his own section right after the champions, laid out like a champion entry: commander overview (portrait), live codex-world demos of A shot, B war banner, R war horn and Z rally, an L formations scene (nine troops on hold cycling loose/column/line/wedge) and an X transform scene (Warlord takes up the banner and back, on a loop). Field pages now render each actual map live from above with a slow drift (both pages of each field) instead of the old pre-baked thumbnail.
- Level-up auto-pick: a pending talent choice is made for you after 10 s (sim-side, deterministic): the option that combos with a talent you already own if there is one, otherwise random. The picker shows the countdown between the two icons.
- Menu/results window on FFA maps follows one hero's fight up close (rotating every 12 s, max 34 m wide) instead of framing all four spread-out heroes.
- Frostcross causeways: the neutral pad on each causeway (too far from everything to matter) is replaced by an Avalanche Horn, a bone war-horn on a snowy cairn with a pennant in the last user's colour. A hero standing at it with no rival hero nearby for 3 s blows it: the next avalanche goes down a neighbouring arm, never the blower's own (if neither arm is theirs, the one with more enemy troops in its lane), with the usual 6 s warning; then the horn recharges for 75 s. Ring on the ground shows capture progress (house colour) or recharge (grey); minimap shows horns as white triangles; event card and a low horn blast sound. Bots go for ready horns nearby sometimes.
- Bombs: a house can buy one every 20 s; heroes carrying a bomb can't use jump pads. Bots only buy a bomb with 250 gold to spare, at least 4 troops alive and their troops already near an enemy tower, and at most once every 90 s each (was ~11 bombs per bot per match, now ~1.5-2). Eliminated FFA bots stop acting (fixes the rare 'reading structure' crash when a bot's keep was gone).
- FFA armies (measured: 5-9 live troops per house, ~450 gold unspent at 4 min, i.e. production-bound): FFA only, troops every 12 s instead of 16, soldier cap x1.5 (24 at the default 16), outposts and troops 25% cheaper (`match.json` `ffa`). Each keep gets a guard of 3 archers that hold a post in front of the keep whatever the orders, don't count toward the cap, and respawn one at a time every 25 s. After: Bellwick armies average 8-14 with peaks of 20-34; Frostcross 7-11 with peaks around 20. 1v1/2v2 unchanged.
- Bot build plan is human-like: barracks and range at home, a home tower, then a foundry and a second barracks on the nearest forward/neutral pads, never more than 4 outposts; everything after that is towers (damage, control, a support at home). Plan zone "front" means any non-home pad (FFA maps have no forward pads).
- FFA outposts send one extra troop per wave (`ffa.outpostBonus`), so 3-4 outposts make a real army. Measured: FFA armies average 11-14 per house and reach the 24 cap, with bots on ~3.5 outposts and ~2 towers.
- FFA targeted attack: ordering ATTACK while that group is already attacking cycles the target house: nearest keep, then each standing rival in turn, then nearest again (`TeamState.attackTeam`). Troops on attack march on that house's keep; a notice names the target and the attack badge on the army roster takes the target house's colour.
- Select/stage cursor art regenerated with fal (`tools/gen-cursor-art.mjs`, sliced by `tools/cursor-slice.py`): player chips are lumpy wax seals stamped 1P-4P/CP in house colours, the hands are a brown leather gauntlet with riveted iron knuckle plates and an iron cuff seen from the back (point, grab, open), and the player tags are small swallowtail cloth pennants. Sprites are 2x resolution and drawn at half scale; hotspots retuned.
- Champion portrait cards (select screen, HUD, codex list) frame every hero on the head bone at Stig's tightness (head-and-shoulders) instead of the model's top, which had pushed horns, plumes and antlers into the shot and shrunk the face; Stig keeps the original framing.
- Keep shield: if the ward is broken by damage, the shop won't sell a new one for 15 s (`shop.ward.brokenLockout`).
- FFA: everything moves 20% faster (heroes and troops; `ffa.speedMul`).
- Cannon aiming: the aim reticle is added to the camera's framing points (and its must-keep set), and the camera may widen past its usual max to fit hero and reticle, so the reticle stays on screen as it moves.
- Prompt icons keep a constant on-screen size at every zoom (scale = camera distance / 16, measured ~60 px from closest to farthest zoom); the earlier floor/cap let them shrink when zoomed out far.
- Minimap is 50% translucent everywhere and sits lower (4 px from the bottom) in single view.
- Personal camera (any view following one hero) centres on that hero: focus is 85% the hero (and cannon reticle), 15% the rest of the framing points, with a tight keep-in-view box (±0.45 x, ±0.3 y), and no longer clamps to the map bounds (the surround is real land now). Max zoom-out for these views is capped at ~0.9 of the map so you don't look at empty land. Measured: hero settles at screen centre at every map corner, in auto and manual zoom.
- Camera mode is per player: the global setting is only SPLIT VIEW / SHARED VIEW (select-screen ribbon and options); each human's champion card has an AUTO ZOOM / D-PAD ZOOM plaque they click to toggle (saved in `options.zoom[slot]`; old "split · d-pad zoom" saves migrate to split with every player on d-pad zoom).
- Under-attack feedback: when your keep takes a hit (ward or health) the keep gem shakes hard for 0.7 s; the header also shows how many pads your house holds (gold-and-house-colour pad icon × count), and when any of your pad buildings is hit that counter shakes, its icon flashes red and the number turns orange.
- Name tags: picking your name on the champion card opens a list first (dark wood panel of parchment slips): a red "ADD >" slip on top opens the keyboard, then your saved names (most recent first, with W-L), and "NO NAME" if you're signed. Names held by another seat are greyed with a red TAKEN stamp and skipped; typing a held name shows NAME TAKEN. One name per seat, no sharing (host enforces this for online seats too).
- Tags are stored locally keyed by a UUID (`save.data.tags[uuid] = { name, ... }`); old name-keyed saves migrate on load. Adding a name that already exists locally reuses that tag. Local records only update tags this machine owns.
- Server stats: the online host sends one result per match to the relay (`{t:"result"}` over the websocket), which records per-UUID W/L/D, kills and hero records in `.grudge-stats.json` (or `STATS_FILE`). Only matches with humans on at least two teams count, CPU-team wins are ignored, duplicate UUIDs reject the report, one report per room per minute. `GET /net/stats` lists the top 50 and recent matches (no UUIDs); `GET /net/stats?id=<uuid>` returns one tag.
- Docker: stats live in `/app/data/stats.json` (`STATS_FILE`), a node-owned volume; mount it to keep stats across redeploys. Server code must stay strip-only TypeScript (no parameter properties, enums or namespaces) because the image runs `node tools/server.ts` directly.
- Codex: the Herald's L (formations) and X (morph) demos no longer give him demo invulnerability, which made him blink (invulnerable heroes flicker). The Herald's codex row shows his portrait like the champions.
