- The shared "sigil" ground model (two rings + spokes) is gone. Each user now has its own 3D piece: Warlord (war cry, roar, quake) gets an iron crest — a band of dark iron spikes (long barbed ones alternating with short) that bursts up out of the ground around a molten ring with scattered shards; Herald banner plant and rally get a gold laurel wreath (two leafy branches with a ribbon knot) at the Herald's feet; Herald horn (two counter-spinning bands) and the rally area use a gold crenellated crown band with spires and gems. The sunburst sprites over the banner (beams) and rally (rays) are replaced with a rising fleur-de-lis plus sparks, and a ring of stars that drift up.
- Invulnerable heroes no longer blink. They wear a gold ward: a faceted crystal shell (icosahedron, per-facet rim light, a light band sweeping upward, occasional facet glints) that fades in and out and pulses gently. Same trigger as the old blink (invulnerable and not mid-dodge or super), so respawn protection, leaps and other i-frames all read the same way.
- Spoked-ring audit: the only spoked model was the shared 3D "sigil" (now replaced). Checked every champion and Herald ability / level-up codex demo frame by frame plus status marks, map mechanics and spawn effects; nothing spoked remains.
- Invulnerability ward reworked: no bubble. The hero model gets a gold outline (inverted hull, pulsing gold-white) plus a wider additive gold glow hull, built lazily per hero the first time they're invulnerable (hero outlines are off in render config, so these are dedicated ward hulls).
- Champion select plaques: CPU level is now three gems next to the CPU plaque (empty sockets; filled gold for easy, orange for normal, red for hard), no "LV" text; hover shows "CPU EASY/NORMAL/HARD". The camera toggle is an icon chip (eye = auto zoom, d-pad cross = d-pad zoom) with a hover tip. The plaque and its chip are centred as a group so nothing spills off narrow 2v2/FFA cards.
- Online: CPU level is in the lobby view, so guests see it; guests can click a CPU's gems too ({t:"lvl"} to the host), and host changes push to guests immediately.
- Music: the procedural chiptune is replaced by real CC0 tracks from OpenGameArt (credits in `assets/music/CREDITS.txt`): Old Tower Inn on title/menus, Minstrel Dance on champion/map select and the online lobby, Determined Pursuit in battle, Epic Boss Battle in sudden death, Rejoicing on the results screen. All are seamless loops, level-matched to about -16 LUFS, 128 kbps MP3 (~6 MB total). The current track loads first and the rest prefetch in the background; switching crossfades over 1.2 s; pausing ducks the battle music to 35% instead of stopping it.
- Battle music slowed to 85% speed with the pitch dropping along with it (about 2.8 semitones lower): less frantic, darker and a bit eerie. Loop is now 127.06 s.
- Keep shield: in 1v1 and FFA the keep's ward starts at half (`shop.ward.soloScale` 0.5, 750 instead of 1500); 2v2 is unchanged. Buying a shield now patches 25% of that maximum (`shop.ward.buyFraction`) instead of refilling it, capped at the maximum. Grudge regen and HUD/bar fractions use the mode's maximum. 20-match warlord v warden sim on Crossing: average keep damage 440 → 551; win split unchanged (that matchup was already 20/80 before the change).
- Broken keep shield lockout raised from 15 s to 45 s, the same as the buy cooldown, so losing the built-in shield costs as much shop time as buying one.
- Level 3 towers: a level-2 Damage or Control tower can be specialised for 250 gold (`specCost`, scaled by the usual cost multipliers and Stig's upgrade discount). Hold Y at it: the cross menu becomes LEVEL 3 with three choices (up/left/right); tapping Y there does nothing. It builds like an upgrade (upgradeSeconds), +30-40% HP. Defined in `data/structures.json` under each type's `specs`.
  - Damage: BALLISTA (×2.6 damage, 2.1 s cooldown, ×1.3 range; the bolt hits its target and everything within 1.1 m of the line up to 6 m past it for 75%; grunts take at least 80%), FIREPOTS (lobbed pot, 2.6 m splash, leaves a 2.2 m burning patch for 3 s at 34 dps), VOLLEY (three arrows at three different targets every 0.85 s at 80% damage, and it targets hidden enemies).
  - Control: FROST SPIRE (pulse freezes soldiers for 1.2 s, slows champions to 35% for 1.6 s, ×1.1 range), STORM BELL (×2.6 damage, 0.55 s stun on everyone, hard knockback, 2.8 s cooldown), GRAVITY WELL (pulls enemies toward the tower about 1.5 m per pulse, champions half as much, slows to 35%, ×1.25 range, 1.8 s cooldown).
  - Isolated test (6 mixed soldiers, 6 s): a level-2 damage tower dealt 290 damage, Ballista and Firepots killed all six, Volley killed five. Control: level 2 = 708 damage, Storm 940, Well 724 + pull, Frost 708 + 1.2 s freezes.
  - Bots buy a random level 3 after 150 s when they have 420+ gold and a level-2 tower.
- Building levels are visible on every building: each type has its own level-2 build (Damage: wooden hoardings, iron bands, gold spikes, hanging banners; Control: four crystal pylons with arcs over the top, gold crown and collar, banners; Support: gold spire and hanging lanterns; Barracks: tall watchtower; Range: second target, awning, quiver, flag; Foundry: second chimney and crane; Outpost: palisade and stilted lookout). Each level-3 tower has its own top: Ballista (big siege crossbow, the crystal is removed), Firepots (brazier with burning coals, catapult arm, pots; flames and smoke at idle), Volley (four corner turrets with red roofs, quivers), Frost Spire (ice spire crown, ice ring and base shards; snow motes), Storm Bell (bronze bell in an iron frame, coiled lightning rod, four rods; sparks), Gravity Well (dark orb with tilted crystal rings, floating stones, runestones; motes drifting in). Models in `tools/blender/build_structures.py` (`LEVEL2`, `LEVEL3`), nodes `level2_<type>` / `level3_<spec>`.
- Level-3 effects (`src/render/towerFx.ts`): 3D ballista bolts and volley arrows that face their flight, a spinning clay firepot with a fire trail and a fire burst on landing (lava patch from the zone), Frost = ice shards bursting from the ground plus a frost ring and mist, Storm = lightning from the bell to the ground around it plus thunder rings, Well = an inward-swirling vortex with the ring contracting and motes sucked in.
- Codex: TOWERS has a LEVEL 3 page and one live demo page per level-3 tower (the tower at the demo spot fighting waves of soldiers).
- Barracks model fix: the two team-paint panels sticking out of the roof (mis-rotated trim since the first build) are gone; the roof now has proper triangular gable ends in house colour front and back, plus a wooden ridge cap.
- X at a level-2 Damage/Control tower now opens the LEVEL 3 picker too (the pad hint says X to upgrade, but only Y opened it, so X fell through to MAX LEVEL / "PICK A LEVEL 3"). Releasing X or Y there without picking does nothing.
- Ballista aims: the tower stores its aim angle (`StructureState.aim`), and when a new target is more than ~17° off it turns first and fires 0.3 s later. The crossbow on top (the `level3_ballista` node) swings smoothly to that angle on its own; the tower body stays put.
- Prompt icon size fix (the real one): the distance scaling ran after the sprite batcher had already copied the icon sizes, so it never reached the screen and icons shrank with zoom. The scaling now runs before the batch fill. The icon's side offset (shop icon beside the keep, upgrade icon beside a building) is now fixed in world units, so the icon stays next to its building while staying the same size on screen. Checked with screenshots at zoom steps 0, 4 and 9.
- Hidden enemy info: a team's notices ("NEED 120", "BUILDING..."), army order cross, build/shop/level-up menus, cannon-aim text and Herald-morph meter are only drawn if that team has a player on this machine. With one human (vs CPUs, or online) you no longer see the other houses' orders or gold complaints; local same-screen versus still shows each side's own.
- Bellwick Gardens court gates fixed: each pair of court gates opened straight into a hedge flower bed, so they blocked nothing. That bed is trimmed back and a 5-wide gravel path now runs from each pair of court gates out to the side corridor. With the court gates shut the only way into the middle is the diagonal avenues (a 30 m detour from the side corridor); when the bells swap them open the side corridor walks straight in. One topiary that sat on the new path was removed.
- Bellwick Gardens court gates, take two (replaces the side-path change): hedges are back to the original layout, and the old side gaps in the court hedge are now solid hedge. The set-b court gates sit diagonally across each of the four avenue mouths, hedge to hedge, so when they're up the court is completely sealed (pathfinding confirms no route in from anywhere); when the bells swap them down, all four avenues open. Gate slots can now list their own cells and a fence line (`cells`, `line`) so a gate can run diagonally; the fence model follows the line. The base jump pads now land on the avenue just outside the court gates (40.5, 40.5 and its rotations) instead of inside the court; the court-to-base pads are unchanged so anyone inside can still get out.
- Respawn time scales with mode: the HERO RETURNS rule is the 1v1 time; 2v2 and FFA multiply it by `economy.respawnBigMul` (1.667), so 3/6/10/15 s become 5/10/16.7/25 s. Catch-up's respawn cut still applies on top. The rule blurb says so.
- Mercy's faster respawn (catchUp.respawnCut) now only applies in FFA; 1v1 and 2v2 respawn at the full time even when behind. Mercy's other boosts (income, production, fortify, bounty cut) are unchanged in every mode. Codex dying tip updated.
- Removed two dead hero hooks that nothing read: Grim's awayProductionMul/awayRadius and Remnil's productionMul.
- Single-view minimap: 62% of its old size fully zoomed in, shrinking smoothly to 45% fully zoomed out (log scale over the zoom range), drawn at 36% opacity instead of 50%. Split-screen centre minimap unchanged.
- Pathing fixes for hedges and gates (measured with a stuck counter: something told to move that went nowhere for a second, 4 CPUs × 300 s × 2 seeds):
  - The pathfinder now reports whether the goal was actually reached (`nav.lastFound`, `nav.reachable`). When a goal can't be reached (e.g. the Grudge in Bellwick's sealed court), CPU heroes and soldiers walk to the end of the partial route and wait there instead of pushing straight at the goal into the bars. CPU heroes only take a jump pad if they can reach it and reach their goal from where it lands.
  - CPU hero routes are smoothed with their body width (`nav.wideClear`), so they stop clipping hedge corners.
  - Every soldier (not just big ones) now sidesteps briefly when it hasn't made progress for 1.2 s; two soldiers meeting head-on in a one-cell lane along a hedge used to push each other back forever.
  - Result: Bellwick hero stuck-seconds 27 → 4, soldier 356-630 → 43; Crossing hero 6 → 2, soldier 184-235 → 28. Warlord v Warden Crossing sim 20/80 → 35/65 (Brutes stop getting pinned).
- CPUs replan around unreachable goals: the pathfinder keeps a connected-area map (flood fill over walkable cells, rebuilt only when walls, gates or buildings change) so "can I walk there?" is a cheap lookup (`nav.reachable`). Every CPU decision that sets a destination (enemy hero to chase, the Grudge, enemy shrine, lantern, horn, base threat, build pads, front targets, fight targets, retreat towers) skips options it can't reach and falls through to its next priority. A CPU walled off from its goal (e.g. caught inside Bellwick's court when the gates rise) uses any jump pad it can reach whose landing can reach the goal. Waiting at an unreachable goal on Bellwick: 25 s → 5 s per 2×300 s run.
- Shoals: each team's islet pad sat right in the one-cell mouth of its causeway (red's at the south causeway, blue's mirrored at the north), so a building there left about half a metre of gap and everything that tried the causeway got stuck. The pad moved 4 cells inland (blue 34.5,8.5 → 38.5,8.5; red mirrored 33.5,43.5 → 29.5,43.5); the causeway is now a straight 25 m crossing with towers on every pad. The generator also gained the ogre patrol and sea surround that had only been added to the JSON by hand.
- Buildings on pads now block pathfinding cells within their radius + 0.45 (body clearance), not just their radius, so routes never squeeze through gaps nothing can physically fit through. Checked on every map with a building on every pad: no route or pad became unreachable and no extra area was cut off.
- Shoals causeways now reach the islets: the stone bridge stopped one cell short (last tile x=31, islet ground from x=33) and the channel's sloped edge dipped the cell between to the waterline, so the bridge visibly ended in water on both causeways. The bridge is one cell longer (w 17 → 18) and lands flush on the islet.
- Outpost waves now queue outposts farthest from their own keep first, so forward outposts get first claim on gold and the population cap; home ones are the first to idle.
- Random cannon barrage targeting: each targeted shot first picks a house at random (houses not yet hit this barrage first, so a barrage spreads across houses), then a random hero or soldier of that house standing outside every base (FFA keep guards excluded). Shots whose blast circle would touch any base are re-rolled (up to 10 tries) instead of landing; the one random-spot shot obeys the same rule. Measured over 2 Frostcross FFA + 2 Crossing games: barrage shots in a base 30/131 → 0/180; Frostcross shots per house 20/5/19/29 → 14/21/23/19. Bought (shop) cannons are unchanged and can still be aimed anywhere.
- Second currency: GRAIN (sack icon, shown after gold in the HUD header with its per-second rate; also in the pause/results stats).
  - Grain pays for soldiers (wave spawn costs, unchanged numbers: grunt 12, archer 16, brute 32) and for tower fire: each shot/pulse costs grain (Damage 1, Control 2; level 3: Ballista 4, Firepots 3, Volley 2, Frost 3, Storm 4, Well 3). A tower that can't pay fires at 1/2.5 speed ("NO GRAIN · TOWERS SLOW"). Hero-made turrets/ballistas and Support healing are free.
  - Grain income: keep 1.4/s + each finished tower or outpost 0.6 / 1.0 / 1.4 per second at level 1 / 2 / 3. Start 60. Surplus sink: above 150 in the sack, each outpost trains one extra soldier per wave (forward outposts first, pop cap still applies).
  - Gold pays for buildings, upgrades, level 3, the shop and the Herald's change back. Income: keep trickle 1.0/s (was 3.5 + 0.35-0.45 per pad), bounties hero 80 (was 60), building 60 (was 50), soldiers unchanged (3/4/7 + rank).
  - GOLD RATE rule scales both incomes; Mercy (unchanged constants) now counts grain as wealth when deciding who's behind and boosts both incomes (before this, a grain-rich house could read as "fully behind" and get 2.2× grain and 80% cheaper troops).
  - Tuning runs (18 CPU 1v1s each: 6 hero pairings × Crossing/Ruins/Shoals): old economy — match 310 s, buildings per team at 2/4/6 min 4.1/3.6/2.4, army 5.7/5.4/2.6, 203 soldiers per game, keep damage 3086, lead@90 converts 56%. Final grain economy — 355 s, buildings 4.2/4.1/2.8, army 4.9/8.2/3.1, 251 soldiers, keep damage 2738, lead@90 67%, idle-outpost notices 4.8/game (was 4.3). Richer grain (base 1.8) piled up 570 by the end and made games one-sided (14/4 wins); leaner grain (base 0.6) stalled games to time with half the keep damage.
- Faster outposts: waves every 12 s (was 16), FFA every 9 s (was 12). Grain income raised to match (keep 1.6/s, buildings 0.7 / 1.1 / 1.5 per level). 18-game 1v1 CPU run: soldiers per game 251 → 320, army at 2/4/6 min 8.2/8.4/7.5, grain banked at 6 min 286, keep damage 2278, wins 9/9. (12 s with the old grain rates starved: idle outposts 13/game, keep damage 1803.)
- Lanes in 1v1/2v2: maps can list named lane waypoints (`lanes` in the map JSON; Crossing TOP/MIDDLE/BOTTOM, Ruins MIDDLE/BOTTOM, Shoals TOP/MIDDLE/BOTTOM via the generator). Flicking ATTACK again while already attacking cycles TOP → MIDDLE → BOTTOM → ANY (like FFA's house cycling). Attacking soldiers walk to the lane's waypoint first, then on to the keep; soldiers already past it go straight on, and each new lane order resets that. The Attack badges show a small lane marker with the chosen lane lit. Measured on all three maps: every soldier crosses the middle in the chosen lane.
- Ruins top lane: each base's north/west wall now has a 3-cell exit (blue z=29 x5-7, red mirrored x=29 z5-7), opening a TOP lane through the top-left corner (lane waypoint 4.5,4.5; lanes are TOP/MIDDLE/BOTTOM). Neutral pads added in the two lane corners (8.5,8.5 top-left and 39.5,39.5 bottom-right). Verified soldiers ordered TOP leave by the new exit and cross the diagonal at the corner; nothing on the map is cut off.
- Outposts spawn on their own clocks (replaces the shared wave tick): each production building sends one soldier every `cadence` seconds from when it finishes building — Barracks 4.5 s, Range 5.5 s, Foundry 9 s, mixed Outpost 5.5 s; level 2 ×0.65. Multipliers: TROOP OUTPUT rule (`waves.rateMul`), FFA ×1.3 rate (`ffa.spawnRateMul`, replaces the +1-per-wave outpost bonus), enshrined outpost ×(1 + outpostExtra), sudden death ×productionMul, and ×0.7 interval while grain ≥ 150 (`grain.surplusMul`, replaces the extra-soldier surplus). Each soldier remembers its outpost; when it dies that outpost's next spawn slips 0.8 s (capped at 2 intervals ahead) so losses aren't instantly refilled. At pop cap or short of grain an outpost retries every 0.5 s. The shared tick now only drives FFA keep guards.
  - 18-game 1v1 CPU run vs the 12 s waves: soldiers per game 355 → 491, army at 2/4/6 min 8.1/10.1/9.7, grain banked at 2/4/6 min 178/219/298 → 84/131/199.
- Hero down halts production: while any champion of a house is dead (one is enough in 2v2), none of its outposts spawn ("HERO DOWN · OUTPOSTS HALT"); on respawn every outpost restarts its clock from zero, so the first soldier comes a full interval later. 18-game CPU 1v1: soldiers per game 491 → 390, matches end sooner (403 → 350 s), grain banked at 6 min ~146.
- Lane orders stick: while attacking soldiers are still heading to a chosen lane's waypoint they only engage enemies within their attack range + 1.5 m and drop targets that drift further, instead of chasing fights in other lanes. Once past the waypoint (or on ANY lane / other orders) they behave as before. Test with enemies parked in Crossing's middle: soldiers sent BOTTOM previously crossed at z 21-28 for 5 of 14; now all 14 cross in the bottom lane.
- Grim nerf (early dives): the stealth ambush bonus no longer stacks with the generic "attacker hidden" bonus (only the smoke one applies while smoked; was ×2.0 × ×1.3); smoke ambush ×2.0 → ×1.6, cooldown 14 → 16 s; flank ×1.5 → ×1.3; Leap 70 → 60 damage, cooldown 5 → 6 s; combo hits 24/24/24/40 → 21/21/21/36; execute dash ×1.4 → ×1.25 below 40%. A champion kill now halves Leap's remaining cooldown instead of resetting it (buildings still reset it). Scripted opener (smoke, leap from behind, combo) on a level-1 800 HP hero: median time to kill 2.0 s → 3.7 s (fastest 1.3 → 3.0).
- Training Ground (main menu, second item): goes to champion select in 1v1 with a TRAINING ribbon; P2's card reads DUMMY and whichever champion it holds becomes the dummy (a second player can sit there and move it). In the match: the dummy and the enemy keep can't drop below 1 HP, and the dummy regenerates to full 3 s after the last hit; your house has unlimited gold and grain; no clock, no Grudge, no cannon barrage, no ogre; nothing is recorded. The clock is replaced by a DPS panel counting only damage your side deals to the dummy: DPS over the last 5 s, the current combo (total and duration, resets after 2.5 s without a hit), biggest hit, total and hit count. Pause menu in training: Resume, Level Up (one level, pick the evolution as usual), Reset Cooldowns (also fills the super), Reset Meter, Change Champion (back to select), Quit Training.
- Grim's nerf reverted (original numbers, double ambush and Leap reset back). New passive for solo play, LONE WOLF (`hooks.loneRadius/loneLeech/loneArmor`): with no friendly champion within 14 m Grim heals for 30% of the damage he deals to champions and takes 30% less damage from soldiers and towers. Always on in 1v1, off when he dives alongside a partner in 2v2. CPU 1v1 Grim 21-27% → 34-39%, K/D 2/6 → 4.3/4.6; 2v2 Grim pairs 30-50%.
- CPU overhaul, measured with `tools/roundrobin.ts` (every hero vs every hero, Crossing/Ruins/Shoals, both sides; parallel runner kept in /tmp during tuning):
  - All heroes: once below their retreat health (default 30%) the CPU goes home and stays out until 80% (recalls when safe), except to finish an enemy hero who is nearly dead and weaker than it; escaping heroes path home instead of loitering half-dead. Evolution choices: each hero follows a tested best path (`botPlan.picks`, found by trying all 8 paths for levels 2-4 against the field; level 5 never mattered): Warlord 1-0-1, Stig 0-0-1, Grim 0-0-0, Remnil 0-0-0, Francois 0-0-1, Thorn 1-1-0. Otherwise CPUs prefer synergy picks.
  - Per-hero plans (`botPlan` in heroes.json; tested knobs: retreatHp, crowd, opener, openerRange, flank, escape, gateB, hitAndRun, hunt, picks): Grim smokes before engaging (from ≤15 m), approaches from behind, won't fight a hero standing in more than one enemy soldier beyond his own, only leaps in when smoked/target hurt/target alone, fights in 4.5 s windows after an opener then backs off until smoke is ready, hunts an isolated enemy hero within 45 m when healthy, retreats at 45% and leaps toward home to escape. Sweeps of retreat health and crowd-avoidance for the other heroes were within noise of the defaults, so they use the defaults.
  - Round-robin, 180 games per hero: before Warlord 58 / Stig 41 / Grim 27 / Remnil 51 / Francois 51 / Thorn 71 %; after 48 / 38 / 39 / 53 / 57 / 65 % (spread 44 → 27 points). Deaths per match fell for every hero (Grim 10.4 → 4.6).
- Thorn nerf attempts reverted: slap cooldown 4.5 → 5.5 s + finisher 80 → 70 (CPU 1v1 70%), and structure ×1.2 → ×1.1 + Control tower ×1.5 → ×1.3 (71%), were both within noise of his 65%; his edge is elsewhere and still to be found.
- Execution combos (`combo()` in heroes.ts, codex page "COMBO · …" on each hero after the evolutions):
  - Warlord CRACK CHARGE: with Earthsplitter, B within 0.9 s of the finisher's rock wave charges 11 m along the crack (normal charge is 5) then slams; uses B's cooldown.
  - Thorn ARM SHOVE: L+A while the Long Arm Slap is still out shoves everything along the arm (1.6 m either side) for 35, knockback 45, stun 0.5 s (about 6 m of push); uses the shove cooldown.
  - Stig RAMP JUMP: dodge while standing on his siege ramp launches a 0.9 s jump up to 11 m in the stick direction, over walls (lands on the nearest walkable cell); uses the dodge cooldown.
  - Remnil HEX SWAP: dodge while an enemy he hexed is within 10 m swaps places with it (nearest, champions preferred); the enemy is stunned 0.4 s.
  - Francois RIPOSTE WHIRL: A within 0.7 s of a successful parry spins 360°, 3.4 m, 65 damage, big knockback.
  - Herald BANNER VAULT: dodge toward his own planted banner (3-13 m away, within ~50°) jumps to it.
  - New general `world.startJump` for non-pad jumps (pad index -1).
- Stig's siege ramp reworked: 3×3 platform (was 2×2), 2.6 high (was 1.5), 2 cells wide approach (was 0.5), so it's easy to walk up and stay on, and high enough to jump walls from. Tesla Tower no longer replaces the ramp: the ramp is still built and a tesla coil (400 HP, 45 chain lightning, 8 m) stands on its top for as long as the ramp lasts.
- Thorn nerf: Brambles (Z) 35 → 30 damage per second, 6 → 5 s. CPU 1v1 round-robin, 300 games: 68% → 61% (25 dps gave 60%). Health, wall cooldown, slap, speed and the earlier slap/passive nerfs were all within noise.
- Fix: two Francois parrying each other at the same moment countered forever (stack overflow crash in 2v2). A parry now ends past its window so a counter can't be parried back. Found by `tools/roundrobin2v2.ts` (every 2-hero team vs every other, 3 maps, both sides, 1260 games): Francois 70% / Remnil 63% / Thorn 62% / Stig 37% / Grim 36% / Warlord 33% in 2v2; best team Francois+Francois 88%, worst Warlord+Warlord 8%.
- 2v2 balance pass (tools/roundrobin2v2.ts, 1260 games; per-hero detail showed Francois far ahead on damage to champions and Warlord/Stig dying most):
  - Francois: each combo swing, lunge and flurry beat hits at most 2 soldiers (`hooks.maxHits`, champions always hit); parry counter 110 → 85.
  - Warlord: MARCH (+35% move speed after 2.5 s out of combat and not hit, `marchSpeed/marchAfter`); UNBREAKABLE (with 2+ enemy champions within 8 m: takes 40% less from champions and deals 30% more to them, `outnumberedArmor/outnumberedDamage`); +15% damage to champions.
  - Stig: damage tier mid → high; OVERHAUL (Repair also heals nearby soldiers 120 and his partner 60, and gives soldiers +25% damage for 6 s); the CPU now uses Repair whenever 3+ of its soldiers are near a fight.
  - Fix: Stig's tesla coil on the ramp uses a fixed 8 m range.
  - Result 2v2 (before → after): Francois 70 → 58, Remnil 63 → 65, Thorn 62 → 55, Stig 37 → 48, Warlord 33 → 45, Grim 36 → 30 (left alone for human testing). Teams 8-88% → 15-76%. 1v1 round-robin after: Warlord 49, Stig 58, Grim 35, Remnil 56, Francois 47, Thorn 52.
- Hero model pipeline, AI-assisted (first used for Wren, `marksman`): turntable sheet of the procedural model → Nano Banana Pro redesigns it as a 4-view sheet → the cleaned front view goes to Tripo P1 (`tools/gen-hero-sheet.mjs`, ~$0.65 a hero) → `tools/blender/build_tripo_hero.py` makes it a Grudge hero. That script scales the mesh, places the shared humanoid bones on joint landmarks in the A-pose, auto-weights a welded copy and transfers the weights, assigns any part the heat solver missed to the nearest bone, makes listed parts rigid (Pip and the arrow fletchings → chest), swings the arms down and applies that as the rest pose (roll 0, so every clip in anims.py plays unchanged), recolours the team-hue texels to grey and puts those faces in a `team_*` material so they take the dye, drops the texture to 1024 JPEG, adds procedural gear (bow in hand_L, arrow shafts) and exports. The source mesh is kept in `assets/source/<hero>_tripo.glb`. Wren: 15.3k triangles.
- Meshes with a texture wider than 256 px are left out of the part merger (it squashes every part into a 256 px texture array), so AI-textured heroes keep their full texture at the cost of 2 extra draw calls.
- Stig (`engineer`) rebuilt with the same pipeline: his old turntable plus Wren's finished sheet as the style reference went to Nano Banana Pro (the sheet step now takes extra reference images), then Tripo P1. The backpack and chimney are rigid on the chest; the wrench is still procedural in hand_R. Team colour now goes only to texels inside the team faces' UVs (faces of the team hue inside an optional `team_box`), so the blue pauldrons take the dye. 15.5k triangles.
- Stig's props from the same AI pipeline (one Nano Banana sheet in his style, then one Tripo P1 run each): the wrench is a Tripo mesh fixed to hand_R (Tripo dropped the brass shaft between head and grip, so the build bridges it with a brass tube textured from the grip's brass); the ballista and tesla coil are `assets/props/*.glb` from `tools/blender/build_tripo_props.py`. The ballista is split at its turntable into a fixed base and the `yaw` > `tilt` turret, with `tip_L`/`tip_R`/`nut` markers the game strings at runtime; its pennant takes the team dye. The bolt is part of the mesh now, so it no longer hides between shots. `src/render/props.ts` preloads props at boot; `ballistaMesh` and `teslaCoil` fall back to the old procedural models if a prop is missing.
- Stig's ramp wrench throw uses his real wrench: the thrown boomerang is the Tripo wrench (`assets/props/wrench.glb`, same mesh and brass bridge as the held one, shown 1.3× while flying), and the wrench in his hand (every node named `<hero>_wrench`, outline hulls included) is hidden while any of his boomerangs is out, so it reappears when it comes back.
- Stig holds his wrench forward and up from his fist (like a hammer at the ready) instead of over his shoulder, and has his own second combo swing (`engineer_clips` in build_tripo_hero.py overrides `attack_b`): wind-up with the wrench cocked behind his right shoulder, then the forearm untwists and the wrist turns so the wrench sweeps flat across his front and follows through to his left. Heroes built by build_tripo_hero.py can override any clip this way; `CLIP=<names> FRAMES=… ELEV=…` renders posed frames from Blender for tuning.
- Hero ability props from the AI pipeline (one Nano Banana sheet, one Tripo call each, built as static props by `build_tripo_props.py`, used through `propParts()` with the old procedural shapes as fallback): Warlord's quake and rock-wave spikes are a layered rock spire (instanced, same rise/sink animation); Thorn's stone wall cells are mossy standing stones (random quarter turns, slight height jitter); Stig's palisade cells are a three-stake section turned to follow the wall line; Remnil's hex now erupts a horned-skull hex idol from the ground where it lands (rises in 0.13 s with a wobble, sinks after 1.3 s, larger for bigger hexes); his bones zone grows a few tombstones. Wall merging and fx cleanup keep shared prop geometry (`userData.model`).
- Rest of the roster through the AI pipeline (one subagent each, reviewed): Warlord (green ogre warlord, spiked club in hand_R, pauldrons rigid on chest, cast/block wrist override), Grim (hooded goblin, a knife in each hand, cape rigid on hips, gentler attack_a/c raises), Remnil (purple-hooded necromancer, crystal staff, hand-made skin so the robe follows the hips, damped leg swing), Francois (cavalier, stretched rapier, baguette, his own thrust/lunge/spin attacks, hat rigid on head), Thorn (treant with round shield on the left forearm), Herald (plate knight, sword in hand_R, banner strapped to his back). Ogre unit rebuilt on the 8-bone unit rig (`tools/blender/build_tripo_unit.py`, club on arm_R, own death pose). Damage/Control/Support towers rebuilt from Tripo bodies (`tools/blender/build_tripo_structures.py`); level-2/3 parts stay procedural, refitted to the new shapes, every named node kept; the L3 ballista uses the turret from Stig's ballista prop. Hero GLBs 1.9-2.2 MB each, towers 0.4-0.9 MB.
- Thorn's long-arm slap stretches his real right arm: on the reach event the renderer aims arm_R and forearm_R at the slap target and scales the forearm along its length to reach it (hand counter-scaled), with the old out/hold/retract timing (0.06/0.24/0.5 s); the impact, streak and leaf effects stay, the spawned bark arm is only a fallback for models without those bones.
- Thorn's texture warmed: the bake can grade low-saturation texels toward a tint (`warm` in the hero config); Thorn's bark and shield go from mauve-grey to warm brown.
- Francois carries the baguette strapped diagonally across his back instead of in his left hand.
- Stig's portrait icon hides his wrench so it doesn't cover his face (in-match wrench unchanged); icons now frame every hero's head the same way.
- New painted plank texture (`assets/textures/planks.png`, Nano Banana, tileable, 128 px) for Stig's siege ramp and works bridges (one material, no more checkerboard of light/dark planks); map bridges are switched over in the map build.
- Production outposts (barracks, range, foundry, outpost) and the keep rebuilt from Tripo bodies (`tools/blender/build_tripo_outposts.py`): same footprint (plinth radius 1.55) and heights, team texels dyed, flags/foundry gear (`spin_*`) kept procedural on new masts, new procedural level-2 trims fitted to the new bodies, keep crystal procedural on a gold cradle (Tripo fused it into the claws). 5.5-9.5k tris, 0.47-0.69 MB each.
- Arena rocks and trees from the AI pipeline (one Nano Banana sheet, 8 Tripo variants: three rocks, round oak, tall leafy tree, pine, snowy pine, dead tree), placed exactly where the old ones were (same footprints, random yaw, ±6-8% scale, seated into the ground); in-arena rubble uses the low-poly rocks; frostcross uses snowy pines, ruins dead trees; scenery outside the arena unchanged. Each variant has one 128 px texture; the map merger now takes textures up to 128 px (upscaled smoothly, was 64), so props stay one draw call. Draw calls unchanged on every map; map GLBs +8.5 MB total. Map bridges use the painted planks texture with planks across the walking direction.
- Soldiers (grunt, ranged, heavy) rebuilt from Tripo bodies plus Tripo weapons on the 8-bone unit rig (`build_tripo_unit.py`), 2.7-3.0k tris each, 256 px textures so they stay in the merged/batched unit path (draw calls unchanged in busy 1v1/2v2 split-screen matches). Team colour for these is per-texel: materials named `dye*` have their blue texels greyed and flagged, and the merged shader dyes flagged texels (per-face recolouring left stripes at this tri count).
- Ogre is a brighter, more saturated green: the bake can push a hue range toward a target hue and boost saturation/value (`vivid` in a model's config); the ogre pulls greens toward 100° with ×1.7 saturation, ×1.15 value.

## Tall grass hides you (brush)
- Tall grass already hid units from range and towers; it is now proper brush. A hero or soldier standing in tall grass (or river mist) is hidden from the enemy until it attacks or casts (any A/B/R/Z firing counts, not only landing damage) and for 1.5 s after. While hidden, a team only sees it if one of its heroes or soldiers is within 2.2 m, stands in the same grass patch (8-connected tall-grass cells, `World.grassPatchAt`), or the hidden unit carries that team's mark. That sight is shared by the whole team (`Status.seenBy`, one bit per team), so towers shoot a hidden hero their soldier is standing next to. Mist has no patches: inside it only the 2.2 m rule applies.
- Grim's smoke is unchanged and stronger than grass: while smoked, patches, marks and team sight don't count, only each viewer's own 2.2 m. When the smoke ends in grass he stays hidden by the grass rules.
- One rule everywhere: soldiers, ogre and towers already used `canSee`/hidden; heroes' aim assist and chain lightning now skip units they can't see, tower pierce skips them, the volley tower still sees everything. Area hits and skillshots still hit what's hidden (you can face-check). The grass ambush bonus (×1.3) no longer applies when the victim is in the same patch.
- Rendering follows Grim's smoke exactly: a view drawn for one team hides enemy heroes and soldiers that team can't see; allies see their hidden heroes ghosted (soldiers stay normal, they're batched). When two teams share one screen the views stay split while any hero is hidden from a team on that screen. Hidden units leave no footstep dust; with a single team on screen, its hidden heroes kick up a little green rustle instead. Minimap shows a hidden unit only once every other team sees it.
- CPUs: they no longer pick fights with, hunt or chase heroes they can't see (they used to only skip hidden heroes more than 2.5 m away, and could still pick one through the "nearby" fallback). Retreating at low health with the enemy hero 3-10 m away, a CPU ducks into the nearest grass within 5 m that is not the enemy's patch and not toward the enemy, and holds still there up to 3 s while unseen, then carries on home (6 s before it tries again).
- Measured with `tools/roundrobin.ts`-style runs (6 seeds, 180 games per hero; before → after): Warlord 49 → 56%, Engineer 58 → 52%, Grim 35 → 33%, Remnil 56 → 55%, Francois 47 → 44%, Thorn 52 → 59%. No crashes; two runs of the new code matched exactly.
- Level-2 add-ons and level-3 tower specs are painted Tripo props (12 meshes, 1-2.4k tris each, 256 px), same named nodes: damage L2 wall banners with shields, firepot coal cauldron (rim ~4.0), volley four tiled corner turrets, ballista turret on a painted turntable; control L2 crystal pylons, frost ice cluster (+4 small), storm brass-and-copper rod (bolts start at its tip, 5.0), well void orb in rune stones (pulse orb raised to 3.15); support L2 gilded bell spire; barracks training dummies + banner, range arrow cart, foundry crane with crucible, outpost lookout tower. Team-coloured where they carry cloth or trim.
- Wading: characters whose feet are below the drawn water surface (waterLevel + tide rise) are lowered visually (sim unchanged) until submerged by a fixed depth (heroes/ogre 0.6, heavy 0.42, other units 0.34), easing in near banks and skipped while airborne; the water mesh then covers their lower legs. One instanced foam ring at the waterline per wading character, instanced ripple rings while moving (heroes every 0.2 s, units 0.45 s, slow when idle, none while stealthed), splashes instead of footstep dust; silhouettes skip fragments below the water surface; player rings drawn at the surface after the water. +≤2 draw calls per viewport.
- Hero FX repaint (2 Nano Banana sheets): Warlord's quake fissures are flat painted lava cracks with jagged tapering edges lying on the ground (`assets/fx/lava.png`, replaces the flat orange boxes; firepot lava zones use the same builder); the quake throws painted magma splashes instead of glow blobs; `lavaGlow` is a cluster of small painted flames; embers and helmet repainted without halos. Herald's whole atlas repainted in the painted style (was pixel-art with thick outlines; horn-blast ring opacity 0.55); Stig's heal sprite is a rounded green plus with a brass rim; Francois's glint/sparkle and the shared twinkle/rock/pebbles repainted. Prompts and the cutter for these sheets are in `tools/fx-prompts/` (tools/gen-fx-sheets.mjs still has the old prompts; don't rerun it for herald/warlord).
- Map art repaint (2 Nano Banana sheets, 1 Tripo): painted tileable ground/wall textures (grass, dirt, gravel, cobble, cliff, brick, wallblock, new ruinstone and hedge at 128 px; sand, snow 64 px) made by `tools/phototex.py --painted` from `assets/source/map_textures_ai.png`; brick was blurry from phototex blur + 64→128 upscale + no anisotropy: now native 128 px and anisotropy 4 on merged props and terrain. Planks remade without nails/knots (no shimmering dark squares) and bridge vertex shading smoothed across plank faces (no per-tile bands). Gardens hedges are lumpy rounded painted hedge cells (same cells/collision; 16k → 62k tris on that map). Tall grass clumps are crossed alpha cards from three painted tufts. Map pads are a painted Tripo pad (~520 tris) with team-tinted rim/inlay, merged into the props mesh. Draw calls flat on every map.
- Thorn's bark is a saturated brown: on top of the warm grade, browns (hue 5-40°) are pulled toward 26° with ×1.9 saturation, ×1.12 value (body and shield); leaves untouched.
- Frostcross chasms: the corner cirques under the narrow causeways are deadly. Maps can set `chasm` (frostcross −2.2, the cirque floors are at −4, every path stays above −1.5); any hero or unit standing on ground below it (not mid-jump) dies instantly, credited to whoever last damaged it within 6 s (new `status.hurtBy/hurtAt`), else no one (opponent in 2-team modes, as other unowned kills). Nav treats ground below chasm+0.5 as closed, so soldiers and CPU paths never go down there; you get there by walking off the edge or being knocked off. 3 CPU FFA matches of 400 s: 0-1 chasm deaths each, all heroes. The floors are filled with instanced painted ice spike clusters and broken ice slabs (Tripo, 500 tris each, ~20%/16% of floor cells, one instanced mesh per type per quadrant); a chasm death throws an ice-and-snow burst.
- Ruins walls weathered (2 Nano Banana, 3 Tripo): seamless step no longer duplicates tile content (fills seams from outside the tile); three cracked wall stones, mossy lower stone, mossy broken wall-top, rubble and ivy textures; per-section stone tints, jagged moss line at the base, grime streaks, broken tilted battlements, low collapsed stretches (≥1 m), hanging ivy, rubble heaps at wall bases (collision/nav unchanged). Gardens hedges use three colour-matched hedge textures switched in patches, laid at an angle/offset, with large-scale light and hue variation. Gardens topiary: Tripo spiral, ball topiary (Tripo pot/trunk with smooth painted balls), Tripo flower bushes in five flower colours for beds and urns.
- Frostcross ice now also fills the pits themselves (the rim-wall cells of the cirques, which are drawn as deep floor), with sparser, 1.35× larger pieces using 180-tri versions of the ice models (`iceshard_lo`/`icechunk_lo`, 128 px) at 10%/8% of cells.
- Loading splash regenerated for the remade Tripo heroes (1 usable Nano Banana call): the board is in-game `actionShot` renders of all seven heroes, front and three-quarter views, and `tools/gen-loading.mjs <board.png[,more.png]> art` now takes several reference images. Passing the old splash as a composition reference made the model return the old art unchanged, so the composition is described in the prompt only. Same framing: empty sky in the top third for the logo, bottom-right left clear for NOW LOADING. Logo unchanged. Wren is left out until she joins the roster.
- Discrete amounts shown to players are integers; only per-second rates (`+1.6/S`) keep a decimal. Damage/heal floaters keep a float accumulator for merged hits but draw `Math.round` (min 1); the hero-death/tower-loss gold notice rounds its amount. The random "999999999" was a merged damage floater: hits carry fractional damage, `String(58.300000000000004)` overflowed the 128 px text-atlas slot and the centred crop showed only the run of 9s/0s.
- FFA pause cards show house stats as a two-column label/value grid (KEEP, GOLD / GRAIN, ARMY / KILLS) instead of one dotted line: text never renders below 0.64 scale, so shrinking the line to fit can't work. Menu and end-screen house colours follow `teamColors` (team 2 yellow, team 3 green); they were swapped.
- Ruins floor weathered (no fal): `tools/phototex.py --paving` derives a per-stone id map (`cobble_id.png`) and a cracked variant (`cobble_crack.png`) from cobble; the terrain shader, opt-in per map (ruins sets a marker in its GLB), gives every stone a random value that also changes per tile (no visible 4 m repeat) driving tint, cracks and missing stones (recessed soil), plus moss in gaps and over stones, bare dirt patches, puddle stains, grime near walls and large-scale colour shifts. ~87 loose slabs, 85 pebbles and 20 short grass tufts merged into the existing props/grass meshes. Draw calls unchanged, +0.6% tris in 4-player split.
- Grim's A combo reads now: his hits are 0.16 s (hit at 0.03 s, finisher 0.26 s), so clips played 3.6-4.7× fast and the 0.1 s crossfade ate them. New raider clips: attack_a/b mirrored horizontal knife slashes with torso twist and lunge, chained wind-up to wind-up; attack_c a coiled hop into a full spin with both arms out, landing crossed. Playback: combos longer than three hits alternate a/b and the last hit plays attack_c (Grim: a, b, a, c); melee combo clips play at most 2× with a 0.04 s fade when they'd be faster (no other hero exceeds 1.96×); a standing hero's attack plays out its follow-through instead of cutting to idle. Sim timing unchanged.
- Look direction update: the game now reads as hi-res early-2000s PC rather than strict N64, so the boot splash is no longer crushed to 480×270 with 15-bit dither: `public/loading/art.jpg` is 1920×1072 (q88), the logo is 360 px tall with soft alpha (no 32-colour quantize), NOW LOADING 144 px. In-game the logo draws on the hi-res UI layer (`hiImage`) instead of the low-res UI canvas.
- Shoals tidal flats dressed with painted beach debris (1 Nano Banana sheet cut into `assets/fx/beach.png`, 4×3 atlas of 128 px top-down sprites: scallop, conch, starfish, mussels, dead fish, fish skeleton, crab, kelp, driftwood, pebbles, sand dollar, rope with float), scattered on tide cells (~45% of cells, weighted kinds, random yaw/size) as one instanced flat-decal mesh in the map scene; covered by the water at high tide. Frostcross chasm ice moved into the map scene too, so map select shows it.
- Shoals flats read as a dried lakebed: painted cracked-mud texture (`assets/textures/lakebed.png`, Nano Banana, 256 px tileable) blended in by the terrain shader on tide cells (`aLake` weight from the tide-cell mask with a noise edge, weaker below the waterline; tiled every 8 m at half contrast and tinted brown so it blends), with a large-scale lightness variation from the same texture for salt crust. Beach debris thinned to ~9% of tide cells.

## Opening lockdown (anti-rush gates)
- Every match on every map and mode (1v1, 2v2, FFA) starts with every house's keep sealed: all of its entrances are shut by gates for `match.lockdown.seconds` (30 s in `data/match.json`, `warnSeconds` 5). Nobody gets in or out. Training skips it (`makeTraining` opens the gates silently): it's a solo sandbox against dummies, there's no rush to stop and waiting would only get in the way.
- Entrances are found from the map, not authored. `World.computeBase` already flood-fills each base inside the bounding box of its castle walls; the base cells that step straight out of that box are the wall gaps, grouped into one gate each (it now returns them as `gates` plus the `box`). Bellwick's keep walls sit 16 m from the keep, past the old 14 m search, so it used to fall back to a 20 m square; the search now retries at 18 m when nothing is found, so Bellwick gets its real walls and 3 entrances (east, south and the open corner toward the court, sealed by an L-shaped gate). That also gives Bellwick 3 DEFEND posts instead of 1. Gates per base: Crossing 3 (N/S bridges, E), Ruins 3 (corner, side, top exit), Shoals 3 (N, S, E), Frostcross 2 (its keeps only have two openings), Bellwick 3.
- Sim: the gate cells become `Kind.Wall` (style `lockgate`) and the nav grid is recomputed, so walking, pathing, soldiers, the Ogre (which only arrives at 110 s anyway) and projectiles are stopped exactly like by a castle wall. While locked, each keep's box is a zone (`MapEvents.zoneAt`): no move may change zone. Leap and blink targets, jump pads, `startJump` (Stig's ramp jump, Herald's banner vault) and Remnil's hex swap refuse a target in another zone (pads and jumps also show "THE GATES ARE SHUT · NS"). As a catch-all, an entity whose zone changes between ticks without a teleport is put back and its jump/leap is cancelled. Terrain mods (Thorn's walls, Stig's ramps and works) can't take gate cells, and the "every spawn stays reachable" check for Thorn's walls runs as if the gates were open.
- Opening: 5 s before, a `gates` event (`lock: true`) rings the bells (existing gate audio) and shows a "THE GATES OPEN IN N" countdown card; at 30 s the cells are restored, every soldier repaths, a `gates` shift event plays the existing clank and a "THE GATES OPEN" card and notice show. Under the match clock there's a "GATES OPEN IN N" line the whole time; on the minimap the gate cells are wood-coloured.
- Visuals (`MapFx.buildLockGates`, no new assets): iron-banded double doors of the painted planks texture with darker Z-braces, rivets, ring pulls and a row of iron spikes on top, between wallblock stone posts; one mesh per material for the whole map. In the last 5 s they rattle and shed dust; on opening they jolt and sink into the ground over ~2.5 s with dust, chips and camera shake, then hide. Fast-forwarded matches (`time=`) skip straight to the opened state.
- CPUs: they build as normal (barracks at 1 s, range at 10 s), then wait just inside the gate on the side they want to go, since unreachable goals already end at the partial path; they no longer pick jump pads whose landing is sealed. Soldiers mass at the gates and pour out at 30 s.
- Checked on all five maps (`/tmp/opencode/gates/navcheck.ts`): at 0 s and 15 s no spawn can reach any enemy spawn or the map centre, nobody leaves their zone while locked, at 60 s everything is reachable and the first hero leaves at 30.0 s. CPU 1v1 round-robin (12 seeds, 360 games per hero, before → after): Warlord 55 → 48, Stig 54 → 46, Grim 35 → 37, Remnil 55 → 58, Francois 41 → 49, Thorn 59 → 61. No crashes. Early deaths (before 90 s) fell for most heroes (Warlord 2.1 → 1.7 per game).
- Stig nerfs (no sims, by request): his own attacks and thrown wrench deal ×0.2 to buildings (`hooks.heroStructureMul`, only when the hero himself is the source; his ballista and tesla are unaffected); thrown wrench 95 → 38 damage. The siege ramp has 500 HP (`rampHp`): an invisible structure anchored at the platform (doesn't block walking, never fires, no bounty, shown as a health bar over the ramp) that soldiers, towers and heroes can attack; killing it collapses the ramp early ("RAMP DESTROYED"). Anything on a ramp breaks with it when it falls, expires or is replaced: perched ballistas (as before) and now the tesla coil (`structure.onMod`). Building a new ballista no longer removes his own ramp.

## Remnil: Gravewalk replaces Summon Grunts
- Remnil had two abilities that only spawned troops: R (2 grunts, 15 s, 24 s cooldown) and Z (Raise an Army: grunts, brute, archer, plus the hex burst, Legion evolution). R was the weaker, less distinct one (a small Z on a cooldown), so R is replaced; Z, Hex and the HEX SWAP combo are untouched.
- R · GRAVEWALK (`kind: "gravewalk"`, data in `heroes.json`): hold R and the hold/aim UI turns into a destination picker over every ready building on a pad your house owns, plus your keep. Tilt the stick toward one to pick it (the spot whose direction is closest); tilt again the same way to cycle through spots in that cone (within ~26°), nearest first. The pick is a purple marching-dot path from Remnil toward it, a purple ring of the empower radius on it, and a dashed line and blinking ring on the minimap. Tap R = go to the keep (if you're already beside the keep, tapping says "ALREADY HOME · HOLD TO PICK A GRAVE"). Spots within 8 m of Remnil are not offered. Not available while carrying the Grudge.
- Release: 1.0 s channel (action 1.15 s, rooted), then he teleports beside the building (on the side facing his own keep, nearest open cell; the keep sends him to the spawn point), 0.4 s invulnerable. A stun cancels the channel; starting the channel sets a 5 s cooldown (`interruptCooldown`), arriving sets the full 20 s. If the building dies or turns before he arrives: "THE GRAVE IS GONE", no teleport.
- Empower: for 10 s after arriving (`seconds`), while Remnil is within 6 m (`radius`, + building radius) of that building, it is empowered: an outpost's spawn interval ×0.6 (`spawnMul`, also pulls the next spawn in when it starts), a tower attacks ×1.5 faster (`towerHaste`, stacks with Stig's overclock). The keep is a retreat only, it isn't empowered. Visible as a spinning purple grave circle under the building with ghosts and soul flames rising and the odd skull above it, only while he's actually in range.
- Gate lockdown: the destination must be in the same lock zone as Remnil (`mapEvents.sealed`), checked when picking and again on arrival, so during the opening lockdown only buildings inside his own walls (and the keep) are offered.
- FX (SUMMONER atlas and props, no new art): channel = grave circle and rising grave hands at his feet, ghosts streaming up, a hex sigil and soul flames at the destination; arrival = burst, shockwave, smoke and ghosts where he left, and at the destination a hex idol bursting out of the ground, four tombstones rising and sinking around him, bones, skull. Ability glyph is a tombstone (`icons.ts`), anim "cast".
- Evolutions (level 2, replacing Bone Golem / Wraith Archers; their icons were renamed, not redrawn): RISEN RANKS (`fx.graveRank: 1`, +4 s empower) makes soldiers from an outpost while it's empowered spawn at rank 1 (VETERAN, via `World.promote`, so they get the rank-up flash and stats). WRAITH GATE: 0.6 s channel, and on arrival foes within 5 m take 60, are hexed (Remnil's hex duration) and slowed 50% for 2 s, and he gets a 120 shield. Hex combos re-pointed: Grave Pull now pairs with Wraith Gate (stun 1 s), Cascade with Risen Ranks (each hex raises a grunt). The bot plan still picks index 0 everywhere (Risen Ranks).
- CPUs: when retreating at low health with Gravewalk ready they gravewalk to the keep (unless an enemy hero is within 2.5 m). Otherwise, out of a fight with no enemy hero within 14 m, they gravewalk to one of their buildings at least 28 m away that has enemies around it (enemy soldiers within 14 m + 3 per enemy hero ≥ 3, at least some enemies, never with two enemy heroes there; outposts also count their own soldiers at half), then stay within reach of it while the empower lasts unless they get into a fight.
- Codex: ability, evolution, tactics and fear text updated; the R demo spawns a tower 10 m away and shows the channel, the walk and the empowered tower. No sims or round-robins were run for this change (by request): balance is untested.
- Economy: more outposts now means more soldiers. Each production building also yields grain worth 85% of its own soldiers' upkeep (`grain.outpostShare`; e.g. a level-1 barracks +2.27/s on top of the 0.7 building income), so outposts no longer starve each other on grain. Rubble on home (base) pads lasts 6 s (`rubbleHomeSeconds`), other pads still 20 s.
- Stig: ramp HP 500 → 300; a ballista standing on any cell of a ramp (not just the top deck) is tied to it and breaks when it falls or expires (perch bonus still top-deck only).
- Gravewalk follow-up: the empower starts the moment Remnil arrives and lasts exactly as long as he stays within 6 m (no 10 s window; Risen Ranks no longer adds time). Picking works like aiming Thorn's wall: while R is held every valid building gets a yellow square, the stick moves a floating marker (34 m/s, up to 90 m), and the building nearest the marker is selected (purple, pulsing, with the grave circle). CPU Remnils stay at a building at most 12 s.
- Gravewalk picker is a compass ring around Remnil (always on screen): each valid building is a small square in its real direction, placed from the inside out by distance rank (keep slightly larger and paler); rotating the stick selects the building whose bearing is closest; the selected square turns purple with a floating marker, and the real building gets the grave circle.
- Hex Swap only swaps with a hexed enemy champion within 10 m (it used to take the nearest hexed anything, so it usually swapped with a random hexed soldier).
- Online pre-match presence: guests now see and share the host's pre-match screens. New messages, all sent only when changed (rate-capped at 12 Hz): guest → host `{ t: "hand", k, h }` (its glove per seated device, or null) and `{ t: "nm", k, v }` (name-entry state `[typing, text]` or null); host → all `{ t: "pres", h, n, f }` with every hand by seat, every open name entry by seat, and the field pick `[pickIndex, mapIndex]` while on the field screen (plus a 1 s keepalive so late joiners catch up). A hand is `[hitId, fx, fy, dx, y, holding, pose]`: anchored to the button/card under it (fraction of its box, 0..63) with a centre-relative fallback, because the UI width follows each window's aspect ratio. Remote hands are drawn as smoothed "ghost" gloves in the seat's colour, carrying the chip they hold; a remote player's name entry shows a "SIGNING NAME" panel on their card with the letters as typed. The field picker is host-only: guests see the same screen (live preview, highlighted field, RANDOM) with the host's hand, "THE HOST PICKS THE FIELD" and only B LEAVE; their own hands still show on the host's screen. The full `lobby` snapshot stays at 4 Hz, and the in-match lockstep is unchanged.
- Brutes (heavies) were too hard to clear in swarms (no sims, by request): HP 380 → 320, grunts deal ×2.8 to them (was ×2.2), archers ×0.8 (was ×0.5), control tower ×0.8 (was ×0.5). A grunt now kills a brute in ~6 s (was ~10 s) while the brute needs ~19 s for a grunt.
- Free-for-all matches run 1.5× the rules' match length (`ffa.timeMul`; the default 6 minutes becomes 9), via `World.matchLength`, which the clock, sudden death and the pause screen all use. Sudden death length unchanged.
- Stig's discount is smaller in free-for-all (many neutral pads made it too strong): builds ×0.92 instead of ×0.8, upgrades and level-3 specs ×0.8 instead of ×0.6 (`ffaCostMul`/`ffaUpgradeCostMul`). 1v1/2v2 unchanged.
- Online lobby camera button: each seat's camera choice belongs to whoever plays that seat. The host can't toggle a remote seat; a guest toggles their own seat (saved in their local options, which their match camera uses) and sends `{t:"cam"}` to the host, which shows it on that seat and rebroadcasts it in the lobby view (`slot.cam`) so every screen shows the same eye state.
- Hero portraits are painted art (one Nano Banana sheet, `assets/generated/portraits_raw.png`, prompt in `tools/fx-prompts/portraits_prompt.txt`): eight full-frame head-and-shoulders paintings with a background that suits each hero (Wren included for later), cropped square to `assets/ui/portraits/<hero>.jpg` (256 px). `Portraits.icon()` returns the painting whenever one exists (character select row, HUD/pause/end-screen cards, records), falling back to the 3D render.
- Character select stage backdrops: one Nano Banana sheet of eight empty painted scenes matching each hero's portrait setting (`assets/generated/stages_raw.png`), cropped to 768×432 (`assets/ui/stages/<hero>.jpg`, labels trimmed off) and cover-fitted behind the hero model (works for the wide 1v1 cards and square 2v2/FFA cards); the seat's team shows as a thin team-coloured frame and a team glow rising from the floor.
- Titles are gold leaf in Pirata One (OFL blackletter, `assets/fonts/PirataOne.ttf`; chosen from eight candidates, matches the gothic logo) instead of the cracked western wood-type atlas: warm gold fill with a little texture and sheen, thin dark edge, soft drop shadow, wider tracking, capitals centred vertically in their box.
- No more upscaling blur: the UI's low layer now renders at the screen's pixel density (≥2×, ≤4× the 240-unit layout, like the hi layer) instead of a fixed 480 px canvas, and the 3D scene renders at the window height up to 1440 px (`lowResHeight`, was 720). The "dedither" blur stays off.
- "THE GRUDGE IS SWORN" ready banner is a crimson cloth banner hanging from a wooden rod with gold finials, gold trim and a swallowtail hem, with a drop shadow over a light dimming veil, gold-leaf title and cream PRESS START, so it reads as an overlay rather than another parchment card.
- Character select ability glyphs are per hero and per slot (A/B/R/Z): one Nano Banana sheet of flat white vector-style glyphs (`assets/generated/ability_glyphs_raw.png`), cut into a 7×4 alpha atlas `assets/ui/abilities.png` (96 px cells; columns warlord, engineer, raider, summoner, duelist, warden, herald), drawn engraved on the card (dark brown with a faint light lower edge) on the hi-res layer. Heroes not in the atlas fall back to the old generic kind glyphs.
- Body text no longer blurs: each text string used to be baked at a fixed 2.2 px per layout unit and then stretched to the hi layer (up to 4.5 at 1080p); it now bakes at the hi layer's actual density (rounded to 0.5, 2.2 to 6). The glyph atlas itself was already the high-res redraw.
- Debug: `npm run dev:split4` (or `?split4` on the URL) gives every seat a split-screen view even when CPUs play it, to test 4-player split performance alone. Title card's PRESS START is dark ink without the drop shadow (it sits on parchment).
- Costumes (first: Stig "FROSTFORGE"): a costume is a set of repainted textures for a hero's existing model and props, so rigging and animation are untouched. Pipeline (`tools/costume/`): render the model (rest pose) and each prop from four sides (`exp.py`), have Nano Banana repaint the views keeping silhouettes exact (body sheet + one props sheet, ~$0.15 each), then project the paintings back onto each texture with per-view depth tests and normal weighting (`bake2.py`); texels no view saw take a colour learned from the seen ones (original→new colour lookup); team texels stay grey for the dye. Files live in `assets/costumes/<hero>/<costume>/<material base>.jpg` (Stig: engineer, engineer_wrench, wrench (thrown), tesla, ballista). Runtime: `src/render/costumes.ts` swaps the map on matching materials for the hero, his held weapon, thrown wrench, tesla coil and ballista (props look up their owner's player costume). Picking: flick the C stick left/right on character select to cycle your seat's costume (label on the card, preview updates); sent through the online lobby (`{t:"costume"}`) and the match spec (`costumes`), cosmetic only. `?costumes=frost,...` for debug matches.
- Frostforge props repainted with more contrast (second props sheet): ballista in deep navy-stained wood with frosted dark iron, snow on top and icicles; tesla coil on dark slate stone with glowing ice-crystal rings and a sapphire orb with swirling cyan energy; darker frosted-steel wrench. Ramp decks: plank UVs now run continuously across cells (no per-cell tile squares) and support posts stop under the deck instead of poking through it.
- Costume roster: every hero has Classic + 3 costumes (Warlord: Bloodmoon, Gilded Tyrant, Swamp Brute; Stig: Frostforge, Soot & Ember, Clockwork Gold; Grim: Nightshade, Desert Jackal, Blood Goblin (red skin, black hood with ember trim; replaced Bonecloak, which read too close to his default); Remnil: Lich King, Plague Doctor, Crimson Cult; Francois: Black Rose, Musketeer Bleu, Carnival; Thorn: Winterbark, Autumn Elder, Blossom; Herald: Black Knight, Paladin of the Sun, Rusted Revenant), each covering the body and all held/placed props. Smash-style picker on the seat card: a row of round head icons (Nano Banana, Smash Ultimate stock-icon style, `assets/ui/costume_icons/<hero>_<costume>.png`, 96 px) with the current one framed and its name in a pill; C stick left/right cycles. Raw sheets in `assets/generated/costumes/`, prompts and scripts in `tools/costume/`.
- Costume picker only appears while you're using it: the head-icon row shows when you flick the C stick and hides 2.5 s after the last C-stick input; no name pill. CPU seats that pick a random hero also pick a random costume.
- Fixes: Francois's scabbard tip had an open, inside-out end (Tripo): the build recalculates its normals and caps the hole, and his body material renders double-sided so it reads solid from any angle (the hilt in the scabbard stays, as a gag). His baguette sits 7 cm closer, against his back. The archer's quiver and fletchings moved 7 cm closer to its back (unit rig island shift −0.06 → −0.13).
- Hi-res pass on procedural canvas textures (they were drawn at 32-128 px, the N64 size, and some forced pixelated sampling): cannon/target reticle and other combat FX textures (`canvasTex`), fxKit composites, status marks, rank chevrons, gear sprite, glow/radial sprites, zone ground decals, ability aim rings/areas, and the P1/P2 tags over heroes now render 4× supersampled (≈256-512 px); rank chevrons, map-event textures and gate stone use linear instead of nearest filtering.
- Wren (`marksman`) art pass: her procedural bow is now a Tripo longbow (`marksman_bow`, 512 px texture so it stays out of the part merger) bound to hand_L with a string whose middle rides a new `bow_string` bone (child of hand_L) that the draw clips pin to her right fingers; the quiver shafts are `marksman_arrows` on the chest, textured from the bow's wood texel. Pip is split off the body as `marksman_pip` (rigid on the chest) and rebaked into the left half of a shared 1024 atlas (`marksman_pip_skin`); the right half holds the flying Pip (`assets/props/pip.glb`, Tripo hawk with wings spread, `wing_L`/`wing_R` pivots at the wing roots, three.js `rotation.z` positive raises wing_L and lowers wing_R), so one costume texture recolours both. New clips in `tripo_heroes/marksman.py`, posed with small IK helpers (bow hand oriented so the bow stands upright with its back to the target, two-bone IK for the draw hand): `shoot` (nock, draw to the chin, loose at 9/18 frames, draw hand flies back), `cast` (right hand to Pip on the left shoulder, then a lunging forward-up throw at 8/17), `volley` (bow raised ~55° skyward, loose at 11/21), `heartseeker` (deep slow draw with a hold, loose at 13/22 with recoil); attack_a/b/c alias `shoot`. Costumes WINTER HUNT (white fur, snowy-owl Pip, birch bow), RAVEN (black feathers, raven Pip, ebony bow), SUNFIRE (crimson and gold, phoenix Pip, gilded bow) via the usual repaint-and-bake (`tools/costume/marksman_bake.py`), Smash-style icons from one sheet (`tools/costume/marksman_icons.py`, flipped so the braid sits on her right like the model, braid faded at the bottom edge), ability glyph strip (`marksman_glyphs.py`, 4×128 px), stage backdrop repainted with an empty perch. Portrait unchanged (still matches the model). fal: ~$2.7 (10 Nano Banana, 2 Tripo).
- Brother Maddock "The Alemonk" (`friar`) art: a big round red-bearded friar in a deep teal-green habit (gold hop-vine embroidery, cream cuffs, wine-red cord, blue team stole), 1.95 m. Nano Banana turnaround (Stig/Wren/portrait refs, one A-pose fix edit) → Tripo P1 body (14k tris) + props sheet (tankard, ale keg, powder keg, giant cask) → 4 Tripo props. `tools/blender/tripo_heroes/friar.py`: hand skinning like Remnil (robe on the hips, belly kept off the sleeves, shoulder blend), Tripo tankard in hand_R (`friar_tankard`), the runtime `keg` prop strapped rigid to his back (`friar_keg`, shares the keg costume texture), new `throw` clip (overhand heave, release ~55%), horizontal tankard sweeps for attack_a/b, overhead tankard smash attack_c, tankard-raise blessing cast, two-handed cask slam; legs damped on every clip so the robe doesn't tear. Runtime props `keg`/`powderkeg` (Tripo barrels are hollow loose stave shells that tear when decimated: `build_tripo_props.py` now rebuilds them as a lathe body following the measured outer profile, keeps protruding bits like the fuse, and bakes the painted texture back with inward ray casts, mirrored for the side the sheet didn't show) and `bigkeg` (open end capped with a disc textured from the other head). Costumes ABBOT (ivory and gold), HOPMASTER (golden hops harvest), GROG PIRATE (black coat, striped sleeves, bandana, black grog kegs) covering body, tankard, keg, powder keg and cask; one icon sheet for all four heads; painted portrait (brewhouse), stage backdrop (vaulted brewery cellar) and ability glyph strip `assets/ui/ability_glyphs/friar.png`. About $4.90 of fal credit.

## Two new champions: Wren (Longbow) and Maddock (Alemonk)
- Both are added purely through data plus a sim module each (`src/sim/marksman.ts`, `src/sim/friar.ts`); the roster, select row, codex, records, pause and end screens all read `heroes.json`, so they show up everywhere. Select row: cards shrink to fit nine (42 px → ~39 px at 16:9, never below 26). No sims or round-robins were run (by request); numbers were set against the existing kits by hand.
- WREN (`marksman`, low health, mid speed, mid damage, bot range 9). VANTAGE (`hooks.vantage*`): +25% damage and +20% reach when she stands 1 m above her target or hasn't moved 0.25 m for 1 s (`HeroState.stillAt`); shown as a gold glint on her and a gold diamond beside her Z meter. A: longbow arrow 42, 11 m (13.2 with vantage), 0.55 s; holding A to at least half charge looses a power shot, a piercing missile (14 m, ×1.15 on top of the charge) that knocks back. B SEND PIP (14 s, range 14, tap = nearest champion in front, hold = aim): Pip is sim state (`HeroState.pip`: out → on → back, 24 m/s) and latches for 5 s: +15% damage from Wren (`markMul`), reveals through grass and smoke (`status.pipUntil` beats `hidden`), and every tick breaks any channel the victim runs (recall, jump-pad charge, enshrine/steal, Gravewalk, horn capture) with an INTERRUPTED! callout; each of Wren's arrows that lands adds a 12 peck. R VOLLEY (12 s, placeable 9 m, 3.5 m): 5 waves × 26 over 2 s, slows to 60%, ×1.5 to soldiers, ×0.5 to buildings. Z HEARTSEEKER: 0.9 s draw (invulnerable like every super), then a hitscan line 28 m stopped by walls; first champion 220 + knockback 12, everything else in the line 110 (buildings 120); a ±20° lock-on nudges it onto a champion. SKYSHOT combo: dodge while Pip holds someone within 14 m → 4 m backflip away from them (`startJump`) and a guaranteed crit arrow (×1.3) as she lands.
- Wren evolutions: R Fire Arrows (burning ground) / Double Volley (second rain at 80%); B Long Watch (8 s, +25%; with Double Volley the prey is slowed 30%) / Raking Talons (Pip pecks every second, pecks bleed; with Fire Arrows +8 per peck); A Broadhead (18 splash, longer power shot) / Quickdraw (20% faster, champion hits cut Pip's cooldown 1 s); Z Ricochet (60% bounce within 10 m) / Hunter's Mark (house-wide 20% mark on everything hit, Pip launches at the champion). Bot plan 0-1-0-0. Z now has two choices for these heroes (picked like the others; the 10 s auto-pick still applies).
- MADDOCK (`friar`, sturdy health, mid speed ×0.94, low damage, bot range 4.5). PLENTY: allies within 7 m regenerate 0.6%/s (heroes) or 1.2%/s (soldiers), and all his healing is ×1.25 on anyone under half health (`healFrom`, also used by grove zones). A: tankard/staff combo 40/40/64 (×0.8 tier). B HEALING KEG (10 s, 8 m, tap auto-picks the most hurt group): 110 splash heal to every ally in 3 m, then a 2.6 m ale puddle healing 22/s for 5 s. R POWDER KEG (11 s, 8 m): lands with a 1 s fuse (instantly if it lands within 1.4 m of a champion), 110 in 3.2 m, ×1.6 to soldiers, 160 to buildings, knockback 10. Kegs are sim objects (`World.kegs`) so they arc and fuse deterministically. Z BREWFEST: a 600 HP cask (`structure.cask`: siege-flagged so towers and troops attack it, doesn't block movement or nav) for 10 s with a 7 m zone (`Zone.anchor/brew`): allies heal 24/s, deal +15% (`status.brewUntil`) and are cleansed of slows; enemies are slowed to 70%. The zone ends with the cask. KEG ROCKET combo: dodge while standing in his own puddle → 0.62 s keg ride at 17 m/s, 45 + sideways knockback to everything touched, CC immune, leaves 2.5 s healing trail puddles that can't re-trigger it.
- Maddock evolutions: R Cluster Keg (three 40-damage minis) / Tar Keg (burning tar, 50% slow); B Hearty Stout (80 shields; with Cluster Keg two 40-heal minis) / Wellspring (bigger, 8 s, +15% speed; with Tar Keg foes in it slowed 30%); A Taproom Brawler (15% lifesteal, heals soldiers) / Belly Flop (finisher ×1.35, stun 0.6 s); Z Grand Cask (14 s, 9 m, 900 HP) / Last Call (cask bursts for 150 heal / 80 damage when it ends or breaks). Bot plan 0-0-0-0 plus `botPlan.healer`: kegs whenever enough missing health is in reach (`bot: "heal"`), powder kegs at clumps/heroes/buildings (`clumpScore`), kites champions at 4.5 m but melees soldiers and anything that steps into reach; ranged bots now face their target when they fire their super.
- Visuals (`src/render/newHeroFx.ts`, no new painted sheets): Pip is `prop("pip")` (owner's costume, `wing_L/R` flap) flying out, circling and pecking over the victim's head and flying home; the `marksman_pip` node on Wren hides while it's away (and `friar_keg` while a keg is in the air or he's rocketing); `_pip`/`_wrench` meshes are never merged so they can hide. Arrows are small 3D arrows with sparkle trails; power shot a glowing spear; volley a gold ring decal and a real rain of arrows that stick in the ground; Heartseeker a heart-rune draw, a beam with a flying arrow and a big impact. Kegs use `prop("keg"/"powderkeg"/"bigkeg")` (procedural barrels as fallback), with amber splash, foam, droplets and pluses, a golden bubbling puddle decal, a sparking fuse and pulsing red ring before the blast, fire/smoke/plank explosions, and the cask with a golden ring zone and rising bubbles. Heal numbers float in green (`heroFx` `healNum`). New sim event `heroFx` carries all of this; sfx have chirps, bowstrings, splashes and booms. HUD: a red dot on B while Pip is out.
- Wren and Maddock have one super evolution each like everyone else (Wren: Hunter's Mark, Maddock: Grand Cask; Ricochet and Last Call cut). Their 14 evolution icons are painted in the talent-icon style (one Nano Banana sheet, `assets/generated/new_talents_raw.png`).
- Wren damage pass (playtest: her real DPS felt ~30): arrows 42→62 and 0.55→0.5 s, full-power shot scales with charge (×1.88–2.16 instead of ×1.15), Pip peck 12→20, Volley waves 26→36, Heartseeker 220/110→300/140. Pip now pecks on his own every 1.5 s while latched (Raking Talons: every 0.75 s), and pressing B again while he's latched is TALON RAKE: 80 damage, blinds 2.5 s (50% of the victim's hits miss, champions and soldiers), interrupts, and Pip flies home. Gives Pip a hold-or-cash-in decision; bots rake when the mark is about to expire, the target is low, or it's swinging at them.
- Wren auto-aim: her A no longer uses the generic narrow-cone `aimTarget`. `wrenTarget` picks with no stick input the best visible enemy in any direction (champions strongly preferred, Pip's prey next, structures last); with stick input it uses a wide forward cone. Arrows with no target fly as real missiles (damage on hit) instead of zero-damage point shots, and her arrows ignore the uphill miss roll. Pip's prey is slowed 25% while he's latched (Long Watch + Double Volley makes it 30%).
- Wren and Maddock have their own painted FX atlases (`assets/fx/wren.png`, `assets/fx/friar.png`; raw sheets in `assets/generated/fx/`): feathers, talon claws, arrows, splinters, autumn leaves, hunter's mark ring, arrow ring, heart, glint, blind swirl; foam, bubbles, ale drops, splash, ale puddle, hops, staves, heal plus, powder smoke, fuse spark, blast, hop-vine ring. No other hero's atlas is used by their FX any more; the procedural foam/bubble/ring textures were replaced with the painted ones.
- Wren's portrait repainted: she's drawing her bow with Pip screeching on her shoulder (the old one had her arms spread stiffly).
- Wren's Winter Hunt repainted with colour (it read as flat white): midnight-blue cloak and hood with a red/cream/teal folk-knit band, cream fur, teal quilted jerkin, red-brown straps with a holly sprig, navy boots with red laces, birch quiver with red bands; navy folk-painted bow with a red grip; Pip becomes a slate-blue frost falcon with a barred cream breast and rusty throat. Her four costume icons were regenerated together as one set to match.
- HUD resource and order icons are painted sprites (one Nano Banana sheet, `assets/ui/hud/*.png`, raw `assets/generated/hud_icons_raw.png`): gold coin, grain sack, steel kettle helmet with a team-tinted plume (army count), team-tinted marble hex plot (pads), and enamel order badges with gold rims (attack red chevrons, follow blue loop, defend green shield, hold gold palm, hunt orange crosshair, siege purple tower). Tinting is a cached multiply per colour; an attack order aimed at a specific house gets that house's colour as a ring. Wren's Winter Hunt textures got +60% saturation.
- Gate lockdown on the clock: the match timer holds at full length while the gates are shut and starts when they open (match length = matchSeconds + lockdown, so the 30 s build-up no longer eats match time). A painted padlock sits left of the timer with a gold ring that ticks down the lockdown, flashes and shakes in the last 5 s, then pops open and fades. The "GATES OPEN IN" label and warning card are gone; the "THE GATES OPEN" card stays.
- First model costume: Warlord "IRON COLOSSUS" (`colossus`), a steam war-automaton (riveted gunmetal, magenta enamel plates with brass trim, glowing furnace grate and eyes, smokestack shoulders, piston tusks, clawed iron feet) with a piston STEAM HAMMER instead of the club. Pipeline as for new heroes: Nano Banana turnaround from the Warlord's Tripo mesh + Stig's sheet (one fix edit for vivid magenta and a wider A-pose), a hammer sheet, Tripo P1 body (14k) and hammer (Tripo dropped the haft; `bridge()` rebuilds it). `tools/blender/tripo_heroes/warlord_colossus.py` reuses `warlord.py`'s CFG and clips with its own joints, rigid shoulders/feet, and `name`/`src`/`out` keys (new, optional in `build_tripo_hero.py`; existing heroes unchanged) so it exports `assets/heroes/warlord@colossus.glb` with the same bones, node names (`warlord`, `warlord_club`), materials (`team_warlord` dyed) and clips as `warlord.glb`.
- Runtime: any `assets/heroes/<hero>@<costume>.glb` is loaded at boot with the other heroes and adds `<costume>` to `costumesOf(hero)` (after the texture costumes); `HeroModels.create` uses it when that costume is picked (matches, select stage), texture costumes unchanged. `prop(name, team, owner)` likewise prefers `assets/props/<name>@<costume>.glb`. Viewer: `?costume=<id>`. All five Warlord costume icons were regenerated together from one sheet (`tools/costume/warlord_icons.py`) so the set stays consistent. fal: ~$1.70 (4 Nano Banana, 2 Tripo).
- Iron Colossus rig fix: rigid boxes that cut a mesh island in half left pieces split between bones (pauldron rims tore into flaps at the armpit when the arm lifted; tabard, belt wheel and gauge were split hips/thigh/spine). `rigid_parts` takes an optional `whole: True` (only islands lying entirely inside the box, welded by position, go 100% to the bone; existing heroes unaffected). The Colossus uses it for the head (helmet, horns, tusks), both pauldrons with their smokestacks and the furnace grate (chest), and tabard/buckle/gauge (hips); toe claws and heel spurs stay rigid on the shins. Every small island is now on a single bone.
- Francois model costume "THE DROWNED CAPTAIN" (`drowned`): a new body, not a repaint: a gaunt, hunched, skeletal drowned naval captain (long skull-like grey-green face with gills and glowing cyan eyes, big barnacled tricorn with kelp, tattered indigo frock coat with tarnished gold braid, coral on the right shoulder, coral-crusted scabbard on the left hip, royal-blue sash and half-cape as the team dye). The base model was only used for the A-pose/scale; Nano Banana was told to redesign the build (one fix edit for a bigger hat, hunch and coral side). Props from one Tripo mesh split in two: a narwhal-tusk rapier with a coral guard (`duelist_rapier`) and a swordfish across his back in place of the baguette (`duelist_baguette`). `tools/blender/tripo_heroes/duelist_drowned.py` reuses `duelist.py`'s clips with its own joints and whole-island rigid parts (head/hat/kelp, coral, epaulette, cape, scabbard, boots); output `assets/heroes/duelist@drowned.glb` with the same bones, nodes, `team_duelist` material and clips. New optional `weld` key in `build_tripo_hero.py` (proxy weld distance for bone-heat weighting, default 0.002 unchanged): this mesh needs 0.005 or heat weighting fails. All five Francois icons regenerated together from one sheet (`tools/costume/duelist_icons.py`). fal: ~$1.85 (5 Nano Banana, 2 Tripo).
- Remnil model costume THE SHADOW PLAY (`shadowplay`): a shadow-theatre puppet in thick black lacquered card with bold gold leaf, punched lace and brass joint pins, much thinner and taller (2.3 m incl. the crown) with a flat gilded beak mask and a tall black-and-gold crown, mid-thigh paper-panel skirt, blue team sash and chest diamond.
