Nano Banana was given the base model only for pose/framing and told to redesign the body; Tripo body + Tripo calligraphy-brush staff (stretched 2.45 m along its shaft, drips bent down along it, gold boosted). `tools/blender/tripo_heroes/summoner_shadowplay.py` (no builder changes): hand skinning (skirt mostly on the hips, arms/legs by nearest segment), gold puppet rods built procedurally and joined into the body, rigid on the forearms, Remnil's clips with lighter leg damping and a per-key ground solve (hips lifted so nothing sinks below the floor, death ignores arms/head and nods the head so the crown doesn't dig in). Exports `assets/heroes/summoner@shadowplay.glb` with the same bones, nodes (`summoner`, `summoner_staff`), materials and clips. All five Remnil icons regenerated from one sheet (`tools/costume/summoner_icons.py`). fal: ~$1.55 (3 Nano Banana, 2 Tripo).
- Thorn model costume THE SUN TOTEM (`suntotem`): a giant saguaro-cactus guardian with a new body shape (thick columnar ribbed trunk, saguaro branches curving up from the shoulders, low stump head with a carved sandstone sun-disc mask with rays, pink/yellow cactus-flower crown, spines, turquoise-and-gold bands, sandstone-wrapped root feet, blue woven sash and loincloth as the team dye) and a carved stone sun-disc shield on the left forearm. The base render was only a pose/scale reference; Nano Banana was told to redesign the body (one fix edit added the shoulder branches), then Tripo P1 body (14k) and shield. `tools/blender/tripo_heroes/warden_suntotem.py` reuses `warden.py` with its own joints (2.4 m), `whole` rigid head/shoulder branches/loincloth/feet, spines and other small islands copying the weights of the nearest body vertex, and a procedural ribbed cactus cap closing the open back of the Tripo head; exports `assets/heroes/warden@suntotem.glb` with the same bones, node names (`warden`, `warden_shield`), materials and clips as `warden.glb`. Runtime props (wall, snares) unchanged. All five Thorn icons regenerated from one sheet (`tools/costume/warden_icons.py`). fal: ~$1.70 (4 Nano Banana, 2 Tripo).
- Maddock model costume BROTHER CELADON (`celadon`): a squat glazed-porcelain tea-house monk figurine (ball belly, big round head, short legs, 1.85 m, ~3.5 heads tall): white porcelain with cobalt willow-pattern scenes, red lacquer trim/cord/slippers, gold kintsugi cracks, glossy red porcelain beard and tonsure; the team part is a jade-glazed stole (green on the texture only so it can be told apart from the blue glaze, then dyed). Nano Banana kept copying the base proportions when asked to reshape, so the new silhouette was forced by hand: `tools/costume/friar_celadon_warp.py` bulges/shortens the first sheet's front view into a rough mock-up that Nano Banana repaints, and `friar_celadon_arms.py` rotates the arms of a raised-arm edit down into an A-pose with a clear gap. Tripo P1 body; one Tripo call for all four props on one 2×2 sheet, split and rebaked per prop by `tools/costume/friar_celadon_split.py` (the urn body is re-lathed from its median profile, keeping the stand and spout, because Tripo grew a lump on its back). `tools/blender/tripo_heroes/friar_celadon.py` reuses the friar clips (hips offsets ×0.45 for the short legs) with its own hand skinning (arms by distance to the arm chain, everything beyond the belly on the arm), a wider arms-down rest so the arms clear the belly, and stole detection by majority of texel samples per face. Exports `assets/heroes/friar@celadon.glb` (same bones, clips, `friar_tankard` = porcelain teapot, `friar_keg` = ginger jar, materials `friar_skin`/`team_friar`/`friar_tankard_skin`/`keg_skin`) and props `keg@celadon` (lidded ginger jar), `powderkeg@celadon` (red lacquer firecracker jar with fuse), `bigkeg@celadon` (celadon urn on a red lacquer stand with a brass tap). All five Maddock icons regenerated from one sheet (`tools/costume/friar_icons.py`). fal: ~$2.00 (6 Nano Banana, 2 Tripo).
- Grim costume "SPOREBLIGHT" (`sporeblight`): Grim overgrown by a giant crimson toadstool, a new body rather than a repaint: scrawny, stooped and pot-bellied with very long gangly arms (hands below the knees), bowed legs and bare root-toed feet, mossy mottled skin with glowing spore pods, a white-spotted crimson cap (gills underneath) about twice his shoulder width instead of the hood, puffballs on a rope belt, tiers of shelf fungus down his back; blue scarf and loincloth are the team dye. His knives are jagged bracket-fungus shards with root grips and toadstool pommels (`raider_knives`; the thrown knife stays the FX sprite). Nano Banana turnaround (Grim's model used only for pose/scale, body explicitly redesigned), Tripo P1 body; `tools/costume/raider_sporeblight_hunch.py` bends the Tripo mesh into a 24° stoop (head kept level, arms carried with the shoulders) → `assets/source/raider_sporeblight_hunch.glb`. `tools/blender/tripo_heroes/raider_sporeblight.py` reuses Grim's CFG/clips with its own joints and rigid parts, adds the procedural shelf fungus (body texture, chest/spine), retints the skin toward moss green and the cap toward crimson, and keeps raised arms below the cap (slam/cast arms forward and wide, lower block, death lands on the cap with arms up). Exports `assets/heroes/raider@sporeblight.glb` (same bones, clips, nodes `raider`/`raider_knives`, materials). All five Grim icons regenerated from one sheet (`tools/costume/raider_icons.py`; eye colours restored per costume in the cut). fal: ~$1.70 (4 Nano Banana incl. one knife retry, 2 Tripo).
- Wren model costume STARFALL (`starfall`): a tall slender elf huntress of the night sky (2.1 m vs 1.9, ~8 heads, small head with pointed ears, glowing silver eyes, nebula hair, star-dusted violet-magenta hooded cloak with a crescent-moon clasp, silver star-metal armour, blue team tabard). Nano Banana was given the base Tripo mesh only for pose/frame and told to redesign the body; Tripo P1 body (shifted 12 cm so the fit is centred). `tools/blender/tripo_heroes/marksman_starfall.py` reuses `marksman.py`'s clips and IK (cast reach moved up to the new shoulder) with its own joints, hands the cloak island's arm weights to the torso (`fix_cloak`) and gently retints the cloak toward violet. Bow: a silver crescent-moon bow lofted from the silhouette in the props sheet (`tools/costume/marksman_starfall_bow.py`, front-projected texture plus swatches) on the same `bow_string` bone with a glowing string; a slim silver quiver with five comet-tipped arrows (`marksman_arrows`); a Tripo constellation phoenix whose wings are folded procedurally sits on the left pauldron (`marksman_pip`), and the unfolded one is the flying Pip `assets/props/pip@starfall.glb` (`tools/costume/marksman_starfall_pip.py`, same node names as `pip.glb`). All five Wren icons regenerated from one sheet (`tools/costume/marksman_icons.py`, now cut by detected columns with a shared square). fal: ~$1.55 (3 Nano Banana, 2 Tripo).
- Stig model costume CALLIOPE STIG (`calliope`): a travelling-carnival calliope mechanic with a new body shape (round barrel chest and belly, short stubby legs, 1.9 m incl. the organ pipes; the head is now a round rosy face under a violet bowler with brass goggles and a huge curled white handlebar moustache instead of the full beard), violet-and-gold striped tailcoat with gold epaulettes, checked violet/gold trousers, white gloves, blue waistcoat as the team colour (`team_engineer`), and a calliope organ case with brass pipes rising over his head rigid on the chest. Weapon (`engineer_wrench`) is a giant brass WIND-UP KEY. Pipeline: Nano Banana from Stig's Tripo turnaround (pose/scale only, body explicitly redesigned), props sheet (key, carousel tower, cannon), Tripo P1 body 14k, key 4k, tower 5k. `tools/costume/engineer_calliope_prep.py` pushes the organ islands 7 cm back off the coat before rigging (raw source kept); `tools/blender/tripo_heroes/engineer_calliope.py` has its own joints, whole-island rigid organ/head/epaulettes/shoes, glues the small coat buttons to the nearest body weights, drops the two back-strap islands that tore through the coat when the spine bent, and places the key. Prop overrides (`tools/costume/engineer_calliope_props.py`): `wrench@calliope` (thrown key) and `tesla@calliope` (carousel calliope tower, base gap closed); the tesla zone (Z) now passes its owner's costume to `teslaCoil`. The human-cannonball ballista was left for later (budget). All five Stig icons regenerated together from one sheet (`tools/costume/engineer_icons.py`). fal: $2.10 (3 Nano Banana, 3 Tripo).
- Brewfest cask is solid now: heroes and units collide with it like other structures (only its centre cell is nav-blocked so it can't seal a bridge). The Brewfest ring is a dedicated 1024 px painted hop-vine wreath (`assets/fx/brewfest_ring.png`, one Nano Banana call, polar-remapped into a thin rim band repeated 3× so cones stay small) instead of a stretched 128 px atlas cell.
- Text sharpness: the HUD's sharp text layer was capped at 4× the 240-line layout (960 px), so on 1440p/4K screens it was stretched and blurry; the cap is now 8×. World-space callouts and damage numbers bake at 3× (callouts were 30 px tall canvases, damage number slots 32 px; the number atlas is now 384×8192).
- Costume FX atlases: the eight model costumes (warlord@colossus, engineer@calliope, raider@sporeblight, summoner@shadowplay, duelist@drowned, warden@suntotem, wren@starfall for the marksman, friar@celadon) each have their own painted 4×4 FX atlas `assets/fx/<atlas>@<costume>.png`, same slot meanings as the base atlas, repainted in the costume's theme (one Nano Banana edit per costume: base atlas on magenta/green + a viewer render of the costume, slot-by-slot list in `tools/fx-prompts/costume_fx_*_prompt.txt`; raws in `assets/generated/fx/`, cut with `tools/fx-prompts/costume_fx_cut.py`). Engine (`fxKit.ts`): the hero atlas objects are getters that resolve against an "active costume" (`withCostume`/`useCostume`), variants are lazily loaded per costume (preloaded when the match's costumes are set) and fall back to the base cell. `CombatFx.handle` sets the costume from the event's source (hero, or the owner of a summon), projectiles/missiles from their source, and `add`/`after` callbacks re-enter the costume they were scheduled under; HeroPropViews (Pip, kegs, keg rocket), zones, snares, wall FX and the grave ring do the same. Captured textures, composites (zone decals) and module materials go through `cv(tex)` / `cm(mat)` which return cached per-costume variants (composites are redrawn with the variant cells only if they use one); 3D-decal/fissure lookups use `baseTex`. Kit colours that clash are remapped per costume (`tint`, table in `costumeFx.ts`, e.g. Shadow Play violet→gold, Starfall green/red→violet, Celadon ale-gold→jade/blue), plus per-costume weapon trail colours (`trailOf`). Classic costumes and costumes without an atlas are unchanged. fal: $1.35 (9 Nano Banana; Sporeblight regenerated once because its background came out pale).
- Costume runtime props (1 Nano Banana sheet, 6 Tripo): the props a model costume spawns now match it. `prop()` already preferred `assets/props/<name>@<costume>.glb`; `propParts(name, costume)` (instanced/merged props) now does too (`hasCostumeProp`), and every call site passes the owner's costume: Warlord's quake/earthsplitter spikes (`spikeBatch(root, costume)`, batch keyed per costume), Remnil's hex idol and Gravewalk tombs (`rosterFx` event/act use `src`), the bones zone tombs, Thorn's wall (`wallstone`/`palisade` from the mod's `owner`), Stig's ballista. New props in `build_tripo_props.py` with the base prop's size/pivot: `spike@colossus` (riveted gunmetal piston stake with magenta enamel bands), `hexidol@shadowplay` (black-and-gold paper lantern idol with a beak mask and crown on a gilded post), `tomb@shadowplay` (black lacquer paper-theatre coffin stage with gold curtains), `wallstone@suntotem` (adobe pillar with turquoise zig-zag inlay, sun disc and beam ends), `ballista@calliope` (the striped human-cannonball cannon from the earlier Calliope props sheet; new `build_cannon`: the whole mesh turns under `yaw` > `tilt`, no string/bolt nodes so the syncBallista recoil/yaw still works). Thorn's bramble zone with Sun Totem grows `cactus@suntotem` saguaros and `thorns@suntotem` desert thorn tangles (one Tripo call for both, split by the empty gap: new `keep` option) instead of the vine arches and thorn clumps; his snares, the bramble cast roots, the snap vines and the reach arm use a procedural ribbed cactus material with pale spines and flowers (`src/render/desertKit.ts`, `isDesert(costume)`). Wren's arrows (shot, volley rain, power shot) are silver with violet fletching for Starfall. Classic costumes unchanged (checked side by side). Prompt `tools/costume/costume_runtime_props_prompt.txt`, cutter `costume_runtime_props_crop.py`, raws in `assets/generated/costumes/`. Still classic: Remnil's summoned soldiers (they are the shared grunt/brute/archer units, no skeleton model), Stig's ramps/works planks, Grim's knives and other sprite FX (FX atlases). fal: $3.45 (1 Nano Banana, 6 Tripo).
- GameCube adapter driver is vendored in the repo: `tools/gc-adapter/src/` (upstream hid-gamecube-adapter r11.25387ab with the per-port patch applied, DKMS version r11.25387ab.grudge1), udev rules in `tools/gc-adapter/udev/`, and a self-contained `install.sh` (checks dkms/headers, removes other DKMS versions incl. the AUR one, installs, reloads, prints ports). Steps in `tools/gc-adapter/README.md`.
- Sun Totem / Starfall cleanup (no fal): `warden_suntotem.py` decimates the Tripo body per island before the cap is added (`lighten`): spines smaller than 6 cm are dropped, other spines halved, every other island capped to a density by its size (the flower crown had ~650 tris per 4 cm flower) and the head/mask islands ×0.6 more; shield decimated to 1.3k. 16.8k → 11.5k tris (the build script's own count said 30.9k because it summed the joined source objects twice; the exported GLB had 16.8k). The procedural head cap now samples a flat 24 px patch of cactus green with its rib UVs instead of two single texels, so its back no longer streaks. `marksman_starfall.py`: the elf ear tips are pulled radially under the hood and take the hood's weights (`tuck_ears`), the dented hood patch where they came through is relaxed and its painted crease filled with the surrounding cloak colour; the cloak (non-team maroon faces, face excluded) gets a lighter violet gradient toward the hem and bigger, brighter stars with a soft halo plus a sprinkle of new ones (`paint_cloak`), so the dusting reads at game distance. Tris unchanged (18.5k). Base `warden.glb`/`marksman.glb` untouched.
- HD texture pass for large on-screen FX: `assets/fx/hq/<atlas>.<key>[@costume].png` holds dedicated 512–1024 px paintings that override a 4×4 atlas cell wherever it is drawn big. `fxKit.hd(tex)` resolves base cell → costume (an atlas with an `@costume` sheet but no HQ variant keeps its themed 128 px cell rather than showing the wrong theme) → HQ texture once loaded (base HQs start loading after `fxReady`, costume HQs in `preloadCostumeFx`); `decal()`, the ale/brewfest zone decals and the grave ring use it. Overrides: `warden.rune` (+suntotem), `summoner.circle`/`summoner.hex` (+shadowplay), `wren.arrowRing` (+starfall), `friar.puddle` (+celadon), `raider.vortex`, and `friar.hopRing` (the old `brewfest_ring.png`, so the Brewfest zone and its cast burst share it) plus a new celadon willow-pattern porcelain rim with tea leaves (painted wreath polar-remapped into the same thin 3× band). Three Nano Banana 2×2 sheets ($0.45). `setCostumeTex` alias removed. Procedural decals `targetTex`/crack/frost/emblem/swirl/rune/gear canvases and the friar `disc()` decals (tar) now paint at 512 px (`canvasTex`/`composite` take a `res`). Audited and left: warlord/engineer/herald/duelist/raider big ground FX are 3D (fissures, crest, crown, laurel, gear, torus shockwaves); 0.2–0.3 s bursts (FX.burst, SUMMONER.burst, FRIAR.blast, ALE_SPLASH at 3–6 m) stay 128 px cells.
- Wren nerf (she shredded everything): one arrow then a 1.4 s reload (hold A to charge the power shot), arrows 62→56, new health tier frail (480), speed low, Pip flies at 8 m/s (was 24) so he's visible coming and can be dodged, drawn twice as big, Talon Rake 80→70.
- Costume FX made unique, not recoloured (8 Nano Banana, $1.20): many costume atlas cells were the classic subject in new colours (Colossus kept the horn, horned helm, lava crack, fireballs and rubble ring; Calliope, Sporeblight, Starfall, Celadon mostly recolours; Shadow Play was the same skulls/bones/ghosts in black paper). Each costume sheet was regenerated from its current atlas + costume render with a KEEP/NEW list (`tools/fx-prompts/costume_fx_<atlas>_<costume>_unique_prompt.txt`, raws `assets/generated/fx/<atlas>@<costume>_unique.png`) and only the NEW cells were pasted in, so cells with HQ variants are untouched (Colossus 12 cells: steam valve blast, buckled plate with molten seams, steam whistle, automaton bucket helm, steam jet, hot rivet, molten iron splash, brass pipe ring, plate-fragment impact, furnace vent, gear/bolt/plate debris ring; Calliope 14: pinwheel, music-box key, sparkler, steam with notes, popcorn, streamer, marquee bulb, confetti smoke, cymbal, bulb ring/cross, confetti; Sporeblight 11: spore pods, spore/gill slashes, glowing mushroom, sap drip, puffball, fairy ring; Shadow Play 12: moon puppet, crow puppet, puppet limbs, lantern, footlight, rod-puppet hand, paper scraps, ink, tragedy mask, gold streamer; Drowned 8: anglerfish lure, foam, spray, eel, starfish, waterspout, coral ring, urchin; Sun Totem 6: sand, mesquite roots, cactus spines, sun disc; Starfall 8: star crystals, comet trails, constellation ring/heart, stardust, star-shard burst; Celadon 9: tea steam, plum blossom, tea drop/splash, kintsugi flame, clinking teacups, gold leaf). Code: `setCostumeSwap(costume, from, to)` in `fxKit` swaps a common `FX.*` cell for a themed atlas cell while that costume is active (dust/smoke → steam, spores, ink scraps, sea foam, sand, stardust, tea steam; Colossus fire/splash → furnace vent/molten iron; Celadon fire → kintsugi flame, Starfall twinkle → star), falling back to the classic cell until the variant has loaded. `COSTUME_SKIN` in `fxParts` (filled in `costumeFx.ts`) themes the procedural 3D pieces: chunk geometry/colours (Colossus iron plates/brass cogs/nuts, Calliope popcorn/confetti/party hats, Sporeblight puffballs/caps, Shadow Play paper flakes, Drowned shells/coral, Sun Totem sandstone, Starfall crystals, Celadon porcelain shards), fissure materials (Colossus: dark iron cut, flattened riveted-plate lips, `assets/fx/colossus_seam.png` molten seam core; Celadon porcelain lips), fissure style remap (Sporeblight crack → moss) and per-texture decal overrides (Colossus rubble ring → flat gear/bolt ring, crest → brass gear; Calliope gear → flat pinwheel; Sporeblight smoke torus → flat spore ring). Colossus orange shock/rune colours are tinted to steam white/magenta. Classic is unchanged. Still shared: the generic white shock torus, `FX.burst`/`burst2` flashes (additive), shield shards/debris, Thorn's moss fissures for Sun Totem (sand-tinted), Stig's works planks/hammers.

## Performance pass (4-player split screen on integrated GPUs, Ryzen 5 2400G / Vega 11)
- Target: hold 60 fps in 4-player split at 1080p on a 2400G (Vega 11 shares DDR4 bandwidth with a 4-core Zen 1 CPU). Measured on the dev box (Ryzen 7 5700G CPU, RX 5700 XT), headless Chromium at 1920x1080, `?bots=4&mode=2v2&split4&map=crossing&time=150&rank=2&seed=7&perf`, busy mid-game fight.
- `?perf` adds a profiler overlay (`src/perf.ts`): CPU ms per section (sim, render: events/entities/fx, per-view prep and draw, post; HUD draw), GPU ms per view and post via `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2`, draw calls and triangles per view, HUD memo redraws and text bakes per frame, worst frame. Results are also in `window.grudgePerf`. It costs nothing without the flag.
- Before → after (live fight, frame averages; the fight differs a bit between captures): triangles per frame (4 views) 1.76M → 1.12M (high) / 0.91M (fast); draw calls ~260 → ~270 / ~245; GPU (all views + post) 1.71 → 1.61 / 1.44 ms; CPU frame 7.7 → 7.4 / 7.05 ms; HUD draw 1.22 → 0.87 / 0.57 ms. On the frozen same frame, fast vs high: 899k vs 1103k triangles, GPU 1.40 vs 1.53 ms (the RX 5700 XT is not fill-bound, so the iGPU gains from the bandwidth cuts are larger than these numbers show).
- For everyone (same picture):
  - Map meshes (terrain, merged props, grass) are cut into cells at load (`src/render/stripMesh.ts`): triangles are reordered by column (12 m cells on the field, 48 m outside), the field and the out-of-bounds north/south bands are separate meshes sharing the vertex buffers, and before each draw the draw range is trimmed to the first..last cell inside that view's frustum. Each split view used to draw the whole 146k-triangle terrain and 146k-triangle props mesh.
  - The sky dome is skipped (and the clear colour is the sky's horizon colour, which is what it drew below the horizon) when no corner of the view looks above the horizon, which is every gameplay camera.
  - When the scene target is the window size and there's no VI blur, the colour pass draws straight to the screen instead of through a second full-screen copy.
  - Wren's volley rain arrows are one instanced batch (one merged vertex-coloured mesh) instead of four meshes each; a volley used to add ~100 draws per view.
  - Hero panel HUD bitmaps redraw when the Z meter or XP ring moves by 1/240 of a turn instead of on every meter tick.
- FAST graphics (Options → GRAPHICS: AUTO / HIGH / FAST; `?quality=low` or `?quality=high` overrides). AUTO picks FAST when the WebGL renderer string is an APU/iGPU (Vega 3–11, "Radeon Graphics"/"Radeon 7xxM Graphics", Intel except Arc, Mali/Adreno/PowerVR, software), or when in 3–4 views more than a quarter of frames over 4 s take longer than 20 ms. FAST:
  - Scene target is 8-bit sRGB instead of half float (half the colour bandwidth; values above 1 now clip before the grade instead of after, only visible on the brightest additive flashes).
  - With 2+ views, heroes and soldiers draw a meshoptimizer-simplified index buffer (`src/render/lod.ts`, ~30% of the triangles for heroes, 40% for soldiers, max 1.2% shape error, computed the first time it's needed and shared with the silhouettes and the instanced soldier batches).
  - In 3–4 views the HUD canvases redraw every other frame, on the frames without a sim tick, so the two halves of the work don't stack.
  - Render height is capped at 1080 (each quadrant stays at 540 lines). Only if frames still drop in 3–4 views does it step down to 900 lines (450 per quadrant, sharp-bilinear upscaled).
- Remaining risk on the 2400G: the main thread is still ~7 ms in a busy fight on Zen 3, roughly 12 ms on Zen 1, mostly three.js per-draw overhead (~60–90 draws per view: hero parts and their silhouettes, structures with large textures, FX batches) and the 2D canvas HUD (`drawImage` of cached bitmaps and text). Next steps if it's still short: merge each hero's parts and silhouettes into one draw (needs per-part hide masks for kegs/Pip), and bake HUD numbers from per-digit glyphs.
- HD pass 2 + Colossus debris ring scale (9 Nano Banana 2×2 sheets, $1.35; prompts `tools/fx-prompts/hq_{zones,rings,bursts,warden_wisp,friar_splash,smoke}_*_prompt.txt`, raws `assets/generated/fx/hq_pass2/`, cutter `tools/fx-prompts/hq_pass2_cut.py`). The Colossus quake's flat gear/bolt ring was one 128 px cell stretched over 10.8 m (a gear bigger than a hero); `warlord.crackRing@colossus` is now a 1024 px band built from ~280 small debris pieces (cogs, nuts, bolts, springs, pipe elbows, plate scraps cut from two scatter paintings, 0.2–0.7 m each) around a faint soot ring. New HQ overrides (`assets/fx/hq/`, 512 px unless noted): `engineer.gear@calliope` (pinwheel), `raider.smokeRing@sporeblight`, `wren.spiral` (+starfall), `zone.bramble` (+suntotem, 1024, the snare ring), and the big bursts/clouds `common.burst/smoke/splash/shock`, `warlord.rage`/`impact` (+colossus), `summoner.burst` (+shadowplay), `warden.wisp`/`natureBurst` (+suntotem), `friar.blast` (+celadon), `friar.splash` (+celadon), `raider.smoke` (+sporeblight). Engine: `hdAlias(tex, atlas, id)` registers composites/common cells (an id counts if the base or any `@costume` file exists); `emit()` now resolves `hd()` so big particles use the HQ texture; `hd()` follows costume swaps (`setCostumeSwap`) before the HQ lookup. Zone fissures (`zoneFissures`) now use the costume skin, so Colossus sinkhole/lava zones get iron plates and magenta seams and Sporeblight craters mossy cracks. Finding: the hazard `ZONE_DECAL` discs (sinkhole/crater/lava/bones/tesla/grove) and the bramble zone disc have been invisible since the 3D-fissure change, so their 1024 px repaints (+colossus/sporeblight/calliope/shadowplay/suntotem) are kept in `assets/generated/fx/hq_zones/` rather than shipped; turning those decals back on is a separate call. Left at 128 px: bursts under ~3 m or ≤0.25 s (FX.burst2, DUELIST.clash, HERALD), the map lantern circle (2.6 m), Wren mark ring (2.2 m).
- Map culling (stripMesh) and the sky skip from the perf pass are disabled: the strip split broke the map previews (missing terrain/trees) and they weren't worth the risk. Stig's Repair no longer buffs soldiers' damage (he heals them, he doesn't sharpen them). Belly Flop icon repainted (clothed friar mid-leap). Maddock modest buffs: Plenty champion regen 0.6→0.8%/s, Healing Keg cooldown 10→9 s, Brewfest heal 24→28/s (sim vs 7 heroes, crossing+ruins, 84 games: 54% → 55%).
- Menu/split slowdown found (real headed Chrome, DPR 2): the full UI canvas scale cap had been raised to 8× along with the text layer, so at 1440p/DPR 2 the whole menu canvas was ~3800×1900 and redrawn every frame (20 fps). The UI canvas is back to a 4× cap; only the sharp text layer keeps the 8× cap. Menus, match and 4p split are 60 fps again.
- Playtest fixes: split-screen merging removed (each local player always has their own view); split dividers are a 1 px line; only the player who paused can drive the pause menu (others' pads and mouse ignored there); a human's select card starts empty ("PICK A CHAMPION", no model) until they place their seal, but returning from a match (results or pause → champion) keeps everyone's picked champion and costume sealed; only a card's owner can open its name entry or flip its camera button (local and online); the ready banner and start are blocked while anyone is signing a name. Menu FPS: entering a match from any menu now reallocates the UI canvases and re-fits the render targets (the same thing a window resize did), pending a real root-cause repro.
- One render/UI pipeline (supersedes the low-res target, dither/VI filters, the two-layer UI and the FAST/AUTO graphics option):
  - 3D (`src/render/gameRenderer.ts`): every view renders straight into a half-float target at drawing-buffer size (CSS size × min(DPR, 2) × render scale; the canvas is sized in device pixels with pixel ratio 1, re-checked every frame), then one grade pass (sRGB encode, `saturation`, `vignette` from `data/render.json`) writes it to the canvas 1:1. Split screen is scissor viewports in that target. Removed: `lowResHeight`, `colorBits`, `dither`, `viBlur`, `splitNear`, the VI/de-dither pass, the 8-bit FAST target swap, `dropRes`/`watchFrames`, mesh LOD (`lod.ts`) and HUD frame-skipping. Options → Graphics is now RENDER SCALE 100% / 75% (nothing automatic; `?quality` is gone).
  - UI (`UiCanvas` in `src/ui/hud.ts`): one canvas at CSS size × DPR (capped at 2160 lines) with a context transform onto the 240-unit logical layout, so all layout code is unchanged and shapes, art and text rasterise at native resolution in paint order. The separate text canvas (`setTextLayer`/`onHiLayer`/`occlude`) is gone. `font.ts` bakes each string once at the exact device size it is drawn at (from the context transform) out of the 6× atlas. HUD `memo` panels are one canvas each at device scale (~30% less HUD CPU in 4p split, so kept).
  - Renames: `n64ui.ts` → `uiPaint.ts`, `hiImage` → `smoothImage`, `assets/fonts/n64font_hi.png/.json/.png` → `gameFont.png/.json`, `gameFont_lo.png` (tools/font-bake.py and font-hires.py still write the old names).
  - Root cause of "FPS drops to ~40 after any menu until the window is resized": Chrome permanently switches a GPU 2D canvas to software raster the first time it `fillRect`s with an image pattern (Blink's `kLargePatternDrawnToGPU` fallback). `goldArrow`, `woodDisc`, `pennant` and pole knobs did exactly that on the UI canvas in menus, and the old `realloc()` stopgap only hid it by recreating the canvas. Pattern fills now only happen inside cached bakes (`bakedPlate`/`texturedRect`), which are CPU canvases (`cacheCanvas()` in `src/ui/cacheCanvas.ts`, used for all static cached art and canvas textures). Per-frame canvases (portrait live previews, memo panels) stay GPU-backed so updating them never reads back. The `pixel.realloc()`/`view.refreshTargets()` stopgap is removed.
  - Headed Chrome, 2506×1259 CSS at DPR 2 (3D 5012×2518, UI 4299×2160): title, champion select, field select, map → match, match, pause, unpause and 4p split all 59–60 fps (field select and pause were 7–9 fps before the pattern fix at this resolution; pause was 53 fps on the old pipeline).
- Balance: Maddock always self-heals (Plenty selfRegen 1.2%/s); his ale puddle poisons enemies who touch it (18 dps for 3 s) and allies keep healing for 3 s after leaving it (in-zone healing unchanged); Powder Keg cooldown 11→6 s; Brewfest reworked into a fight-starter: the slam heals nearby allies 140 and frees them from stuns/slows, blasts nearby foes (60, knockback, 0.6 s stun); the zone heals 36/s, +20% damage and +15% speed for allies, foes slowed 35% and take 15% more damage. Warlord's second combo hit is a 260° 3.4 m swipe hitting everyone in it. Home sanctuary: a hero inside its own castle walls with no enemy hero/soldier inside them heals to full in 3 s. Determinism baseline rewritten for these gameplay changes.
- Warlord buff (easy attacking tank): health high, combo 58/88 and faster first swing, slam 75 dmg / 7 s and now stuns 0.5 s, +25% vs heroes. New hidden combo HEAVE (replaces Crack Charge page): L+A next to a stunned enemy hero/soldier grabs and hurls them 10 m toward the stick; heroes fly an arc and crash for 90 + 1 s stun + splash, soldiers get launched. Bots heave stunned heroes toward their own core. Removed the 3D crest/gear decal from Warlord's challenge/war cry FX. Maddock: speed 0.94→0.82, Powder Keg 110→85 (130 vs buildings); Plenty/self regen unchanged (sim ~70%).
- Duelist structure damage ×0.55 (hook structureMul) so his lunge/flurry/roll chain stays but solo base sneaks hurt less; 2v2 sim 58% → 54%, structure damage per game 2.4k → 1.3k.

## Bot expert techniques (power-ceiling research)
Per hero: what a top player would exploit, taught to the CPUs (`src/sim/bot/tactics.ts`, hooked from `bot/think.ts`), measured with skill-0.8 bots on crossing+ruins: 1v1 vs each other hero both sides (28 games) and a 16-game 2v2 set, same seeds before/after. Techniques apply to every bot of that hero, mirrors included. No hero numbers changed; noise is roughly ±10-15%.
- Shared: bots can now hold a button to charge (`Bot.wantCharge`): `cmd.charging` while held (so they slow to 45% like a human) and the release carries the real hold time as `cmd.charge` (0.2 s tap threshold, full at 1.0 s, same as the pad).
- Wren: fully charged power shots released the instant the 1.4 s reload ends, standing still for Vantage, ~12 m out (stepping onto ground 1 m above the target when one is within a few steps); only a melee hero inside 4.5 m stops the charge. Pip sent pre-emptively at melee divers at ~7 m, SKYSHOT when they reach her, TALON RAKE if they catch her again; Pip at channels (recall/relic/horn) out of arrow range; Heartseeker held for a kill, two champions in line, or a channel. 1v1 32% → 79% (K/D 3.0/3.6 → 7.1/1.0, champion damage 3.8k → 6.2k per game); 2v2 56% → 69%. The Pip/SKYSHOT/Heartseeker rules alone were neutral (32%); the charged shot is the whole ceiling. Why: a full power shot is `damage × power(1.8, via damageMulOf) × (0.9 + 0.7 × power)` ≈ 3.9× a tap (≈225 with Vantage, ~half a frail health bar), pierces, knocks back 7 and reaches 14-16.8 m, and the hold overlaps the reload so it costs no DPS. The power multiplier is applied twice (generic charge bonus plus the shot's own scale), likely unintended; the levers are that double dip, `pierceRange`, or the 1.4 s reload.

## Terrain anti-tiling (grass, paving)
- Grass and the cobble floor read as grids (one 128 px tile every 7 m / 4 m). Grass now uses hex-tile stochastic sampling in the terrain shader (`hexTex` in `terrainMesh.ts`: triangle grid, random rotation + offset per vertex, 3 `textureGrad` taps with rotated derivatives, luminance-weighted sharpened blend, variance-preserving), plus a slow world-space tint/brightness drift. Same texture, same painted look, no visible repeat.
- Cobbles can't be rotated (rows), so the floor is a new 1024 px tile covering 16 m (`assets/textures/paving.jpg`, ~800 unique stones) built by `tools/pavinggen.py`: one Nano Banana sheet of small painted cobbles in the old style (`assets/source/paving_ai.jpg`) cut into ~190 stone sprites and re-laid in random-height staggered rows (random sprite per stone matched by aspect, flip, slight tint). The script writes the id map directly (`paving_id.png`, same encoding as before, now mipmapped) and the cracked variant (`paving_crack.jpg`). Every map's paving now gets per-stone tint/brightness from the id map and a macro colour drift; ruins keeps its weathering on top. `cobble_id/cobble_crack.png` removed (`phototex.py --paving` is obsolete); `cobble.png` stays for jump pads and horns. Cost: +3 texture taps on grass pixels, +1 on paving. fal: $0.30 (2 Nano Banana; the grass one came out with big tufts and wasn't used).
- Warlord: holds B through his swings (A swings are actions, so the hold costs no speed) and lets the slam go at full power (×1.6, 75→120) once the target is in reach; HEAVE now throws toward a friendly tower within 13 m of the victim, else toward the core (from the Warlord's position, since the throw lands 10 m along the stick). 1v1 57% → 54% (neutral, champion damage 2.9k → 3.2k), 2v2 63% → 81% (champion damage 10.6k → 13.2k, team kills 18 → 22). Tried and dropped: holding Quake for a slowed/stunned target (×1.3, +0.6 s stun) cost too many Quakes, 1v1 fell to 36-46%. Read: no dangerous ceiling; the charged slam is a flat +60% on a 7 s cooldown that every B gets (see the shared charge note) and is the lever if needed (`update.ts` B power 0.6).
- Stig: Repair doubles as a 6 m blast (60 to every foe), so he now holds B in fights and releases it at full power (96) once a champion is inside ~4.5 m, healing the towers/troops around him at the same time; with his 60% upgrade price he upgrades every building as soon as it stands (cost + 40 gold) and specialises towers without the generic 150 s / 420 gold gate. 1v1 43% → 68% (K/D 2.0/5.6 → 4.0/3.2, champion damage 1.6k → 2.5k; charged Repair alone 61%), 2v2 44% → 50%. Read: moderate ceiling, mostly from using Repair as a nuke; levers are Repair `damage` (60) / `radius` (6) and the shared B charge (×1.6).
- Grim: holds B through his (very fast) combo and leaps at full power (70 → 112, ×1.25 damage tier, plus ×2 from Smoke / ×1.5 from behind) whenever the target is inside 8 m; Execute Dash goes out immediately from Smoke (the ambush ×2 lands on a 180 hit), on a target below 40% or for a kill, else as before. 1v1 36% → 57% (K/D 2.6/4.8 → 4.6/3.8, champion damage 3.0k → 5.2k), 2v2 19% → 50% (champion damage 8.6k → 16.4k). Tried and dropped: keeping Z only for execute/ambush (Z use fell from 11.7 to 3.8 per game, 1v1 32%). Read: real ceiling: a charged Leap from Smoke and behind is 112×1.25×2×1.5 ≈ 420, then a 180-252 dash, so a frail/low champion dies from stealth in two buttons. Levers: `ambushMul` (2.0), `flankMul` (1.5), the shared B charge (×1.6).
- Remnil: holds Hex to full power (80 → 128 before her ×0.8 tier) and steps in to drop it from just inside 8 m, otherwise kites at ~10.5 m (her bolts reach 13; the default kept her at 8). 1v1 25% → 36% (within noise; champion damage 2.7k → 2.9k), 2v2 38% → 38%. Tried and dropped: leading the hex by the target's velocity × the 0.9 s delay (2v2 fell to 6%). Read: no hidden ceiling found in her own kit: the hex's 0.9 s delay makes it easy to walk out of, and HEX SWAP keeps the distance between the two (it only swaps places), so it isn't an escape from a melee diver. Her power stays in army/buildings.
- Francois: parry reads checked every tick with a human-like 0.15 s reaction (a champion's swing that started ≥ 0.15 s ago and lands within 0.35 s; one 0.9×skill roll per swing, `duelistReflex`), replacing the generic "parry when they're doing anything" hint; Lunge held to full power (70 → 112, ×1.25 tier, crit 25%); Blade Flurry fired at once on a stunned champion (×1.4, e.g. after a parry). 1v1 43% → 64% (clean parries 2 → 5 per game, K/D 3.0/4.9 → 4.8/3.5, champion damage 3.1k → 4.3k), 2v2 69% → 56% (champion damage 14.2k → 15.9k; the drop is two games, within noise). Read: moderate ceiling, mostly in parry timing: each clean parry is 85 + 0.8 s stun + a free Lunge with ×1.5 on the next hit. Levers: `r.counter` / `stunSeconds` / `openingMul`, parry `window` (0.6 s), or B cooldown reset on parry.
- Thorn: holds Long Arm Slap during the melee exchange and lets it go at full power (70 → 112) while the target is still inside 8.5 m. 1v1 21% → 29%, 2v2 69% → 56% (champion damage 2.5k → 2.7k and 9.5k → 11.7k; 17/44 games won before and after, so neutral overall). Tried and dropped: holding the slap from range (slowed his approach: 25% / 50%) and dropping Stone Wall 2.5 m behind hurt or retreating champions to cut their escape (18% / 31%: the defensive wall is worth more). Read: no hidden ceiling; his 1v1 weakness is the issue rather than an exploit.
- Maddock: retreating with a champion within 5 m he throws the Healing Keg at his own feet (110 heal + puddle) and KEG ROCKETs out of the puddle toward home (cc-immune 10 m roll); Powder Keg (1.35 s from throw to blast) only on a champion that is stunned, slowed, mid-swing or within 2.5 m, placed on it, else on a clump of 3+ soldiers, replacing the generic "throw at whoever is in range" rule. 1v1 43% → 50%, 2v2 44% → 44% (Keg Rockets 0.5 → 1.1 and 0.9 → 3.2 per game, deaths 3.4 → 3.2 and 5.4 → 4.8, champion damage +4% / +20%). `inOwnPuddle` is now exported from `hero/friar.ts` for the bot. Read: no dangerous ceiling; the keg rocket escape makes him harder to finish but his damage stays low.
- Wren power shot bug: the charge bonus was applied twice (hero-wide action power ×1.8 in damageMulOf AND the shot's own 0.9+0.7·power), ~3.9× a normal arrow. Now the shot only adds a ×1.15 premium on top of the shared charge bonus (full power ≈ 2.1×). Expert-bot 1v1: 79% → 46%.
- Remnil buffs: Hex lands after 0.55 s (was 0.9) and roots for 0.6 s before its slow; Raise an Army erupts around him (7 m: 70 damage, 0.8 s stun, knockback). Expert-bot sims: 1v1 21% → 50%, 2v2 38% → 56%.
- Shove: knockback 15→19; breaking a block shows GUARD BROKEN!; a shoved target stopped by a wall/cliff takes 30 and double stun (WALL SPLAT!); pits (ruins) stay solid to walking but anything knocked into one falls in and dies (credited to the last attacker); codex page THE SHOVE; a local button hint 'L+A · BREAK GUARD' floats over enemy champions blocking next to you.
- Bellwick Gardens shrunk 104x104 → 72x72 (−31% each way): tools/mapgen-gardens.py re-authored at the smaller scale (15x15 keep yards, shorter avenue and roundabout, smaller parterres, border gates and court gates moved, pads/jump pads/patrol/den relocated), grid re-baked and the map GLB rebuilt in Blender so props/hedges match. FFA bot check: heroes meet ~40 s in and trade about twice as many hits as before.
- Gardens: removed the two pads flanking each roundabout (8 neutral pads gone; each quarter keeps 3 home pads + the roundabout pad + its two border pads). Gates: set a now shuts both border lanes between neighbours (you must cross through the court), set b shuts only the court.
- Snow anti-tiling (Frostcross / alpine): snow replaces every grass vertex on alpine maps but was a plain 64 px textureGrad (one tile per 6 m, strong diagonal strokes) and read as a grid. It now goes through `hexTex` with a slow cool/warm + brightness drift (`SNOW_DIRT` in `terrainMesh.ts`, alpine only; dirt is skipped where snow is full). The grass branch never runs on alpine maps, so the cost equals the grass path. `assets/textures/snow.png` repainted (Nano Banana Pro from the old one, 256 px, quadrant-averaged seamless, mean colour matched); the avalanche blanket uses it too.
- New 1v1/2v2 map **Russet Hollow** (`data/maps/hollow.json`, generated by `tools/mapgen-hollow.py`; seed 41baa37d…, which picked: autumn cider orchard at dusk, eruption hazards, hourglass shape). 88x44, mirrored left/right, pinched by hedgerowed orchard hillsides to one 24 m neck at the cider press where the Grudge sits. Each side: walled keep, a leaf-strewn cart lane, two raised orchard terraces (ramp near the keep, a bank you can drop off but not climb at the neck), tall windfall grass, 7 pads. Map event **cider wells** (`src/sim/mapEvents/geysers.ts`, render `src/render/map/geysers.ts`): 3 wells per side in two groups (lane / terraces) that alternate every 15 s, bubble for 4 s, then erupt: heroes on a well ride a jump arc to its fixed landing point (lane wells throw you past the press into enemy ground, terrace wells to the other terrace), soldiers are blasted aside, everyone takes 30, and the well leaves sticky cider mud (slow 55%, 6 s). Bots step onto a bubbling well when its landing is closer to the enemy keep. New theme: per-map ground palette (`palette: "autumn"` → painted autumn grass / leaf-litter path / russet clay bank textures, `assets/textures/autumn_*`), per-map `atmosphere` (dusk sun/sky/fog/ambient overrides applied in GameRenderer.applyAtmosphere, falling leaves in Effects), 6 Tripo props (apple tree, cider press, cider well, barrel stack, pumpkins, hay cart) and autumn canopy/conifer/straw materials in build_map.py. New "apple" emblem; codex page + mechanic page. Bot check (8 games): wells fire 36–84 times a game and heroes ride them 5–11 times.
- Map previews (field select, codex) now include each map's runtime set pieces (jump pads, timed gates) by building a throwaway MapFx per map. Long field blurbs scroll. Champion select restores each human's last sealed pick however you return to it (results, pause → champion, leaving and coming back).
- New FFA map **Emberdune Spires** (`data/maps/spires.json`, `tools/mapgen-spires.py`; seeds 7bf7713c… → desert canyon sandstone ruins, a giant roaming creature, spiral arms, 72x72; 2cfb954b… → name, a burrowing dune serpent, blue moonlit night, speed 5, breach every 20 s). Four-fold rotation (quad), so it's a pinwheel: a curved sandstone cliff blade from each quadrant's north edge bends the keep's east road in toward the centre, with one narrow pass through each blade; every road ends at a sunken ring of dune sand (r 9-19) around a raised stone island holding the Grudge. 5 pads per house (3 home, 1 forward, 1 on the dune rim). Map event **dune serpent** (`src/sim/mapEvents/serpent.ts`, render `src/render/map/serpent.ts`): a deterministic moving hazard (no entity) that swims the dune ring (churning sand slows anything near it), every 20 s stops, rumbles 2.5 s and breaches (120 damage + knockback + 0.7 s stun to champions, swallows soldiers), and hunts the Grudge carrier while they're in the sand (4.3 m/s, breaches under them 1 s after catching up, 6 s cooldown). Bots step out of the rumble ring. Art: desert palette (painted hardpan floor / dune sand / banded sandstone cliff, `assets/textures/desert_*`), moonlit atmosphere with blowing sand motes, 6 Tripo props (sandstone spire, serpent ribcage, buried colossus head, obelisk, nomad tent, cactus clump) placed on the field and as surround trees, sandstone-tinted surround ruins, pale desert scrub, and a Tripo serpent (`assets/props/serpent.glb`) that bursts out of the sand on breach. New "serpent" emblem, codex page and mechanic page. Also fixed: FFA pause/results windows now use the whole-field overview camera.
- Emberdune Spires polish: keep yards and the centre island use warm sandstone flags (desert palette `cobble`), the spires are graded toward the palette at build time (TRIPO `grade`), the night ambient is warmer so shadowed sand doesn't read blue. Field-select previews are lit with each map's own atmosphere (`MapLight` in `src/ui/portraits.ts`), so night and dusk maps look it.
- Menu hitches when changing fields/modes: the backdrop swap built a new World and its views from cold. Fixes: terrain shape ops only visit their bounding box (World build ~5-10x faster, identical heights); lockdown barricades cached per layout; structure batches pooled across worlds; skinned heroes get a shared bind-pose culling sphere (three.js was CPU-skinning every vertex to compute one); field card thumbs pre-shot at one fixed size and cropped; pad counts primed at load; and `App.rehearse()` shows every field once behind the boot overlay (all champions cycled) so shader compiles and GPU uploads happen at load.
- Sound pass 1 (see `docs/audio-catalog.md`): 3D sound - every live view is a listener (`GameRenderer.listeners()`), sounds fade out past about 1.35x a view's half width, pan by where they land in that view's slice of the screen, and are muffled off-screen; a local champion's own sounds never drop below a floor; map-wide events are global (`src/audio/spatial.ts`). Sound effects are now CC0 samples: `tools/sfx/fetch.sh` downloads the libraries (Kenney, OpenGameArt packs, BigSoundBank), `tools/sfx/manifest.py` lists every id and how it's cut, `tools/sfx/build.py` processes them into `assets/sfx/<id>.<n>.ogg` (~880 files, 15 MB, decoded after the first input). `src/audio/events.ts` has a recipe per sim event (attacker weapon x victim body for hits, per-champion voices cast from shared voice packs by pitch, signature ability layers); `src/audio/tracker.ts` watches the world for statuses, champion-only faint ducked footsteps per surface and gait, and map ambience (beds, torch/fountain/well/serpent loops, critters). Announcer lines for FIGHT, winner/tie and sudden death.
- Jump pads need a held A (cmd.charging "a") while standing on them, so they're never taken by accident; the A's release after the jump is swallowed (`hero.padHold`), bots hold A on the pad they're heading for, and a "HOLD A · JUMP" hint floats over local humans on a ready pad. Recall is 4 s (was 6) and you can walk at 35% speed while it channels; attacks/abilities/dodge/hits still break it. Bellwick's court jump pads moved out from the fountain rim (r 5.4 -> 6.9). Map-select thumbnails re-shoot until the set pieces' textures have loaded (jump pads and gates were black).
- Select polish: "THE GRUDGE IS SWORN!" plays a short drum + two-note sting when it appears, and the seals draw over it (so they can still be picked up); the seat card's seal moved to the portrait's bottom-right with no "SWORN" label. Pins mean something now: a champion card's pin has one wedge per seat whose seal is on it (seal colours, CPUs grey), else the colours of the hands pointing at it; seat card pins are the seat's seal colour; field card pins show the hovering hands. Online field select is a vote: every human seat (host pads and guests) presses A on a field (pin wedges show the votes); it settles when all have voted or 5 s after the first vote, most votes winning and ties drawn by lot (`fieldVoting` in `src/app/select.ts`, guest "vote" message, votes + seconds left ride the host's presence).
- **Team deathmatch** (mode "tdm", 4v4): `src/sim/tdm.ts` + `tdmMap()` turn a 2-house map into an arena with no keeps, tower pads, outposts, army, gold, shop, recall or opening lockdown (jump pads and map events stay). First house to `rules.killLimit` (10/20/30/50, default 20) champion kills wins; regulation time = match length, then sudden death (next kill wins). Respawn after 2 s at a random reachable spot >= 14 m from living enemies, 1.5 s invulnerable. The Grudge buffs its carrier (x1.3 damage, x0.8 taken, x1.1 speed, 6 hp/s) who can still fight; it only drops on death. 10 power-up spots (farthest-point sampled): potion (45% heal), might (x1.4 dmg 10 s), haste (x1.35 speed 10 s), shield (120); 25 s respawn. Chaos every 50 s (first at 45): cannon barrage, the ogre, blood moon (x1.5 damage 15 s, red light), potion rain, swift winds. TDM bots roam to potions/power-ups and hunt the nearest enemy / carrier. Multipliers live in `Status.power*` (undefined outside TDM, so the base modes' determinism is unchanged). Client: MAX_PLAYERS 8 / MAX_LOCAL 4; P5-P8 colours purple, orange, teal, hot pink (seals/tags in `assets/ui/chip_5-8`, `tag_5-8`); champion select shows eight compact seat cards (blue 2x2 left, red 2x2 right; local pads 1-4 split 2 per house), mode ribbon cycles 1v1 -> 2v2 -> FFA -> TDM; HUD head shows kills / limit. TDM fields are the 2-house maps for now (their baked tower-pad slabs still show) until dedicated multi-level TDM maps are made.
- TDM follow-ups: 8 pad/cursor slots (a local pad can SIT HERE in seats 5-8); compact cards show the live champion stage and clickable costume icons (SIT HERE appears when a hand is over a CPU card). Summoned soldiers in TDM hunt the enemy champion nearest their summoner (else stay at its side). Remnil's Gravewalk in TDM walks to a ready power-up (aim picks which; quick press the nearest) and raises 2 skeletons on arrival (+ the grave-rank talent) since tower/outpost grave boosts have nothing to boost. TDM bots: personalities by champion (brawler Warlord/Thorn/Stig, flanker Grim, hunter Francois, skirmisher Wren/Remnil, support Maddock/Herald), approach targets from their own side, steer around friends and sidestep when jammed. Grudge carrier buff toned down (x1.2 dmg, x0.9 taken, x1.05 speed, 3 hp/s): bot matches went from 20-3 blowouts to mostly 20-12..20-16.
- TDM scoreboards: results show kills / deaths / Grudge held (s) / power-ups per house and a kills-sorted card per champion with K / D; pause shows each house's champions (K / D, HP) and kills vs limit + Grudge time (`Tdm.held`, `Tdm.taken`). Result pins use each seat's chip colour and the winner seal its house colour. Online, only the host leaves the results screen (guests see "WAITING FOR THE HOST" and follow the host back to the lobby) - a guest pressing A used to drop to the lobby on its own while the host was still on results. A dodge roll now shakes Wren's Pip off (1v1 sims: Wren 59% overall, 2/8 vs Grim).
- FFA: a fallen house's players can switch who they spectate (left / right or A cycles the standing champions, followed through respawns). FFA construction never stalls: a site always builds at least at one hero's pace without anyone beside it. Super meter: a super's own hits no longer refill it (no chaining Z off a crowd), and the Warlord charges at x0.9 (hooks.superGainMul); 1v1 sims Warlord 53% (was 65%). Control towers a bit stronger: hp 950 (850), damage 18 (14), cooldown 2.1 (2.4), range x0.78 (0.72), vs heroes 0.95 (0.8).
- Crossing's forward pads moved in from z 12.5 / 35.5 to 16 / 32 (x 27.5, mirrored), flanking the middle lane 8 m either side so both towers reach its centre (first try at 21.5 / 26.5 was far too close). Soldier AI: buildings hit in the last 2.5 s call up their nearest soldiers within 40 m onto the attacker (3 per tower, 6 for the keep, +2 when the attacker is a champion under 40% hp), regardless of orders (`defenderTarget`, `World.defense`); and target scoring prefers hurt champions and champions raiding our buildings.
- Wren: a charged A released with the stick held is aimed by hand (only targets within ~20 degrees of the stick get the homing/snap; uncharged shots keep the wide assist). Volley reach 11 (was 9), wave damage 29 (33) to compensate. Pip can only be rolled off after 1 s latched (`pipShakeAfter`). 1v1 sims: Wren 63% vs warlord/raider/duelist/friar/engineer, 4/8 vs Grim.
- **FFA deathmatch** (mode "ffadm"): the deathmatch rules with eight houses of one (`tdmMap(map, 8)`), on any field; houses without a map spawn start at a random safe spot; first champion to the kill limit wins, regulation ends on the unique leader or goes to sudden death (a kill that puts someone strictly ahead wins). Eight house colours/names everywhere (purple, orange, teal, pink after blue/red/yellow/green; the ogre's team moved from 4 to 8). HUD standings show kills; pause shows all eight champions ranked; results place houses by kills. Mode ribbon cycles 1v1 -> 2v2 -> FFA -> TDM -> FFA DM. Also: the big seat card's sworn seal sits lower (frame's bottom edge) so it no longer covers the 5th costume icon.
- Bot fight sense: `situation()` scores the local fight (12 m: champions by hp, soldiers by rank, towers covering) into an edge -1..1 - advantage (> 0.25), neutral, disadvantage (< -0.25). Retreat threshold shifts with it (ahead: plan retreatHp - 0.15, min 12%; behind: + 0.12), bots don't leave when the nearby enemy champion is in worse shape and we're not behind, and healing stops early once nothing threatens (55%) or the fight turns our way (45%) instead of always walking to 80%. Hurt dives under an enemy tower back out of its range (not home), and only when not clearly ahead and the target isn't nearly dead; hit-and-run "outmatched" disengages only when not ahead. Lower retreat floors (Grim 0.35, Wren 0.32, Maddock 0.3) and Maddock fights at brawl range (botRange 2.4, was 4.5 so he kited like an archer). Sims: "moving away from a weaker enemy" 14% -> 8.6% of close-quarters time, time spent healing 7.1% -> 5.7%, hero kills/match 8.8 -> 10.7.
- Army orders: CPU commanders/support bots only call the whole army back when a building is really losing its fight (`realThreat`: attackers within 13 m outweigh its defence x1.3, keep or tower < 60%), never while every enemy champion is dead (they push instead, `foesDown`), and leave a human's orders alone for 90 s (was 25). Bot recalls use the same test. 2v2 bot sims: armies on defend 26.6% -> 2% of the time. DEFEND now marches to whichever building was hit in the last 4 s (anchored there, leash 11), else the gate posts; soldiers always fight back against whatever hit them in the last 2 s or is in weapon reach, whatever their orders/leash (a fighting retreat).
- Grain economy: outposts no longer pay a share of their own upkeep as grain income (outpostShare 0.85 -> 0 - it was paid even with the army at cap, so grain piled into the thousands by minute 4-6); base 2.0/s (1.6), per built structure 0.8 / 1.2 / 1.6 by level (0.7 / 1.1 / 1.5); FFA grain x0.7 (`ffa.grainMul`). Sims: 1v1/2v2 banks stay ~0-600 through 6 min (was 1.5-5k), running every outpost flat out costs ~2x the income so production choices matter until late; FFA under ~1k until 8 min (was 5-6k). Sudden death is reached in most bot games (14/18 over 1v1, 2v2, FFA) - bots finish keeps slowly.
- Army upkeep: every living soldier eats grain each second (grunt 0.5, archer 0.65, brute 1.3; summoned/raised soldiers free), drained from the store (never below 0). With the store empty outposts can't pay for new soldiers, so army size settles at what your buildings feed: losing buildings (or your champion being down, which halts outposts) shrinks it. HUD grain rate is net of upkeep (red when negative). Sims: armies mostly 4-12 mid-game with occasional 16s, grain banks near 0 while fighting.
- Converting leads (1v1/2v2 bot sims: sudden death 16/16 -> 5/16, every game now ends on a keep kill, 2.7-7 min). Causes found: bot target picking skipped warded keeps (keeps start warded, so heroes never hit one outside sudden death), bots spent 20-37% of their time babysitting builds and walked home to shop mid-push, and nothing told them to cash in a lead. Fixes: `pressing()` (enemy champions all down, more champions standing, army +4 / >= 6, or +2 buildings with an even army) drives a bot `siege` branch (break the nearest enemy building no farther from their keep than us, then the keep; waits for soldiers unless the enemies are dead; hysteresis via `Bot.sieging`) and the push directive; bots stop tending builds and skip shop trips (> 26 m from home) while pressing; healing threshold drops to 15% while every enemy champion is dead. Unattended construction creeps at 0.3x (`builderRates.unattended`). Catch-up cut hard (scale 600, structure weight 0.2, income +30%, production -20%, fortify 15%, respawn cut 15%). Grain per pad by zone (`grain.zoneMul` home 1 / forward 1.8 / neutral 2.2), base 3/s, upkeep grunt 0.35 / archer 0.45 / brute 0.9: building lead -> army lead (e.g. 6-9 vs 1-4). Respawn grows 1 s per minute (6 -> max 13, x1.667 in 2v2). Keep bulwark: keeps take 35% damage at 0:00 easing to 100% at 5:00 (not in sudden death), so early stomps take towers first. Keep landing (`respawnSlam`): respawning with enemy champions in your base (or within 10 m of the keep) throws them out through the nearest gate to 4 m beyond it (0.7 s arc, 40 dmg, slow), knocks enemy soldiers back. Brewfest nerf: 6 s, cask 300 hp (Grand Cask +2 s / +150).
- Online champion select: guests may pick up, hover and place CPU chips like the host ("cpu" messages: hover sets the CPU's champion, ready false/true lifts/places the chip; host keeps the chip off the board while it's in a guest's hand and puts it back down on its champion if nobody holds it for 1.5 s, e.g. the guest left). While holding a CPU's chip, the C-stick (and the costume icons) dress that CPU instead of your own champion ("cpucos"; locally too). Mirrored hands carry an 8th field, the seat whose costume strip they have open, and the select cards treat ghost hands like local ones: hover highlights, the see-through champion preview while a held chip hovers a card, and the costume strip. `window.grudge` gained netHost/netJoin/setMode/seatCpu for online playtests.
- Respawn: base x big-match multiplier + 0.75 s per minute, capped at 10 s (1v1 6 -> 10 by ~5:20; 2v2 starts at 10). Respawning no longer punishes abilities: every cooldown comes back ready (the respawn cooldown penalty and frozen cooldowns are gone); only a half-charged super meter is still lost on death. Sims (1v1/2v2, 16 games): sudden death 1/8 in 1v1, 5/8 in 2v2 (close games mostly).
- Champion kills muster the army (`economy.muster`): the killers get 100 grain (less the victim's catch-up bounty cut) and every outpost of theirs sends a burst of 2 quick soldiers 0.6 s apart (paid from grain, pop cap applies), so the army is up and moving by the time the victim respawns. FFA: champions already dead when their keep falls stay dead (eliminate() skipped dead heroes, whose respawn timers kept running), and an eliminated house never respawns. Bots: every enemy champion dead -> siege runs before fighting soldiers; pressing also on a 4+ kill lead backed by buildings. Sims (16): sudden death 1/8 in 1v1, 4/8 in 2v2 (all close games, kills within 1-7); armies avg 6-9.
- Deathmatch fixes: power-up spots and respawn spots were sampled "reachable from the map centre"; on Gardens the centre is the walled court only jump pads reach, so every power-up sat in the court and every respawn failed over to the centre. Both now sample from a map spawn (respawns: nearest enemy >= 14 m in 8-bot FFA DM sims, was median 7 m on Gardens). Rest regen: 2% max hp/s after 5 s out of combat with no enemy champion within 10 m (home/tower turf keeps its own regen). Warlord War Cry: +15% damage / speed to him and allies in 10 m for 6 s, a 10% max-hp shield, and foes within 5 m are cowed 2.5 s and slowed 30% for 2 s (bot hint "roar": enemy champion in range or an army brawl); 1v1 sims 50% -> 55%. Maddock's Brewfest bot hint also "roar" (it needed 3 soldiers nearby, so CPUs never used it in deathmatch). Ability audit for deathmatch: everything else works without soldiers/buildings (Stig's Repair pulse damages/knocks back and CPUs use it as a nuke; Remnil gravewalks to power-ups).
- Deathmatch: CPUs no longer park on potions at full health (a potion can't be drunk at full hp, so they stood on it in a corner until someone came by; idle time in 8-bot FFA DM sims 10% -> 1%). The Grudge can only be carried 15 s (`tdm.relicCarrySeconds`): then it slips back to the middle asleep for 8 s, and the last carrier can't retake it for 10 s after it wakes. FFA DM sims (8 games, 4 maps): Francois 4 wins, Grim 2, Warlord 1, Stig 1; Maddock weakest (K/D 3.4 / 10.9).
- FFA deathmatch-only champion tuning (`tdm.ffaHeroMods`, applied by World.dmMod at spawn and morph): Maddock hp x1.15 / speed x1.25 / damage x1.45 (slow healer with no partner to heal), Wren hp x1.3 / speed x1.15 / damage x1.1, Remnil hp x1.12 / damage x1.2, Thorn speed x1.06 / damage x1.1, Stig hp x1.1, and the runaway kill-race leaders slightly down: Grim damage x0.9, Francois x0.92. 12-game 8-bot sims after: kills/game 7-16 (was 3.4-17), wins Warlord 4, Grim 4, Stig 2, Francois 1, Thorn 1. Jump pad arrows (and tips) go grey while a pad is cooling down or sealed.
- FFA DM tuning, 52-game sims each round. Round 1 (Wren hp 1.4 / speed 1.18 / dmg 1.2, Remnil 1.2 / 1.05 / 1.3, Maddock 1.2 / 1.28 / 1.55): wins Grim 17, Warlord 13, Francois 8, Thorn 7, Stig 3, Remnil 2, Maddock 2, Wren 0. Round 2 (Wren dmg 1.35, Remnil 1.4, Maddock 1.65, Stig hp 1.2 dmg 1.08, Grim dmg 0.82, Warlord 0.92): Warlord 12, Francois 9, Remnil 8, Grim 7, Thorn 6, Stig 5, Wren 3, Maddock 2 (fair share 6.5).
- Online seating: a joining guest takes an open seat, else an automatic CPU's, else any CPU's (a human always beats a CPU, even one added on purpose - a lobby full of CPUs used to be unjoinable); deathmatch modes search all 8 seats (FFA DM only looked at 4). Guests can SIT HERE on any CPU card ("take", moveRemoteSeat accepts any CPU seat). The host gets the X (open this seat) on compact 8-seat CPU cards too.
- War Cry's shield / cowing / slow and the stronger +15% buff are deathmatch-only (ability `dm` overrides merged in fireWarcry when World.tdm); elsewhere it's back to +12% damage / speed for allies in 10 m. Rest regen (out-of-combat heal anywhere) is deathmatch-only too. They had leaked into 1v1/2v2: 280+150-game sims showed sudden death back up to 34% (1v1) / 63% (2v2) and Warlord teams at 74-78% in 2v2. Bot "roar" hint: enemy champion close only in deathmatch, else the old army-brawl rule.
- Two deathmatch arenas (map `mode: "tdm"`, only offered in team / FFA deathmatch, listed before the FFA fields; deathmatch modes no longer list the 1v1/2v2 fields). Field select lays its cards out as a grid (2 columns from 5 cards, 3 from 9) and shows "DEATHMATCH ARENA" / "NO KEEPS" instead of a pad count in deathmatch.
  - **Cutstone Quarry** (`tools/mapgen-quarry.py`, 72x72, point symmetric): three levels - the lip (y 6.2, tall scrub to hide in, cranes, spawn camps), a middle bench (y 3.2) with dead-end mine adits cut into the lip, and the pit floor (y 0.2) with the crusher mound where the Grudge wakes. Haul-road ramps up (2 per level per side), drop anywhere; jump pads from the pit back to the lip. Sunset atmosphere, `palette: "quarry"` (limestone floor, gravel road, cut cliff, sawn slabs, scrub), sandstone-tinted surround ruins and scrub.
  - **Bellfry Abbey** (`tools/mapgen-abbey.py`, 68x68, point symmetric): graveyard ring (long grass, graves, yews, a blind hedge), sunken walled crypt yards with one stair (and a jump pad) out, the raised precinct with the roofless nave (altar = Grudge) and a broken cloister, and bell-tower stumps (y 4.6, one stair, long drops). Moonlit (silvery, not too blue) atmosphere, `palette: "abbey"`; hedges / canopies nudged slightly blue.
  - Art (fal, ~$4): two Nano Banana texture sheets sliced by `tools/slice-map-tex.py` (seam-cut wrap, 512 px), two prop sheets, 12 Tripo props (`tools/gen-map-art.mjs`): crane, cut-stone stack, mine cart, scaffold, rubble heap, stonecutter's shed; fallen bell, yew (mirrored canopy - Tripo gave it leaves on one side), weeping angel, gravestones, candle shrine, broken pillar.
  - Terrain shader for these palettes: paths hex-tiled with drift, palette paving tiled at its own scale (`cobbleM` 4-5 m, hex-sampled, no per-stone id tint - the abbey flags were 4 m stones at the default 16 m), a second grass in noise patches outside the field (`grass2`), natural rock instead of the abbey's built wall on cliffs outside the field (`rim`).
  - 8-bot FFA DM sims: games 4.4-5.8 min, 4 different winners in 4 games, idle < 1%.
- Deathmatch controls: the d-pad turns that player's split-view camera 45 degrees per press (eased; `GameRenderer.rotateView/viewYaw`, `placeCam` yaw, keep-in-view nudges rotated) and the stick is read relative to the turned view. C-stick flicks no longer order an army; in team deathmatch they're callouts (`Command.callout`, screen direction turned by the view yaw): the house's CPUs push to a point 18 m that way for 12 s and pick fights around it (`Tdm.callout/calloutOf`, bot tdmPrey/tdmRoam), with a "P1: PUSH EAST!" notice and a team-coloured ping ring. The order cross becomes "CALL YOUR CPUS" (hidden in FFA deathmatch, where there's nobody to call).
- Deathmatch pace: everyone starts at a random safe spot (no team camps), all champions x1.2 speed (`tdm.speedMul`, via World.dmMod), power-ups respawn 20 s and are mostly fight-changers (2 potions in 10; new **rush** hourglass: every cooldown ready + 35% super), CPUs detour for power-ups within 20 m (potions within 24 m when hurt). Maps can place power-ups (`powerups` in the map JSON). Cutstone Quarry shrunk 72 -> 56 (lip 7 m, bench 9 m, pit 24 m) with might / rush / haste on the lip and in an adit, shields on the bench, potions in the pit. 8-bot sims: Quarry time on the lip 0-13% -> 17%, power-ups taken ~20/min.
- Painted UI icons (Nano Banana Pro, ~$0.30): Remnil's R glyph in the shared ability sheet is now Gravewalk (a ghost rising from a grave toward a tower), drawn in the sheet's own style from the sheet as reference; new `assets/ui/glyphs/` (pencil, eye, dpadzoom; tinted by `uiGlyph` in selectArt.ts) replace the hand-drawn name-pencil and camera icons on the select plaques; the Grudge HUD icon is a painted golden bull's head (matching coin / grain / helmet) and the held bomb a painted iron bomb with a flickering fuse glow. Raw sheets in assets/generated/ (ui_glyphs_raw, hud_relic_bomb_raw). C-stick arrows, keep diamond and menu glyphs stay vector.
- Deathmatch healing back up: sampled power-up spots are 4 potions in 10 (was 2), Quarry places 6 potions of 14 (pit, bench), and deathmatch rest regen is 3% max hp/s after 4 s out of combat (was 2% after 5 s).
- Deathmatch HUD: under each local champion panel, lines for the power-ups at work - MIGHT / HASTE / SHIELD with what they do and seconds left (from tdm config and status), and a 2.5 s flash for instant ones (POTION · HEALED, RUSH · ABILITIES READY · SUPER CHARGED).
- Deathmatch power-ups are painted Tripo models (Nano Banana sheet assets/generated/powerups_raw.png -> assets/source/power_*_tripo.glb -> build_tripo_props.py static props assets/props/power_{potion,might,haste,shield,rush}.glb, ~900 tris, 256 px): red heart-sealed flask, rune-lit battle axe, winged golden boot, lion shield, violet hourglass. Fitted to ~0.95 m and centred on the spin axis at runtime; the vector versions stay as a fallback.
- Deathmatch: Stig's siege tower (works) has half health and his ballista 55% (`tdm.buildHpMul`).
- Maddock: pressing R again while a powder keg is out blows it at once (one still in flight blows the moment it lands, `Keg.blowOnLand`); with no keg out R throws as usual. The detonation doesn't touch the cooldown.
- CPU Maddock detonates a fizzing powder keg early (R again) when an enemy champion or 3+ enemies are in 85% of its blast (~1 in 2 kegs). Kept in every mode: 280+150-game sims after - Maddock 43% in 1v1 (50% before), Warlord+Maddock 62% / Thorn+Maddock 50% in 2v2 (64% / 48%). Same sims: 1v1 spread Grim / Remnil / Francois 63%, Wren 54%, Stig 53%, Maddock 43%, Thorn 34%, Warlord 27%; sudden death 34% (1v1) / 67% (2v2).
- Sudden death in 2v2 sims 67% -> 40% (150 games; 1v1 34% -> 10%, avg 4.3 min). Rules: (1) War drums (`suddenDeath.rampFrom` 90 s, World.surge 0->1 by the end of regulation): sudden death's production / cost / soldier-damage multipliers phase in, and the keep ward catches 80% less by the bell. (2) The keep shield (ward) only holds - and can only be bought - while every home pad of the house has one of its finished buildings (World.homeHeld); the HUD ring hides otherwise. (3) Soldiers of a house with more champions standing march x1.35 (`units.advanceSpeedMul`, World.marchMul; path movement only). (4) Grain: catch-up no longer boosts grain, and a champion death spills 35% of the house's grain (`grain.deathLoss`), so a losing house can't bank an army back. Bots know: sieges knock out the nearest enemy home building first while the keep is shielded, and after 90 s an empty own home pad is refilled before anything else (barracks, or a tower at the outpost cap). Balance after: 1v1 Remnil 69%, Wren 61%, Grim / Francois 56%, Stig 46%, Maddock 44%, Warlord 37%, Thorn 30%; 2v2 Warlord+Maddock 64%, Warlord+Wren 60%, the rest 38-48%.
- Champion classes (`heroes.json` "class": tank Thorn, bruiser Warlord, assassin Grim / Francois, marksman Wren, caster Remnil, support Maddock / Herald, builder Stig) and per-champion team synergies keyed by partner class (`synergy`). First one: Remnil's hex marks foes for his partner, +25% from champion hits, only for assassin partners (helps Grim+Remnil / Francois+Remnil, not Remnil with tanks; nothing in 1v1). Concepts for six more champions (second marksman, bruiser, tank, caster, builder, support) in docs/new-champions.md, seeded with openssl rand.
- Team synergy auras (`synergy.edge` / `synergy.guard`, keyed by partner class, partner within 9 m, not in deathmatch): Thorn guards assassin / builder / bruiser partners (x0.85-0.88 damage taken), Stig sharpens assassin / tank / bruiser partners (x1.08-1.12 damage), Grim lifts bruiser / tank / assassin partners (x1.08-1.1), Francois lifts casters (x1.12). 28-pair 2v2 survey (each pair vs 4 reference teams, 16 games) is noisy (+-12%); clear trends: any pair with Wren 63-81%, pairs with Grim mostly 25-50%. Full retune after the six new champions land.
- Balance targets (14-champion pass): 1v1 matchups within 60/40 is fine; 70/30 is bad (one per champion at most). Most logical 2v2 comps should land within 60/40 of each other.
- Balance approach: don't chase numbers - bots aren't humans. If tuning stalls, improve bot logic or stop. Goal: every champion has a chance and every team comp is worth playing; judge by match length, deaths, K/D, structures built, not just win rate.
- New champion Brindle Tadwick (`harpooner`, class marksman, frogfolk fisherman): straight harpoon missiles stepped by `hero/harpooner.ts` (`Missile.harpoon`) that skewer up to 2 soldiers, ricochet off walls/props/structures and bend toward a foe after a bounce; Wet (3 s, +20% from his harpoons, a banked harpoon into a Wet foe x1.35); Reel In (B: roped harpoon, soldiers dragged all the way, champions half - toward the bounce point if it banked - through knockback velocity with the shove wall-splat flag, so walls/cliffs stun; `synergy.reelTo.tank` drags to a nearby tank partner instead); Tongue Lash (R: scored grapple jump up to 7 m / one terrain step, prefers ledges and anchors away from foes); Riptide (Z: `riptide` zone that slows, wets and drags foes to its centre; his harpoons bounce off the ring edge and chain between foes inside); belly-slide dodge leaves a `puddle` zone that trips Wet foes. CPU: `harpoonerFight` (Reel by walls / low / with a tank, Tongue Lash off divers, charged harpoons). 1v1 vs every champion on crossing (2 games each, 15 min cap): 5/16 - on the weak side, tune `heroes.json` harpooner numbers after merge.

## Abbess Hollin, the Beekeeping Scribe (`scribe`, caster)
- Concept from `docs/new-champions.md` (seed d76b85...: old human, beekeeping hills, ink and quill, ricochets, swaps places). Mid health (low tested at ~35% in 1v1 sims), mid speed ×0.92, mid damage, bot range 9.5, class caster, `synergy.swarmAlly.assassin` 1.15 (a partner assassin's next champion hit on a foe inside her swarm lands +15%, then it's spent; `status.swarmUntil/swarmOwner`, read in `synergyMul`).
- ANNOTATIONS (`hooks.runeMax` 4, `runeSeconds` 12, `runeRadius` 2.2): charged Ink Bolts, Swarms, bookmarks and every bolt during Illuminated Manuscript write a rune (`HeroState.runes`, oldest dropped). A spell that lands within 2.2 m of one of her runes consumes it and is empowered: a bolt ending on a rune bursts it for a bigger blinding blot (`runeDamage` 55), a swarm cast on one is a GREAT SWARM (+1.2 m, +2 s, ×1.4 stings).
- A INK BOLT (`inkbolt`, `src/sim/hero/scribe.ts`, `World.inkBolts`, step phase 5 after kegs): a straight bolt (56, 12.5 m, 22 m/s, 0.6 s) that bends gently (2.2 rad/s) toward the champion it was aimed at and follows its height. It ricochets once (`bounces`): off a wall (reflected on the blocked axis, +10%) or, after hitting a soldier/champion, on to the nearest unhit foe within 6 m (champions strongly preferred) at ×0.6. Held to ≥ half charge it is a charged bolt: up to ×1.48 damage, a 2.4 m ink blot (40) that blinds 1.2 s (`blindUntil`/`blindMiss` 0.5, the existing blind miss roll) and writes a rune.
- B SWARM (`swarm`, 9 s): a bee zone (`Zone` style `swarm`, 2.6 m, 4 s, 32 dps, slow 35%) on the placed point / target / ahead; it writes a rune. B again while it's out calls it: the zone gets `follow` = her id and `scribeTick` moves it onto her every tick for the rest of its time (+1.5 s minimum). A new swarm replaces the old one.
- R ERRATUM (`erratum`, 8 s): swap places with one of her runes within 12 m (placed point → nearest rune; stick → best-aligned rune; else the rune furthest from enemy champions); the rune moves to where she stood, so she can swap back. 0.3 s invulnerable. With no rune in reach R writes a BOOKMARK at her feet instead (cooldown 1.5 s). `placeRanges.spots` lists the reachable runes. Bot hint `never`; the CPU uses it in `scribeFight`.
- Z ILLUMINATED MANUSCRIPT (`manuscript`): every rune flares (80, slow 40% 1.5 s), a 4 m page burst (40, knockback 8) around her, then for 8 s every bolt writes a rune and ricochets once more, B and R are ready at once and the first recast of each is free (`manuscriptUntil`, `freeCast`, `manuscriptRecast`).
- L+X PAGE GUST (`pagegust` dodge replacement, always on): a 0.42 s glide (~5.5 m) along the stick or straight back from her facing, keeping her facing the fight; shoves foes within 2.4 m (15, knockback 9); 0.28 s invulnerable; dodge cooldown +0.3 s.
- Evolutions: R Margin Note (Erratum leaves a 60 blot, blind 1.5 s) / Dog-Eared Pages (−3 s cooldown, +2 runes, +6 s); B Royal Hive (+2 s, +0.6 m; with Margin Note Erratum also leaves a 3 s swarm) / Stinging Prose (×1.5 stings, slow 50%; with Dog-Eared Pages a called swarm stays 3 s longer); A Ricochet Runes (2 ricochets at ×0.75) / Indelible Ink (tapped bolts slow 20% 1 s, blot +1 m, blind 2.2 s); Z Grand Codex (11 s, flares 120).
- Bot (`scribeFight` in `bot/tactics.ts`, called from the ability step): Erratum to the rune furthest from a melee diver (else Page Gust away from it), call the swarm home when dived, bookmark a rune before fights start, charged bolts from range only when nobody has hurt her for 2 s (holding A crawls). Retreat via the generic rules (no `botPlan.escape`: the generic escape aims R at home, which Erratum can't use). Deathmatch personality: skirmisher.
- 1v1 bot sims (scribe vs each champion, 8 games each over crossing / ruins / shoals / hollow, sides swapped): 39% overall (Warlord 5/8, Stig 0/8, Grim 3/8, Remnil 5/8, Francois 3/8, Thorn 6/8, Wren 2/8, Maddock 1/8), inside the existing 30-69% spread. Weak into Wren (out-ranged), Maddock (heals through the poke) and Stig (towers/ballista); deathmatch 8-player sims on quarry ran clean (4/3 and 5/2 K/D).
- Art (fal ≈ $3.7: 17 Nano Banana, 2 Tripo): Nano Banana turnaround (Wren/Stig/Maddock sheets + portraits as refs) → Tripo P1 body (13.8k tris) and a giant quill staff (one prop sheet → Tripo, 1.8k tris) in hand_R. `tools/blender/tripo_heroes/scribe.py`: hand skinning like Maddock (robe on the hips, bell sleeves and the hanging hive lantern on the arms), `aim_quill` keys hand_R so the quill's total pitch follows a curve per clip; clips `shoot` (Ink Bolt flick, release ~40%), `cast` (Swarm, quill up, left palm sweeps out), `attack_a/b` (quill sweeps, for the shove), `attack_c` (Erratum, quill raised then stabbed down), `slam` (Manuscript, quill driven into the ground), new `gust` (leaning back reading a page; `ANIM_FALLBACK` gust → dodge). Team dye: the blue tabard (`team_box` narrowed to the front panel). Costumes QUEEN BEE (black velvet, gold bee stripes), MIDNIGHT VIGIL (indigo, silver stars and moons), RED INK (parchment robe with black script and red corrections) via the usual repaint and bake (`tools/costume/scribe_bake.py`), one icon sheet (`scribe_icons.py`). Painted FX atlas `assets/fx/scribe.png` (ink, drops, splat, stroke, bees, honeycomb, hive ring, pages, quill, rune, sigil, star, ink cloud, manuscript ring) plus unique costume atlases (honey/royal bees, moonlit moths and star charts, red ink/wasps/wax seals) and HQ paintings for the rune, manuscript ring, ink splat and hive ring (+ costume runes, rings and splats). Glyph strip, portrait (scriptorium), stage (beehive cloister garden), 7 evolution icons.
- Render: `kits/scribe.ts` (ink splash hits, bolts, blots, rune flashes/flares, swarm casts and calls, Erratum page swaps, Manuscript, Page Gust), `heroProps/scribe.ts` (bolts in flight with drip trails, runes as slowly turning additive gold circles that blink before they expire, bees around her hat, pages during Manuscript), the swarm zone in `hazards/zones.ts` (faint turning honeycomb ring + orbiting bee sprites, glides after her when called). Costume colour remaps / trails / swaps in `fx/costumeSkins.ts`.
- Audio: no new samples (the raw CC0 libraries aren't fetched on this machine): ink = splash/bubble at high pitch, runes = small bell / magic spell, swarm = `bed.insects` pitched up, Erratum/Gust = blink + page flips + cloth, Manuscript = gong + church bell + pages. Voice: `VOICE_CAST` in `sfx.ts` plays Wren's lines at rate 0.84 with 15% longer cuts until a dedicated `vo.scribe.*` pack is built.


## Mother Kelp, the Wreck Witch (`wreckwitch`, bruiser)
- Art (fal ~$3.75: 15 Nano Banana Pro, 3 Tripo P1): turnaround from the Stig/Wren sheets + portrait sheet (`tools/blender/tripo_heroes/wreckwitch_prompt.txt`), front view → Tripo body (14.5k tris, 2.05 m); props sheet → Tripo anchor flail (rigid in hand_R, `wreckwitch_anchor`) and a drowned grasping hand (`assets/props/drownedhand.glb`); the anchor also ships as the thrown runtime prop `wreckanchor`. `tripo_heroes/wreckwitch.py` skins the sailcloth dress by hand (Maddock's scheme, steep sleeve line because her arms hang wide) and authors anchor clips: two wide flail sweeps (attack_a/b), an overhead anchor smash (attack_c), the looping `whirl` (one full body turn with the anchor held out), `throw` (Dredge hurl), `cast` (Bilge spit), `slam` (Davy's Grip), `swing` (chain swing dodge) and `victory`.
